## III A Change of Masters / 三 换主

===Original===
We must have traveled several miles through the dark and dismal wood when we came suddenly upon a dense village built high among the branches of the trees. As we approached it my escort broke into wild shouting which was immediately answered from within, and a moment later a swarm of creatures of the same strange race as those who had captured me poured out to meet us. Again I was the center of a wildly chattering horde. I was pulled this way and that. Pinched, pounded, and thumped until I was black and blue, yet I do not think that their treatment was dictated by either cruelty or malice﻿—I was a curiosity, a freak, a new plaything, and their childish minds required the added evidence of all their senses to back up the testimony of their eyes.
Presently they dragged me within the village, which consisted of several hundred rude shelters of boughs and leaves supported upon the branches of the trees.
Between the huts, which sometimes formed crooked streets, were dead branches and the trunks of small trees which connected the huts upon one tree to those within adjoining trees; the whole network of huts and pathways forming an almost solid flooring a good fifty feet above the ground.
I wondered why these agile creatures required connecting bridges between the trees, but later when I saw the motley aggregation of half-savage beasts which they kept within their village I realized the necessity for the pathways. There were a number of the same vicious wolf-dogs which we had left worrying the *dyryth*, and many goatlike animals whose distended udders explained the reasons for their presence.
My guard halted before one of the huts into which I was pushed; then two of the creatures squatted down before the entrance﻿—to prevent my escape, doubtless. Though where I should have escaped to I certainly had not the remotest conception. I had no more than entered the dark shadows of the interior than there fell upon my ears the tones of a familiar voice, in prayer.
===Chinese===
想必我们在那幽暗阴郁的树林里走了好几英里，忽然来到一座密密的村落，高高地架在树枝之间。我们走近时，押送我的队伍爆发出狂野的呼喊，村里立刻传来回应，片刻之后，一大群同俘获我的那些家伙同属那个奇异种族的生物涌出来迎接我们。我又一次成了疯狂叽喳的人群的中心，被拉过来拽过去，又掐又捶又撞，直到浑身青一块紫一块；不过我并不认为他们的对待出于残忍或恶意——我是个稀罕物、一个怪物、一件新玩具，他们孩子似的头脑需要一切感官的补充证据，来印证眼睛的证词。
不多时，他们把我拖进村里。村子由几百座粗陋的窝棚组成，以枝条和树叶搭成，架在树木的枝干上。
窝棚之间——它们有时构成弯弯曲曲的街巷——横着枯枝和小树干，把一棵树上的窝棚同毗邻树木中的窝棚连在一起；整张窝棚与小路结成的网络，在离地足足五十英尺的高处，几乎拼成了一片实心的地板。
我起先纳闷，这些敏捷的生物为什么还需要树与树之间的桥；可后来见到他们养在村中的那群五花八门、半野性的牲畜，我便明白这些通路之必要了。其中有好些同我们先前丢下围攻*德里思*（*dyryth*）的那类凶恶狼犬一模一样，还有许多山羊似的动物，它们鼓胀的乳房说明了它们被养在这里的缘故。
押送我的队伍在其中一座窝棚前停下，我被推了进去；随即两个家伙在门口蹲坐下来——多半是为了防我逃跑。虽说要逃去哪儿，我心里实在没有半点概念。我一钻进屋内黑暗的阴影，耳边便响起一个熟悉的声音——在做祷告。

===Original===
“Perry!” I cried. “Dear old Perry! Thank the Lord you are safe.”
“David! Can it be possible that you escaped?” And the old man stumbled toward me and threw his arms about me.
He had seen me fall before the *dyryth*, and then he had been seized by a number of the ape-creatures and borne through the tree tops to their village. His captors had been as inquisitive as to his strange clothing as had mine, with the same result. As we looked at each other we could not help but laugh.
“With a tail, David,” remarked Perry, “you would make a very handsome ape.”
“Maybe we can borrow a couple,” I rejoined. “They seem to be quite the thing this season. I wonder what the creatures intend doing with us, Perry. They don’t seem really savage. What do you suppose they can be? You were about to tell me where we are when that great hairy frigate bore down upon us﻿—have you really any idea at all?”
===Chinese===
“佩里！”我喊道，“亲爱的老佩里！感谢上帝你平安无事。”
“戴维！你居然逃出来了，这可能吗？”老人踉跄着朝我扑来，张开双臂抱住我。
他眼看着我摔倒在*德里思*面前，随后便被一群猿猴般的生物抓住，从树顶上被扛到了他们的村里。捉他的那些家伙对他那身奇怪的衣着，同捉我的一样好奇，结果也一样。我们对望一眼，都忍不住笑了。
“再有条尾巴，戴维，”佩里说，“你就是一只非常英俊的猿猴了。”
“也许咱们能借两条来，”我回敬道，“这一季它们看起来相当流行呢。我在想，这些家伙打算拿我们怎么办，佩里。他们好像并不真凶。你猜他们能是什么？上次那艘毛茸茸的大战舰朝我们扑过来时，你正要告诉我咱们在哪儿——你到底有点眉目没有？”

===Original===
“Yes, David,” he replied, “I know precisely where we are. We have made a magnificent discovery, my boy! We have proved that the earth is hollow. We have passed entirely through its crust to the inner world.”
“Perry, you are mad!”
“Not at all, David. For two hundred and fifty miles our prospector bore us through the crust beneath our outer world. At that point it reached the center of gravity of the five-hundred-mile-thick crust. Up to that point we had been descending﻿—direction is, of course, merely relative. Then at the moment that our seats revolved﻿—the thing that made you believe that we had turned about and were speeding upward﻿—we passed the center of gravity and, though we did not alter the direction of our progress, yet we were in reality moving upward﻿—toward the surface of the inner world. Does not the strange fauna and flora which we have seen convince you that you are not in the world of your birth? And the horizon﻿—could it present the strange aspects which we both noted unless we were indeed standing upon the inside surface of a sphere?”
“But the sun, Perry!” I urged. “How in the world can the sun shine through five hundred miles of solid crust?”
“It is not the sun of the outer world that we see here. It is another sun﻿—an entirely different sun﻿—that casts its eternal noonday effulgence upon the face of the inner world. Look at it now, David﻿—if you can see it from the doorway of this hut﻿—and you will see that it is still in the exact center of the heavens. We have been here for many hours﻿—yet it is still noon.
===Chinese===
“有，戴维，”他答道，“我确切知道我们在哪儿。我们有了一项了不起的发现，孩子！我们证明了地球是空心的。我们已经完全穿透了它的外壳，到了内心世界。”
“佩里，你疯了！”
“一点也没疯，戴维。我们的钻探车载着我们，在外层世界之下的外壳里穿行了两百五十英里。到那一点，它到达了五百英里厚的外壳的引力中心。在那之前我们一直在下降——所谓方向，当然只是相对的说法。随后，就在我们的座椅旋转过来的那一刻——就是那一下，让你以为我们掉了头、正朝上飞驰——我们越过了引力中心，于是，尽管我们并没有改变前进的方向，实际上却在向上行进——朝着内心世界的表面。我们一路见到的这些奇异动植物，还不足以让你相信你已不在你出生的那个世界了吗？还有那地平线——倘若我们不是确确实实站在一只圆球的内表面上，它可能呈现出我们两个都注意到的那些奇特景象吗？”
“可太阳呢，佩里！”我急道，“太阳到底怎么能穿透五百英里厚的实心外壳照进来？”
“我们在这里看到的，不是外层世界的太阳。它是另一个太阳——一颗截然不同的太阳——把永恒的正午的光辉投在内心世界的表面。现在就看看它吧，戴维——要是你从这窝棚门口望得见——你会发现它仍稳稳悬在天空的正中央。我们已经来了好多个钟头——可现在依然是正午。”

===Original===
“And withal it is very simple, David. The earth was once a nebulous mass. It cooled, and as it cooled it shrank. At length a thin crust of solid matter formed upon its outer surface﻿—a sort of shell; but within it was partially molten matter and highly expanded gases. As it continued to cool, what happened? Centrifugal force hurled the particles of the nebulous center toward the crust as rapidly as they approached a solid state. You have seen the same principle practically applied in the modern cream separator. Presently there was only a small super-heated core of gaseous matter remaining within a huge vacant interior left by the contraction of the cooling gases. The equal attraction of the solid crust from all directions maintained this luminous core in the exact center of the hollow globe. What remains of it is the sun you saw today﻿—a relatively tiny thing at the exact center of the earth. Equally to every part of this inner world it diffuses its perpetual noonday light and torrid heat.
“This inner world must have cooled sufficiently to support animal life long ages after life appeared upon the outer crust, but that the same agencies were at work here is evident from the similar forms of both animal and vegetable creation which we have already seen. Take the great beast which attacked us, for example. Unquestionably a counterpart of the Megatherium of the post-Pliocene period of the outer crust, whose fossilized skeleton has been found in South America.”
“But the grotesque inhabitants of this forest?” I urged. “Surely they have no counterpart in the earth’s history.”
“Who can tell?” he rejoined. “They may constitute the link between ape and man, all traces of which have been swallowed by the countless convulsions which have racked the outer crust, or they may be merely the result of evolution along slightly different lines﻿—either is quite possible.”
Further speculation was interrupted by the appearance of several of our captors before the entrance of the hut. Two of them entered and dragged us forth. The perilous pathways and the surrounding trees were filled with the black ape-men, their females, and their young. There was not an ornament, a weapon, or a garment among the lot.
===Chinese===
“而且道理极简单，戴维。地球当年是一团星云状的物质。它冷却，冷却时便收缩。终于，一层薄薄的固体物质外壳在它的外表面上形成了——像一只壳；壳内则是部分熔融的物质和高度膨胀的气体。它继续冷却下去，会发生什么？离心力把星云中心的粒子甩向外壳，其速度之快，不亚于它们逼近固态的速度。同样的原理，你在现代的奶油分离机里见到过实际应用。不久，内部只剩下一小团过热的气体核心，悬在冷却气体收缩后留下的巨大空腔之中。固体外壳从四面八方发出的相等的引力，把这团发光的核心固定在空心地球的正中心。它剩下的部分，就是你今天见到的太阳——一件相当小的东西，悬在地球的正中心。它把永恒的正午之光和酷热，均等地洒向内心世界的每一个角落。”
“这个内心世界冷却到可以养育动物生命的程度，想必是在外壳上出现生命之后许久许久；可是同样的力量在这里起过作用，这一点从我们已见到的动植物造化的相似形态上看得出来。拿袭击我们的那头巨兽来说，毫无疑问，它是外壳后上新世时期大地懒（Megatherium）的翻版——大地懒的化石骨架曾在南美洲被发现。”
“可这片林子里的这些怪模怪样的居民呢？”我追问道，“他们总不会在地球历史上有什么对应之物吧。”
“谁说得准？”他答道，“他们可能正是猿与人之间的那一环，其全部痕迹都已被撼动外壳的无数次剧变吞没；也可能只是沿略不同的路线演化的结果——两种可能都不小。”
进一步的猜测被打断了——几个捕捉我们的人出现在窝棚门口。其中两个进来，把我们拖了出去。那些危险的通路和四周的树上，挤满了黑皮肤的猿人，还有他们的雌性和幼崽。这一大群里，没有一件饰物、一件武器、一片衣裳。

===Original===
“Quite low in the scale of creation,” commented Perry.
“Quite high enough to play the deuce with us, though,” I replied. “Now what do you suppose they intend doing with us?”
We were not long in learning. As on the occasion of our trip to the village we were seized by a couple of the powerful creatures and whirled away through the tree tops, while about us and in our wake raced a chattering, jabbering, grinning horde of sleek, black ape-things.
Twice my bearers missed their footing, and my heart ceased beating as we plunged toward instant death among the tangled deadwood beneath. But on both occasions those lithe, powerful tails reached out and found sustaining branches, nor did either of the creatures loosen their grasp upon me. In fact, it seemed that the incidents were of no greater moment to them than would be the stubbing of one’s toe at a street crossing in the outer world﻿—they but laughed uproariously and sped on with me.
For some time they continued through the forest﻿—how long I could not guess for I was learning, what was later borne very forcefully to my mind, that time ceases to be a factor the moment means for measuring it cease to exist. Our watches were gone, and we were living beneath a stationary sun. Already I was puzzled to compute the period of time which had elapsed since we broke through the crust of the inner world. It might be hours, or it might be days﻿—who in the world could tell where it was always noon! By the sun, no time had elapsed﻿—but my judgment told me that we must have been several hours in this strange world.
===Chinese===
“在造物的阶梯上相当低等。”佩里评论道。
“可要收拾咱们，也绰绰有余了。”我答道，“你说他们打算拿我们怎么办？”
我们没多久就知道了。同上次去村子时一样，我们被两个强壮的家伙一把抓起，从树顶上呼啸而去，我们周围和身后，一群油光乌黑、猿猴似的东西叽叽喳喳、哇啦哇啦、龇牙咧嘴地追赶着。
搬运我的家伙有两次失了脚，我们朝下面缠结的枯木间直坠下去，眼看当场就要没命，我的心都停止了跳动。可那两次，都有一条条柔韧有力的尾巴伸出去，找到了可以攀住的树枝；两个家伙也谁都没有松开抓我的手。事实上，这些意外在他们看来，似乎不比在外层世界过马路时踢了一下脚趾更了不起——他们只是放声大笑，带着我继续飞奔。
他们在森林里走了好一阵——走了多久我猜不出，因为我正在领会一件后来深深印进我脑子的事：计量时间的手段一消失，时间就不再是一个因素。我们的表都不见了，我们又生活在一轮不动的太阳底下。自从突破内心世界的外壳以来，究竟过了多久，我已经算不清了。也许是几个钟头，也许是好几天——在永远是正午的地方，天知道谁能说得清！按太阳算，半点时间也没过去——可我的判断告诉我，我们在这个奇怪的世界里想必已有好几个钟头了。

===Original===
Presently the forest terminated, and we came out upon a level plain. A short distance before us rose a few low, rocky hills. Toward these our captors urged us, and after a short time led us through a narrow pass into a tiny, circular valley. Here they got down to work, and we were soon convinced that if we were not to die to make a Roman holiday, we were to die for some other purpose. The attitude of our captors altered immediately as they entered the natural arena within the rocky hills. Their laughter ceased. Grim ferocity marked their bestial faces﻿—bared fangs menaced us.
We were placed in the center of the amphitheater﻿—the thousand creatures forming a great ring about us. Then a wolf-dog was brought﻿—*Hyaenodon* Perry called it﻿—and turned loose with us inside the circle. The thing’s body was as large as that of a full-grown mastiff, its legs were short and powerful, and its jaws broad and strong. Dark, shaggy hair covered its back and sides, while its breast and belly were quite white. As it slunk toward us it presented a most formidable aspect with its upcurled lips baring its mighty fangs.
Perry was on his knees, praying. I stooped and picked up a small stone. At my movement the beast veered off a bit and commenced circling us. Evidently it had been a target for stones before. The ape-things were dancing up and down urging the brute on with savage cries, until at last, seeing that I did not throw, he charged us.
At Andover, and later at Yale, I had pitched on winning ball teams. My speed and control must both have been above the ordinary, for I made such a record during my senior year at college that overtures were made to me in behalf of one of the great major-league teams; but in the tightest pitch that ever had confronted me in the past I had never been in such need for control as now.
As I wound up for the delivery, I held my nerves and muscles under absolute command, though the grinning jaws were hurtling toward me at terrific speed. And then I let go, with every ounce of my weight and muscle and science in back of that throw. The stone caught the *hyaenodon* full upon the end of the nose, and sent him bowling over upon his back.
===Chinese===
不多时，森林到了尽头，我们来到一片平坦的平原上。前方不远处耸起几座低低的石山。捉我们的人把我们朝那儿赶，走了一小段，便领我们穿过一道窄窄的隘口，进了一个小小的圆形山谷。在这里他们动起手来，我们随即明白：倘若不是要让我们死于一场罗马假日式的取乐，就是要我们死在别的什么用场上。一走进石山环抱的天然竞技场，捉我们的人的态度立刻变了。笑声停了。狰狞的凶残刻上他们的兽脸——龇出的獠牙威吓着我们。
我们被安置在竞技场中央——上千个生物围着我们结成一个大圆圈。随后牵来一只狼犬——佩里管它叫*鬣齿兽*（*Hyaenodon*）——同我们一起放开在圈里。那东西的身躯同一条成年的獒犬一般大，腿短而有力，颚宽阔结实。背上和体侧覆盖着深色粗毛，胸口和肚皮却相当白。它悄悄逼近我们时，翻卷的上唇龇出巨大的獠牙，那副模样煞是吓人。
佩里跪在地上祷告。我俯身捡起一块小石头。见我动作，那野兽稍稍偏开，开始绕着我们转。它显然以前挨过石头砸。猿猴们上蹿下跳，用野蛮的呼号催促那畜生上前，直到最后，见我不扔，它便朝我们猛冲过来。
在安多弗（Andover），后来在耶鲁，我都在获胜的棒球队里当过投手。我的球速和控制力想必都在水准之上，因为大学四年级那年我创下的纪录，使一家大联盟球队来同我接洽过；可是，以往无论遭遇过怎样吃紧的关头，我都从未像此刻这样需要控制力。
我挥臂准备投出时，尽管那咧开的大嘴正以骇人的速度朝我扑来，我的神经和肌肉仍处于绝对的掌控之下。然后我出手了，把全身每一盎司的重量、每一分肌肉和全部技巧都压在那一掷上。石头不偏不倚正中*鬣齿兽*的鼻尖，把它打得四脚朝天，翻倒在地。

===Original===
At the same instant a chorus of shrieks and howls arose from the circle of spectators, so that for a moment I thought that the upsetting of their champion was the cause; but in this I soon saw that I was mistaken. As I looked, the ape-things broke in all directions toward the surrounding hills, and then I distinguished the real cause of their perturbation. Behind them, streaming through the pass which leads into the valley, came a swarm of hairy men﻿—gorilla-like creatures armed with spears and hatchets, and bearing long, oval shields. Like demons they set upon the ape-things, and before them the *hyaenodon*, which had now regained its senses and its feet, fled howling with fright. Past us swept the pursued and the pursuers, nor did the hairy ones accord us more than a passing glance until the arena had been emptied of its former occupants. Then they returned to us, and one who seemed to have authority among them directed that we be brought with them.
When we had passed out of the amphitheater onto the great plain we saw a caravan of men and women﻿—human beings like ourselves﻿—and for the first time hope and relief filled my heart, until I could have cried out in the exuberance of my happiness. It is true that they were a half-naked, wild-appearing aggregation; but they at least were fashioned along the same lines as ourselves﻿—there was nothing grotesque or horrible about them as about the other creatures in this strange, weird world.
But as we came closer, our hearts sank once more, for we discovered that the poor wretches were chained neck to neck in a long line, and that the gorilla-men were their guards. With little ceremony Perry and I were chained at the end of the line, and without further ado the interrupted march was resumed.
Up to this time the excitement had kept us both up; but now the tiresome monotony of the long march across the sunbaked plain brought on all the agonies consequent to a long-denied sleep. On and on we stumbled beneath that hateful noonday sun. If we fell we were prodded with a sharp point. Our companions in chains did not stumble. They strode along proudly erect. Occasionally they would exchange words with one another in a monosyllabic language. They were a noble-appearing race with well-formed heads and perfect physiques. The men were heavily bearded, tall and muscular; the women, smaller and more gracefully molded, with great masses of raven hair caught into loose knots upon their heads. The features of both sexes were well proportioned﻿—there was not a face among them that would have been called even plain if judged by earthly standards. They wore no ornaments; but this I later learned was due to the fact that their captors had stripped them of everything of value. As garmenture the women possessed a single robe of some light-colored, spotted hide, rather similar in appearance to a leopard’s skin. This they wore either supported entirely about the waist by a leathern thong, so that it hung partially below the knee on one side, or possibly looped gracefully across one shoulder. Their feet were shod with skin sandals. The men wore loin cloths of the hide of some shaggy beast, long ends of which depended before and behind nearly to the ground. In some instances these ends were finished with the strong talons of the beast from which the hides had been taken.
Our guards, whom I already have described as gorilla-like men, were rather lighter in build than a gorilla, but even so they were indeed mighty creatures. Their arms and legs were proportioned more in conformity with human standards, but their entire bodies were covered with shaggy, brown hair, and their faces were quite as brutal as those of the few stuffed specimens of the gorilla which I had seen in the museums at home.
===Chinese===
与此同时，围观者围成的圆圈爆出一片尖啸嚎叫，一时间我还以为是他们的冠军被掀翻了的缘故；可我很快就发现自己想错了。只见猿猴们四散奔逃，朝四周的山上跑去，随即我看清了他们惊慌的真正原因。他们身后，涌过通向山谷的隘口，来了一大群毛茸茸的人——大猩猩似的生物，手持长矛和斧头，举着长长的椭圆盾牌。他们像恶魔一样扑向猿猴；在他们面前，方才恢复了知觉、爬起身来的*鬣齿兽*也吓得嗥叫着逃了。被追的和追捕的一股脑从我们身边扫过，那些多毛的人对我们只瞥了一眼，直到竞技场上原先的居民跑得一干二净。然后他们回到我们跟前，其中一个似乎是头目的，吩咐把我们带上一起走。
走出竞技场，来到大平原上，我们望见一支由男人和女人组成的队伍——同我们一样的人类——希望和宽慰第一次涌上我的心头，喜悦得我几乎要喊出声来。不错，他们是一群半裸着身子、样子野蛮的人；可他们至少同我们一样生着人的模样——同这个怪异世界里其他生物不同，他们身上没有半点怪诞和可怕。
可等走近了，我们的心又沉了下去，因为我们发现这些可怜虫被人用锁链一个个锁着脖子，串成一长列，而那些大猩猩似的人正是看守。没有多少客套，佩里和我被锁在队伍末尾，中断的行军立刻重新开始。
直到这时，一路的兴奋还撑着我们俩；可如今，顶着正午的烈日横穿烤干的平原，漫长行军沉闷的单调，把久被剥夺的睡眠所带来的一切痛苦都引了出来。我们跌跌撞撞地在那轮可恨的正午烈日下走啊走。摔倒，就有尖锐的矛尖来戳。戴镣的同伴们却不曾跌撞，他们昂首挺胸，大步前行，偶尔用一种单音节的语言交谈几句。他们是个相貌高贵的种族，头型端正，体格完美。男人们胡须浓密，高大健壮；女人们身材较小，曲线更为秀美，一头头乌黑的头发挽成松松的发髻盘在头上。两性的五官都匀称端正——按地球的标准衡量，他们中间没有一张脸算得上平庸。他们不戴饰物；这一点我后来才知道，是因为捉他们的人把值钱的东西剥夺得一干二净。论衣着，女人们每人有一件浅色带斑点的兽皮长袍，样子颇像豹皮。她们穿它，或用一根皮绳全然系在腰间，让它垂在身体一侧、半掩过膝；或斜挎在一边肩头，显得颇为潇洒。她们脚上穿着兽皮凉鞋。男人们围一条粗毛兽皮的遮羞布，长长的布头前垂后搭，几乎拖到地上。有些布头上还缀着取皮那头巨兽的利爪。
那些看守——我已把他们描述成大猩猩似的人——身形比大猩猩略瘦，但即便如此，他们也确乎是强壮有力的生物。他们的臂腿比例更接近人的标准，可全身覆盖着粗浓的棕色毛发，脸相之凶残，比起我在家乡博物馆里见过的那几件大猩猩标本毫无逊色。

===Original===
Their only redeeming feature lay in the development of the head above and back of the ears. In this respect they were not one whit less human than we. They were clothed in a sort of tunic of light cloth which reached to the knees. Beneath this they wore only a loin cloth of the same material, while their feet were shod with thick hide of some mammoth creature of this inner world.
Their arms and necks were encircled by many ornaments of metal﻿—silver predominating﻿—and on their tunics were sewn the heads of tiny reptiles in odd and rather artistic designs. They talked among themselves as they marched along on either side of us, but in a language which I perceived differed from that employed by our fellow prisoners. When they addressed the latter they used what appeared to be a third language, and which I later learned is a mongrel tongue rather analogous to the Pidgin-English of the Chinese coolie.
How far we marched I have no conception, nor has Perry. Both of us were asleep much of the time for hours before a halt was called﻿—then we dropped in our tracks. I say “for hours,” but how may one measure time where time does not exist! When our march commenced the sun stood at zenith. When we halted our shadows still pointed toward nadir. Whether an instant or an eternity of earthly time elapsed who may say. That march may have occupied nine years and eleven months of the ten years that I spent in the inner world, or it may have been accomplished in the fraction of a second﻿—I cannot tell. But this I do know that since you have told me that ten years have elapsed since I departed from this earth I have lost all respect for time﻿—I am commencing to doubt that such a thing exists other than in the weak, finite mind of man.
===Chinese===
他们唯一可取之处在于耳朵上方和后部头颅的发育。在这一方面，他们同我们相比毫不逊色于人。他们穿一种齐膝的薄布束腰外衣，里面只围一条同样料子的遮羞布，脚上穿着用这内心世界某种巨兽的厚皮做的鞋。
他们的手臂和脖子上套着许多金属饰物——以白银居多——束腰外衣上缝着小爬虫的头，拼出种种古怪而颇有艺术趣味的花样。他们行军时走在我们两侧，彼此交谈，用的语言我听得出同我们的难友所说的不同。他们同难友讲话时，用的似乎是第三种语言，我后来得知，那是一种混杂语，颇似中国苦力的洋泾浜英语。
我们走了多远，我说不上，佩里也说不上。停下之前的好些钟头里，我们俩大半时间都是边走边睡——随后便一头栽倒在原地。我说“好些钟头”，可在一个不存在时间的地方，又怎能计量时间！行军开始时，太阳悬在天顶。我们停下时，我们的影子仍旧指向天底。究竟过去了一瞬还是一永恒，谁说得上呢。那次行军也许占去了我在内心世界那十年的九年零十一个月，也许只用了不到弹指一瞬——我说不清。可这一点我确知不疑：自从你告诉我，自我离开地球以来已过去十年，我就对时间失去了全部敬意——我开始怀疑，除了在人类软弱有限的心智里，这种东西是否真的存在。

## II A Strange World / 一个陌生的世界

===Original===
I was unconscious little more than an instant, for as I lunged forward from the crossbeam to which I had been clinging, and fell with a crash to the floor of the cabin, the shock brought me to myself.
My first concern was with Perry. I was horrified at the thought that upon the very threshold of salvation he might be dead. Tearing open his shirt I placed my ear to his breast. I could have cried with relief﻿—his heart was beating quite regularly.
At the water tank I wetted my handkerchief, slapping it smartly across his forehead and face several times. In a moment I was rewarded by the raising of his lids. For a time he lay wide-eyed and quite uncomprehending. Then his scattered wits slowly foregathered, and he sat up sniffing the air with an expression of wonderment upon his face.
“Why, David,” he cried at last, “it’s air, as sure as I live. Why﻿—why what does it mean? Where in the world are we? What has happened?”

===Chinese===
我昏迷了不过片刻，因为当我从紧攀着的横梁上向前一扑，轰的一声摔在舱室地板上时，这一震便让我回过了神。
我头一件挂心的事是佩里（Perry）。一想到他可能已经死在获救的门槛上，我便惊骇万分。我撕开他的衬衣，把耳朵贴上他的胸膛。我险些喜极而泣——他的心跳得相当规律。
我到水箱边浸湿手帕，利落地在他前额和脸上连拍了几下。不消片刻，他的眼皮抬了起来，算是给我的报偿。有那么一阵，他睁着眼睛躺在那里，全然茫然不解。随后，他涣散的神志缓缓归拢，他坐起身来，嗅着空气，满脸惊奇的神色。
「哎呀，戴维（David），」他终于喊道，「这是空气，千真万确，我活着呢。哎呀——哎呀，这是什么意思？我们到底在哪儿？出什么事了？」

===Original===
“It means that we’re back at the surface all right, Perry,” I cried; “but where, I don’t know. I haven’t opened her up yet. Been too busy reviving you. Lord, man, but you had a close squeak!”
“You say we’re back at the surface, David? How can that be? How long have I been unconscious?”
“Not long. We turned in the ice stratum. Don’t you recall the sudden whirling of our seats? After that the drill was above you instead of below. We didn’t notice it at the time; but I recall it now.”
“You mean to say that we turned back in the ice stratum, David? That is not possible. The prospector cannot turn unless its nose is deflected from the outside﻿—by some external force or resistance﻿—the steering wheel within would have moved in response. The steering wheel has not budged, David, since we started. You know that.”

===Chinese===
「这意思是说，我们确实回到地表了，佩里，」我喊道；「不过是在哪儿，我不知道。我还没开舱呢，一直忙着救你。老天，伙计，你可真是死里逃生！」
「你是说，我们回到地表了，戴维？这怎么可能？我昏迷了多久？」
「不久。我们在冰层里掉的头。你不记得座位突然打转了吗？打那以后，钻头就在你头顶上，而不是脚底下了。当时我们没留意；可我现在想起来了。」
「你是说，我们在冰层里折回去了，戴维？那不可能。探勘器（prospector）不可能掉头，除非它的钻头被外力偏转——受某种来自外界的力量或阻力——否则舱里的方向盘必会跟着转动。而从出发起，方向盘就没有动过一丝一毫，戴维，这你是知道的。」

===Original===
I did know it; but here we were with our drill racing in pure air, and copious volumes of it pouring into the cabin.
“We couldn’t have turned in the ice stratum, Perry, I know as well as you,” I replied; “but the fact remains that we did, for here we are this minute at the surface of the earth again, and I am going out to see just where.”
“Better wait till morning, David﻿—it must be midnight now.”
I glanced at the chronometer.

===Chinese===
我确实知道；可事实摆在眼前：我们的钻头正在纯净的空气中飞转，大量的空气正汩汩涌入舱内。
「我们不可能在冰层里掉了头，佩里，这我知道得不比你差，」我答道；「可事实是我们确实掉了，因为此刻我们好端端地回到了地球表面，我这就出去看看究竟到了哪儿。」
「最好等到天亮再出去，戴维——现在准是半夜了。」
我瞥了一眼精密计时器。

===Original===
“Half after twelve. We have been out seventy-two hours, so it must be midnight. Nevertheless I am going to have a look at the blessed sky that I had given up all hope of ever seeing again,” and so saying I lifted the bars from the inner door, and swung it open. There was quite a quantity of loose material in the jacket, and this I had to remove with a shovel to get at the opposite door in the outer shell.
In a short time I had removed enough of the earth and rock to the floor of the cabin to expose the door beyond. Perry was directly behind me as I threw it open. The upper half was above the surface of the ground. With an expression of surprise I turned and looked at Perry﻿—it was broad daylight without!
“Something seems to have gone wrong either with our calculations or the chronometer,” I said. Perry shook his head﻿—there was a strange expression in his eyes.
“Let’s have a look beyond that door, David,” he cried.

===Chinese===
「十二点半。我们已经出来七十二个小时了，所以现在必是午夜。尽管如此，我还是要去看一眼那片老天爷的天空——我本以为再也见不着它了。」说着，我拔下内门的门闩，把门推开。外套层里积了相当多的松散泥石，我只得用铲子把它们清掉，才够得着外壳上正对的那扇外门。
不多时，我把足够的土石清到了舱室地板上，露出里面那扇门。我猛地拉开它时，佩里就紧跟在我身后。门的上半截高出了地面。我一脸惊讶地转身望向佩里——外面竟是大天白日！
「不是我们的计算出了错，就是计时器出了毛病。」我说。佩里摇了摇头——他眼睛里有一种异样的神情。
「咱们到那扇门外看看去，戴维。」他喊道。

===Original===
Together we stepped out to stand in silent contemplation of a landscape at once weird and beautiful. Before us a low and level shore stretched down to a silent sea. As far as the eye could reach the surface of the water was dotted with countless tiny isles﻿—some of towering, barren, granitic rock﻿—others resplendent in gorgeous trappings of tropical vegetation, myriad starred with the magnificent splendor of vivid blooms.
Behind us rose a dark and forbidding wood of giant arborescent ferns intermingled with the commoner types of a primeval tropical forest. Huge creepers depended in great loops from tree to tree, dense underbrush overgrew a tangled mass of fallen trunks and branches. Upon the outer verge we could see the same splendid coloring of countless blossoms that glorified the islands, but within the dense shadows all seemed dark and gloomy as the grave.
And upon all the noonday sun poured its torrid rays out of a cloudless sky.
“Where on earth can we be?” I asked, turning to Perry.

===Chinese===
我们一同跨出门去，默默伫立，凝望一片既诡奇又美丽的风景。我们面前，一道低平的海岸缓缓伸向一片沉寂的海。极目所至，水面上点缀着数不清的小岛——有的是高耸、荒凉的花岗岩——有的则披着热带草木的华服，光华夺目，千万朵鲜艳的繁花如星辰般缀满其上。
我们身后，耸起一片幽暗可怖的林木，巨大的乔木状蕨类混杂着原始热带森林里更寻常的树种。粗大的藤蔓一环环垂挂在树与树之间，茂密的灌木覆满纠缠成堆的倒木断枝。林子外缘，处处可见装点着海岛的那种绚烂花色，可密林深处却一片幽暗阴森，恍如坟墓。
而在这万物之上，正午的太阳正从万里无云的天空倾泻着灼热的射线。
「我们究竟会在哪儿呢？」我转向佩里问道。

===Original===
For some moments the old man did not reply. He stood with bowed head, buried in deep thought. But at last he spoke.
“David,” he said, “I am not so sure that we are *on* earth.”
“What do you mean, Perry?” I cried. “Do you think that we are dead, and this is heaven?” He smiled, and turning, pointing to the nose of the prospector protruding from the ground at our backs.
“But for that, David, I might believe that we were indeed come to the country beyond the Styx. The prospector renders that theory untenable﻿—it, certainly, could never have gone to heaven. However I am willing to concede that we actually may be in another world from that which we have always known. If we are not *on* earth, there is every reason to believe that we may be *in* it.”

===Chinese===
老人好一阵没有作答。他垂首而立，陷入深深的思索。但他终于开口了。
「戴维，」他说，「我可不敢肯定，我们还在地球*上*。」
「你是什么意思，佩里？」我喊道，「你以为我们死了，这里是天堂吗？」他微微一笑，转过身，指向我们身后那截露出地面的探勘器钻头。
「若不是有那个东西在那儿，戴维，我兴许真会相信，我们确已来到了冥河（Styx）彼岸的国度。探勘器让这套理论站不住脚——它肯定是上不了天堂的。不过，我愿意承认，我们或许真的身处另一个世界，不同于我们一向熟知的这一个。倘若我们不在地球*之上*，那就完全有理由相信，我们在地球*之中*。」

===Original===
“We may have quartered through the earth’s crust and come out upon some tropical island of the West Indies,” I suggested. Again Perry shook his head.
“Let us wait and see, David,” he replied, “and in the meantime suppose we do a bit of exploring up and down the coast﻿—we may find a native who can enlighten us.”
As we walked along the beach Perry gazed long and earnestly across the water. Evidently he was wrestling with a mighty problem.
“David,” he said abruptly, “do you perceive anything unusual about the horizon?”

===Chinese===
「说不定我们斜穿过了地壳，钻出来到了西印度群岛的某座热带海岛上。」我提议。佩里又摇了摇头。
「咱们等着瞧吧，戴维，」他答道，「这会儿，不如咱们沿海岸上下探一探路——说不定能碰上个把土人，给我们指点迷津。」
我们沿着海滩走去，佩里久久地、恳切地凝望着海面。显然，他正在同一个重大难题较劲。
「戴维，」他突然说，「你可察觉到地平线有什么不对劲？」

===Original===
As I looked I began to appreciate the reason for the strangeness of the landscape that had haunted me from the first with an illusive suggestion of the bizarre and unnatural﻿—*There was no horizon*! As far as the eye could reach out the sea continued and upon its bosom floated tiny islands, those in the distance reduced to mere specks; but ever beyond them was the sea, until the impression became quite real that one was *looking up* at the most distant point that the eyes could fathom﻿—the distance was lost in the distance. That was all﻿—there was no clear-cut horizontal line marking the dip of the globe below the line of vision.
“A great light is commencing to break on me,” continued Perry, taking out his watch. “I believe that I have partially solved the riddle. It is now two o’clock. When we emerged from the prospector the sun was directly above us. Where is it now?”
I glanced up to find the great orb still motionless in the center of the heaven. And such a sun! I had scarcely noticed it before. Fully thrice the size of the sun I had known throughout my life, and apparently so near that the sight of it carried the conviction that one might almost reach up and touch it.

===Chinese===
我定睛望去，这才领会到这片风景何以透着古怪——打从一开始，它就以一种离奇诡异的错位感萦绕着我——*根本没有地平线*！极目远眺，海一直在延伸，海面上漂浮着座座小岛，远处的那些缩成了星星点点；可越过它们依然是海，直到一种感觉变得确凿无疑：人是在朝*上*望，望向眼睛所能企及的最远一点——远方消失在远方之中。就这么回事——没有一道清晰的水平线，标出地球沉到视线之下的下缘。
「我脑中豁然亮堂起来了，」佩里接着说，掏出怀表。「我相信，这个谜我已解开了一半。现在是两点钟。我们钻出探勘器时，太阳正悬在头顶。此刻它在哪里？」
我抬眼一望，发现那轮巨大的日轮依旧一动不动地悬在天心。好一轮太阳！我先前竟几乎没有留意它。它足足有我平生所知的太阳的三倍大，而且看上去近得很，一望之下便叫人确信：伸手几乎就能摸到它。

===Original===
“My God, Perry, where are we?” I exclaimed. “This thing is beginning to get on my nerves.”
“I think that I may state quite positively, David,” he commenced, “that we are﻿—” but he got no further. From behind us in the vicinity of the prospector there came the most thunderous, awe-inspiring roar that ever had fallen upon my ears. With one accord we turned to discover the author of that fearsome noise.
Had I still retained the suspicion that we were on earth the sight that met my eyes would quite entirely have banished it. Emerging from the forest was a colossal beast which closely resembled a bear. It was fully as large as the largest elephant and with great forepaws armed with huge claws. Its nose, or snout, depended nearly a foot below its lower jaw, much after the manner of a rudimentary trunk. The giant body was covered by a coat of thick, shaggy hair.

===Chinese===
「我的上帝，佩里，我们到底在哪儿？」我失声道，「这事儿开始搅得我心神不宁了。」
「我想我可以相当有把握地说，戴维，」他开了腔，「我们——」可他没能说下去。我们身后探勘器附近，传来一声我平生所闻最雷霆万钧、最慑人心魄的咆哮。我们不约而同地转过身去，要看清那可怕声响的制造者。
倘若我心头还存着一丝身在地球的疑念，眼前的景象也会将它驱除得干干净净。从森林里正走出一头巨兽，模样酷似熊。它足足有最大的大象那么大，一对巨大的前掌武装着粗大的爪子。它的鼻子——或者说口鼻——垂到下颌以下近一英尺，颇像一条未发育完全的象鼻。庞大的躯体覆着一层厚厚的粗毛。

===Original===
Roaring horribly it came toward us at a ponderous, shuffling trot. I turned to Perry to suggest that it might be wise to seek other surroundings﻿—the idea had evidently occurred to Perry previously, for he was already a hundred paces away, and with each second his prodigious bounds increased the distance. I had never guessed what latent speed possibilities the old gentleman possessed.
I saw that he was headed toward a little point of the forest which ran out toward the sea not far from where we had been standing, and as the mighty creature, the sight of which had galvanized him into such remarkable action, was forging steadily toward me, I set off after Perry, though at a somewhat more decorous pace. It was evident that the massive beast pursuing us was not built for speed, so all that I considered necessary was to gain the trees sufficiently ahead of it to enable me to climb to the safety of some great branch before it came up.
Notwithstanding our danger I could not help but laugh at Perry’s frantic capers as he essayed to gain the safety of the lower branches of the trees he now had reached. The stems were bare for a distance of some fifteen feet﻿—at least on those trees which Perry attempted to ascend, for the suggestion of safety carried by the larger of the forest giants had evidently attracted him to them. A dozen times he scrambled up the trunks like a huge cat only to fall back to the ground once more, and with each failure he cast a horrified glance over his shoulder at the oncoming brute, simultaneously emitting terror-stricken shrieks that awoke the echoes of the grim forest.

===Chinese===
它发出可怕的吼声，迈着笨重拖沓的小跑朝我们走来。我转向佩里，想提议不妨换个地方待着——这个念头显然早就光顾过佩里了，因为他已在百步开外，而且每一秒钟，他那惊人的腾跃都在拉大距离。我从未料到这位老绅士身上还潜藏着这般速度。
我见他直奔森林的一处小岬角而去——那岬角伸向大海，离我们方才站立处不远。那巨兽——就是它的现身把佩里刺激出了这般了不得的动作——正稳步朝我逼近，我便也拔腿追佩里去了，只是步子体面了一些。显然，追我们的这头庞然大物不是为速度而生的，所以我盘算的只是：赶在它够着我之前先赶到树下，好赶在它到来之前爬上某根大枝，安然避祸。
尽管处境危险，瞧着佩里手忙脚乱地想爬上他终于到达的树下部枝条，我还是忍不住大笑。那些树干离地约十五英尺内光溜溜没有枝杈——至少佩里试图攀爬的那几棵是这样，因为较高大森林巨树所暗示的安全感，显然把他吸引过去了。他像一只大猫，十几次往树干上蹿，又一次次跌回地面，每一次失手，都惊恐地回头瞟一眼逼近的巨兽，同时发出声声凄厉的尖叫，惊醒了阴森森林的回声。

===Original===
At length he spied a dangling creeper about the bigness of one’s wrist, and when I reached the trees he was racing madly up it, hand over hand. He had almost reached the lowest branch of the tree from which the creeper depended when the thing parted beneath his weight and he fell sprawling at my feet.
The misfortune now was no longer amusing, for the beast was already too close to us for comfort. Seizing Perry by the shoulder I dragged him to his feet, and rushing to a smaller tree﻿—one that he could easily encircle with his arms and legs﻿—I boosted him as far up as I could, and then left him to his fate, for a glance over my shoulder revealed the awful beast almost upon me.
It was the great size of the thing alone that saved me. Its enormous bulk rendered it too slow upon its feet to cope with the agility of my young muscles, and so I was enabled to dodge out of its way and run completely behind it before its slow wits could direct it in pursuit.

===Chinese===
最后，他瞅见一条垂挂的藤蔓，约有人的手腕粗细；等我赶到树下时，他正双手交替，发疯似的往藤上爬。他差不多已经够着藤蔓所垂的那棵树的最低枝，藤蔓却在他的体重下断了，他四仰八叉摔在我脚边。
这场祸事此刻再也可笑不起来了，因为巨兽已经逼近得叫人不安。我抓住佩里的肩膀，把他拽起来，冲向一棵细一点的树——一棵他的胳膊和腿正好能环抱住的——拼命把他往上推，然后就顾不上他了，因为我回头一瞥，只见那可怕的巨兽几乎已扑到我身上。
救我性命的，全凭这家伙块头太大。它庞大的身躯使它脚下迟缓，对付不了我年轻肌肉的敏捷，于是我得以从它身侧闪开，赶在它迟钝的头脑指挥它追来之前，整个绕到了它的背后。

===Original===
The few seconds of grace that this gave me found me safely lodged in the branches of a tree a few paces from that in which Perry had at last found a haven.
Did I say safely lodged? At the time I thought we were quite safe, and so did Perry. He was praying﻿—raising his voice in thanksgiving at our deliverance﻿—and had just completed a sort of paeon of gratitude that the thing couldn’t climb a tree when without warning it reared up beneath him on its enormous tail and hind feet, and reached those fearfully armed paws quite to the branch upon which he crouched.
The accompanying roar was all but drowned in Perry’s scream of fright, and he came near tumbling headlong into the gaping jaws beneath him, so precipitate was his impetuous haste to vacate the dangerous limb. It was with a deep sigh of relief that I saw him gain a higher branch in safety.

===Chinese===
这争取来的几秒钟，让我稳稳落进了一棵树的枝桠间，离佩里终于寻到避难所的那棵只几步远。
我说了「稳稳落进」吗？当时我们自觉相当安全，佩里也这么想。他正祷告着——扬声为我们得救而谢恩——刚刚唱完一曲感恩的颂歌，庆幸那东西不会爬树，不料它毫无征兆地用粗大的尾巴和后腿在他下方直立起来，那双武装可怕的巨掌竟一直够到了他蜷伏的那根树枝。
伴着而来的咆哮几乎全被佩里的惊叫淹没，他险些一头栽进下方张开的血盆大口，急着离开那根险枝的心情是那样急切。看到他安然攀上一根更高的树枝，我才深深舒了一口气。

===Original===
And then the brute did that which froze us both anew with horror. Grasping the tree’s stem with his powerful paws he dragged down with all the great weight of his huge bulk and all the irresistible force of those mighty muscles. Slowly, but surely, the stem began to bend toward him. Inch by inch he worked his paws upward as the tree leaned more and more from the perpendicular. Perry clung chattering in a panic of terror. Higher and higher into the bending and swaying tree he clambered. More and more rapidly was the tree top inclining toward the ground.
I saw now why the great brute was armed with such enormous paws. The use that he was putting them to was precisely that for which nature had intended them. The sloth-like creature was herbivorous, and to feed that mighty carcass entire trees must be stripped of their foliage. The reason for its attacking us might easily be accounted for on the supposition of an ugly disposition such as that which the fierce and stupid rhinoceros of Africa possesses. But these were later reflections. At the moment I was too frantic with apprehension on Perry’s behalf to consider aught other than a means to save him from the death that loomed so close.
Realizing that I could outdistance the clumsy brute in the open, I dropped from my leafy sanctuary intent only on distracting the thing’s attention from Perry long enough to enable the old man to gain the safety of a larger tree. There were many close by which not even the terrific strength of that titanic monster could bend.

===Chinese===
接着，那畜生干出了一件事，叫我们俩再度吓僵了。它用有力的巨掌攥住树干，拖上它庞大身躯的全部重量，加上那身伟力肌肉不可抗拒的劲道。树干开始朝它缓缓地、却无可挽回地弯去。树身越来越偏离垂直，它便一寸一寸把巨掌往上挪。佩里死死攀着，吓得牙齿打战，在弯曲摇曳的树上越爬越高。树梢朝地面倾斜得越来越快。
我这才明白，这巨兽为何生着如此巨大的爪子。它此刻的用法，正是造化为它安排的本来的用途。这树懒般的生物是食草的，要喂饱那庞大的躯壳，就得把整棵整棵的树叶啃个精光。它之所以攻击我们，不难这样解释：它生性凶横，正如非洲那凶蛮愚蠢的犀牛一般。不过这些都是后话。当时我为佩里的安危忧心如狂，一心只想找法子救他脱离那迫在眉睫的死亡，旁的什么也顾不上想。
我明白，到了开阔地我准能跑过这笨拙的巨兽，便从枝叶葱茏的避难所一跃而下，一心只想把这东西的注意力从佩里身上引开足够久，好让老人登上某棵更大的树避险。近旁就有许多大树，连那泰坦怪物般的神力也压不弯。

===Original===
As I touched the ground I snatched a broken limb from the tangled mass that matted the jungle-like floor of the forest and, leaping unnoticed behind the shaggy back, dealt the brute a terrific blow. My plan worked like magic. From the previous slowness of the beast I had been led to look for no such marvelous agility as he now displayed. Releasing his hold upon the tree he dropped on all fours and at the same time swung his great, wicked tail with a force that would have broken every bone in my body had it struck me; but, fortunately, I had turned to flee at the very instant that I felt my blow land upon the towering back.
As it started in pursuit of me I made the mistake of running along the edge of the forest rather than making for the open beach. In a moment I was knee-deep in rotting vegetation, and the awful thing behind me was gaining rapidly as I floundered and fell in my efforts to extricate myself.
A fallen log gave me an instant’s advantage, for climbing upon it I leaped to another a few paces farther on, and in this way was able to keep clear of the mush that carpeted the surrounding ground. But the zigzag course that this necessitated was placing such a heavy handicap upon me that my pursuer was steadily gaining upon me.

===Chinese===
脚一沾地，我就从铺满森林地面、丛林也似的乱枝堆里抄起一根断枝，趁它不备跃到那多毛的脊背后方，狠狠给了那畜生一下。我这一计立竿见影。照这畜生先前那副迟缓劲儿，我压根想不到它会显出如今这般惊人的敏捷。它松开树干，四脚落地，同时抡起那条又大又凶的尾巴，那力道要是扫着我，能把我浑身骨头尽数打断；所幸在我觉出断枝砸上那高耸脊背的一刹那，我已经转身逃了。
它开始追我时，我犯了个错误：沿着森林边缘跑，而不是奔向开阔的海滩。转眼我就陷进齐膝深的腐殖烂泥里，那可怕的东西在我背后步步逼近，我在泥沼里连跌带爬。
一根倒木让我得了片刻先机：爬上它，再跳到几步外的另一根上，这样就能不沾四周地面上那层软烂如糊的东西。可这一来一去的之字形路线让我吃足了亏，追兵正稳稳地追近。

===Original===
Suddenly from behind I heard a tumult of howls, and sharp, piercing barks﻿—much the sound that a pack of wolves raises when in full cry. Involuntarily I glanced backward to discover the origin of this new and menacing note with the result that I missed my footing and went sprawling once more upon my face in the deep muck.
My mammoth enemy was so close by this time that I knew I must feel the weight of one of his terrible paws before I could rise, but to my surprise the blow did not fall upon me. The howling and snapping and barking of the new element which had been infused into the melee now seemed centered quite close behind me, and as I raised myself upon my hands and glanced around I saw what it was that had distracted the *dyryth*, as I afterward learned the thing is called, from my trail.
It was surrounded by a pack of some hundred wolf-like creatures﻿—wild dogs they seemed﻿—that rushed growling and snapping in upon it from all sides, so that they sank their white fangs into the slow brute and were away again before it could reach them with its huge paws or sweeping tail.

===Chinese===
突然，背后传来一阵嗥叫的嘈杂，还有尖利刺耳的吠声——很像一群狼全力追猎时的动静。我不由自主地回头想看清这新增威胁的来路，结果脚下踩空，又一次扑倒在深泥里。
我那猛犸般的对头此时已近在咫尺，我知道不等我爬起来，必得先挨上它那可怕巨掌的一击；可出乎意料，那一击没有落下来。新加入混战的这路角色的嗥叫、撕咬和吠声，此刻似乎就集中在我身后很近的地方。我双手撑起身子环顾，才看清是什么引开了德里思（dyryth）——后来我才知道那东西叫这个名字——对我的追踪。
它被一群约莫上百的狼样生物团团围住——看去像野狗——从四面八方咆哮着、龇咬着朝它猛冲，白森森的獠牙咬进那迟缓的巨兽，又赶在它巨掌或横扫的尾巴够着之前飞快退开。

===Original===
But these were not all that my startled eyes perceived. Chattering and gibbering through the lower branches of the trees came a company of manlike creatures evidently urging on the dog pack. They were to all appearances strikingly similar in aspect to the Negro of Africa. Their skins were very black, and their features much like those of the more pronounced Negroid type except that the head receded more rapidly above the eyes, leaving little or no forehead. Their arms were rather longer and their legs shorter in proportion to the torso than in man, and later I noticed that their great toes protruded at right angles from their feet﻿—because of their arboreal habits, I presume. Behind them trailed long, slender tails which they used in climbing quite as much as they did either their hands or feet.
I had stumbled to my feet the moment that I discovered that the wolf-dogs were holding the *dyryth* at bay. At sight of me several of the savage creatures left off worrying the great brute to come slinking with bared fangs toward me, and as I turned to run toward the trees again to seek safety among the lower branches, I saw a number of the man-apes leaping and chattering in the foliage of the nearest tree.
Between them and the beasts behind me there was little choice, but at least there was a doubt as to the reception these grotesque parodies on humanity would accord me, while there was none as to the fate which awaited me beneath the grinning fangs of my fierce pursuers.

===Chinese===
可我惊愕的眼里看到的还不止这些。一批似人的生物正穿行在树木的下层枝桠间，叽叽喳喳、咿咿呀呀，显然在给狗群助威。它们模样酷似非洲黑人。皮肤极黑，五官很像较典型的尼格罗人种，只是前额在眼睛上方收得更快，几乎不留额部。与躯干相比，它们的胳膊偏长，腿偏短；后来我还注意到，它们的大脚趾与脚掌成直角外撇——我想，这是树上生活的习性使然。它们身后拖着细长的尾巴，爬树时用得同手脚一样多。
一发现狼狗们缠住了德里思，我便挣扎着爬了起来。一看见我，几只凶悍的家伙便丢下那巨兽，龇着牙朝我悄然逼近；我转身又要往树下跑，想到低枝间寻个安全，却见最近的树上正有几个人猿似的东西在枝叶间蹦跳喧哗。
在它们和身后那群野兽之间，几乎无从选择；可至少，这些对人类的滑稽模仿会给我什么接待，尚在未定之天，而在我那些凶残追兵龇笑的獠牙之下等着我的命运，却是明摆着的。

===Original===
And so I raced on toward the trees intending to pass beneath that which held the man-things and take refuge in another farther on; but the wolf-dogs were very close behind me﻿—so close that I had despaired of escaping them, when one of the creatures in the tree above swung down headforemost, his tail looped about a great limb, and grasping me beneath my armpits swung me in safety up among his fellows.
There they fell to examining me with the utmost excitement and curiosity. They picked at my clothing, my hair, and my flesh. They turned me about to see if I had a tail, and when they discovered that I was not so equipped they fell into roars of laughter. Their teeth were very large and white and even, except for the upper canines which were a trifle longer than the others﻿—protruding just a bit when the mouth was closed.
When they had examined me for a few moments one of them discovered that my clothing was not a part of me, with the result that garment by garment they tore it from me amidst peals of the wildest laughter. Apelike, they essayed to don the apparel themselves, but their ingenuity was not sufficient to the task and so they gave it up.

===Chinese===
于是我朝树下奔去，打算从栖着那些猿人的树下穿过，躲进更前面的一棵里；可狼狗们已紧逼到我身后——近得我几乎断了逃脱的指望，这时上方树里的一只生物头朝下荡了下来，尾巴在一根大枝上挽了个圈，一把攥住我的两边腋下，把我稳稳荡上去，送到它的同伴中间。
它们随即兴冲冲、好奇万分地打量起我来。它们扯我的衣服、我的头发、我的皮肉。它们把我转来转去，看我有没有尾巴，等发现我竟没有这装备，便哄堂大笑起来。它们的牙齿又大又白又整齐，只有上犬齿比别的牙略长些——嘴合拢时会稍稍露出来。
它们端详了我一阵之后，其中一只发现我的衣着并非我身体的一部分，结果它们便一件一件，把衣服从我身上撕了下来，笑声连成一片，狂野至极。它们学猴子的样，想自己把衣裳穿戴起来，可那份巧劲不够使，只得作罢。

===Original===
In the meantime I had been straining my eyes to catch a glimpse of Perry, but nowhere about could I see him, although the clump of trees in which he had first taken refuge was in full view. I was much exercised by fear that something had befallen him, and though I called his name aloud several times there was no response.
Tired at last of playing with my clothing the creatures threw it to the ground, and catching me, one on either side, by an arm, started off at a most terrifying pace through the tree tops. Never have I experienced such a journey before or since﻿—even now I oftentimes awake from a deep sleep haunted by the horrid remembrance of that awful experience.
From tree to tree the agile creatures sprang like flying squirrels, while the cold sweat stood upon my brow as I glimpsed the depths beneath, into which a single misstep on the part of either of my bearers would hurl me. As they bore me along, my mind was occupied with a thousand bewildering thoughts. What had become of Perry? Would I ever see him again? What were the intentions of these half-human things into whose hands I had fallen? Were they inhabitants of the same world into which I had been born? No! It could not be. But yet where else? I had not left that earth﻿—of that I was sure. Still neither could I reconcile the things which I had seen to a belief that I was still in the world of my birth. With a sigh I gave it up.

===Chinese===
这当口，我一直瞪大眼睛想望见佩里，可哪儿也瞧不见他，尽管他最初避难的那一丛树就在视野之内。我忧心忡忡，生怕他遭了什么不测，虽然我高声喊了他几遍名字，却没有回音。
那些生物玩够了我的衣服，把它扔到地上，随即两边各一个，攥住我一条胳膊，以一种极其吓人的速度，穿行树冠而去。此前后此，我从未经历过这样一段旅程——直到如今，我还时常从沉睡中惊醒，被那段可怕经历的骇人记忆纠缠。
这些敏捷的生物像鼯鼠一样从一棵树跃向另一棵树，冷汗浸透我的额头，因为每当我瞥见下方的深渊——两位「轿夫」中任何一个失脚，都会把我抛进去。被它们抬着前行时，我脑子里塞满千头万绪。佩里怎么了？我还能见到他吗？这些半人的东西——我落到了它们手里——意欲何为？它们是我出生的那个世界的居民吗？不！不可能。可不是这儿又是哪儿？我没有离开那个地球——这一点我有把握。可同样，我又无法把亲眼所见的一切，同「我仍在出生的世界里」这个信念调和起来。我叹了口气，索性不去想了。

## XV Back to Earth / 重返地面

===Original===
We crossed the river and passed through the mountains beyond, and finally we came out upon a great level plain which stretched away as far as the eye could reach. I cannot tell you in what direction it stretched even if you would care to know, for all the while that I was within Pellucidar I never discovered any but local methods of indicating direction﻿—there is no north, no south, no east, no west. *Up* is about the only direction which is well defined, and that, of course, is *down* to you of the outer crust. Since the sun neither rises nor sets there is no method of indicating direction beyond visible objects such as high mountains, forests, lakes, and seas.
The plain which lies beyond the white cliffs which flank the Darel Az upon the shore nearest the Mountains of the Clouds is about as near to any direction as any Pellucidarian can come. If you happen not to have heard of the Darel Az, or the white cliffs, or the Mountains of the Clouds you feel that there is something lacking, and long for the good old understandable northeast and southwest of the outer world.
We had barely entered the great plain when we discovered two enormous animals approaching us from a great distance. So far were they that we could not distinguish what manner of beasts they might be, but as they came closer, I saw that they were enormous quadrupeds, eighty or a hundred feet long, with tiny heads perched at the top of very long necks. Their heads must have been quite forty feet from the ground. The beasts moved very slowly﻿—that is their action was slow﻿—but their strides covered such a great distance that in reality they traveled considerably faster than a man walks.
===Chinese===
我们渡过河，穿过河对岸的群山，终于来到一片一望无际的大平原上。它朝哪个方向延伸，我没法告诉你，纵然你想知道也没用，因为在佩卢西达尔逗留的整段日子里，我从没发现除了本地办法之外还有什么指示方向的法子——那里没有北，没有南，没有东，没有西。*上*差不多是唯一界限明确的方向，而它对外层地壳的你们来说，当然就是*下*。太阳既不升也不落，因此除了高山、森林、湖泊、海洋这类看得见的物体，再没有别的指示方向的凭借。
白色断崖之外的那片平原——断崖在达雷尔阿兹（Darel Az）离云中山脉最近的一岸侧翼延伸——大约就是一个佩卢西达尔人所能达到的、最接近「方向」的东西了。倘若你碰巧没听说过达雷尔阿兹，没听说过白崖或云中山脉，你就会觉得缺点什么，不由怀念起外层世界那亲切好懂的东北、西南来。
我们刚踏上大平原，就发现两头巨兽自远处朝我们走来。距离太远，辨不出是何等兽类；待它们走近些，我才看出那是两头巨大的四足兽，身长八十到一百英尺，小小的脑袋架在极长的脖颈顶上，离地足有四十英尺。它们动作很迟缓——我是说动作迟缓——可步子跨得极大，实际行进的速度比人走路快得多。

===Original===
As they drew still nearer we discovered that upon the back of each sat a human being. Then Dian knew what they were, though she never before had seen one.
“They are *lidis* from the land of the Thorians,” she cried. “Thoria lies at the outer verge of the Land of Awful Shadow. The Thorians alone of all the races of Pellucidar ride the *lidi*, for nowhere else than beside the dark country are they found.”
“What is the Land of Awful Shadow?” I asked.
“It is the land which lies beneath the Dead World,” replied Dian; “the Dead World which hangs forever between the sun and Pellucidar above the Land of Awful Shadow. It is the Dead World which makes the great shadow upon this portion of Pellucidar.”
===Chinese===
它们再走近些，我们发现每头兽背上坐着一个人。黛安于是认出了它们是什么，虽然她从未见过。
「它们是索里亚人（Thoria）之地来的*利迪*（*lidi*），」她喊道，「索里亚位于可怖之影之地的最外缘。佩卢西达尔诸种族中，只有索里亚人骑*利迪*，因为这种兽除了那黑暗国度旁边，别处找不到。」
「什么是可怖之影之地？」我问。
「就是悬在死亡世界下面的那片地方，」黛安答道，「死亡世界永远悬在太阳与佩卢西达尔之间、可怖之影之地的上空，是它在佩卢西达尔的这一部分投下了巨大的阴影。」

===Original===
I did not fully understand what she meant, nor am I sure that I do yet, for I have never been to that part of Pellucidar from which the Dead World is visible; but Perry says that it is the moon of Pellucidar﻿—a tiny planet within a planet﻿—and that it revolves around the earth’s axis coincidently with the earth, and thus is always above the same spot within Pellucidar.
I remember that Perry was very much excited when I told him about this Dead World, for he seemed to think that it explained the hitherto inexplicable phenomena of nutation and the precession of the equinoxes.
When the two upon the *lidis* had come quite close to us we saw that one was a man and the other a woman. The former had held up his two hands, palms toward us, in sign of peace, and I had answered him in kind, when he suddenly gave a cry of astonishment and pleasure, and slipping from his enormous mount ran forward toward Dian, throwing his arms about her.
In an instant I was white with jealousy, but only for an instant; since Dian quickly drew the man toward me, telling him that I was David, her mate.
===Chinese===
我没有完全明白她的意思，现在也不敢说就明白了，因为我从未到过佩卢西达尔能望见死亡世界的那个地区；不过据佩里说，它是佩卢西达尔的月亮——行星之中的一颗小行星——沿着地轴与地球同步旋转，因而永远悬在佩卢西达尔的同一处上方。
我记得当我把死亡世界讲给佩里听时，他兴奋极了，因为他似乎认为这解释了迄今无从解释的章动现象和岁差现象。
骑*利迪*的两人来到近前，我们才看清一男一女。男的举起双手、掌心朝我们，以示和平，我也照式还礼；忽然他惊喜地叫了一声，从那庞然坐骑上一滑而下，奔向黛安，张开双臂抱住了她。
一瞬间我妒火中烧，脸都白了；但也只有一瞬间，因为黛安随即把那人拉到我面前，告诉他我就是戴维，她的伴侣。

===Original===
“And this is my brother, Dacor the Strong One, David,” she said to me.
It appeared that the woman was Dacor’s mate. He had found none to his liking among the Sari, nor farther on until he had come to the land of the Thoria, and there he had found and fought for this very lovely Thorian maiden whom he was bringing back to his own people.
When they had heard our story and our plans they decided to accompany us to Sari, that Dacor and Ghak might come to an agreement relative to an alliance, as Dacor was quite as enthusiastic about the proposed annihilation of the Mahars and Sagoths as either Dian or I.
After a journey which was, for Pellucidar, quite uneventful, we came to the first of the Sarian villages which consists of between one and two hundred artificial caves cut into the face of a great cliff. Here to our immense delight, we found both Perry and Ghak. The old man was quite overcome at sight of me for he had long since given me up as dead.
===Chinese===
「这位是我哥哥，强者达科尔（Dacor the Strong One），戴维。」她对我说。
原来那女子是达科尔的妻子。他在萨里人中没挑中意的，一路前行，直到索里亚人之地才遇到这位可爱的索里亚少女，为得到她而战，如今正带她回自己的族人那里去。
他们听了我们的经历和计划，便决定陪我们同去萨里，好让达科尔与格哈克就结盟事宜达成协议，因为对于消灭玛哈尔人和萨戈思人这一提议，达科尔的热忱丝毫不亚于黛安或我。
一路平安无事——以佩卢西达尔的标准而言——我们来到了第一座萨里人村落。村子由一百到两百个凿在大崖壁上的人工洞穴组成。令我们大喜过望的是，佩里和格哈克都在这里。老人一见我激动得难以自持，因为他早已认定我死了。

===Original===
When I introduced Dian as my wife, he didn’t quite know what to say, but he afterward remarked that with the pick of two worlds I could not have done better.
Ghak and Dacor reached a very amicable arrangement, and it was at a council of the head men of the various tribes of the Sari that the eventual form of government was tentatively agreed upon. Roughly, the various kingdoms were to remain virtually independent, but there was to be one great overlord, or emperor. It was decided that I should be the first of the dynasty of the emperors of Pellucidar.
We set about teaching the women how to make bows and arrows, and poison pouches. The young men hunted the vipers which provided the virus, and it was they who mined the iron ore, and fashioned the swords under Perry’s direction. Rapidly the fever spread from one tribe to another until representatives from nations so far distant that the Sarians had never even heard of them came in to take the oath of allegiance which we required, and to learn the art of making the new weapons and using them.
We sent our young men out as instructors to every nation of the federation, and the movement had reached colossal proportions before the Mahars discovered it. The first intimation they had was when three of their great slave caravans were annihilated in rapid succession. They could not comprehend that the lower orders had suddenly developed a power which rendered them really formidable.
===Chinese===
我介绍黛安是我妻子时，他一时不知说什么好；不过后来他说，既然是两个世界里挑出来的，我不能挑得更好了。
格哈克和达科尔达成了十分友好的协议；正是在萨里各部落头人们的议事会上，最终的政体形式初步议定。大致说来，各王国仍保持事实上的独立，但须共戴一位大宗主，即皇帝。会议决定，由我出任佩卢西达尔皇朝的第一代皇帝。
我们着手教妇女们制作弓箭和毒囊。小伙子们猎取提供毒液的蝰蛇，也正是他们在佩里指导下开采铁矿、锻造刀剑。这股热潮迅速从一个部落蔓延到另一个部落，直到遥远得连萨里人从未听说过的那些国家也派代表前来，宣誓向我们效忠，学习制造和使用新武器的技艺。
我们派出本族的青年去联邦的每一国充当教习；等玛哈尔人发现时，运动已成燎原之势。他们最初得到的风声，是三支庞大的掠奴商队在短时间内相继被歼。他们无法理解，下等种族竟突然发展出一种使他们真正可畏的力量。

===Original===
In one of the skirmishes with slave caravans some of our Sarians took a number of Sagoth prisoners, and among them were two who had been members of the guards within the building where we had been confined at Phutra. They told us that the Mahars were frantic with rage when they discovered what had taken place in the cellars of the buildings. The Sagoths knew that something very terrible had befallen their masters, but the Mahars had been most careful to see that no inkling of the true nature of their vital affliction reached beyond their own race. How long it would take for the race to become extinct it was impossible even to guess; but that this must eventually happen seemed inevitable.
The Mahars had offered fabulous rewards for the capture of any one of us alive, and at the same time had threatened to inflict the direst punishment upon whomever should harm us. The Sagoths could not understand these seemingly paradoxical instructions, though their purpose was quite evident to me. The Mahars wanted the Great Secret, and they knew that we alone could deliver it to them.
Perry’s experiments in the manufacture of gunpowder and the fashioning of rifles had not progressed as rapidly as we had hoped﻿—there was a whole lot about these two arts which Perry didn’t know. We were both assured that the solution of these problems would advance the cause of civilization within Pellucidar thousands of years at a single stroke. Then there were various other arts and sciences which we wished to introduce, but our combined knowledge of them did not embrace the mechanical details which alone could render them of commercial, or practical value.
“David,” said Perry, immediately after his latest failure to produce gunpowder that would even burn, “one of us must return to the outer world and bring back the information we lack. Here we have all the labor and materials for reproducing anything that ever has been produced above﻿—what we lack is knowledge. Let us go back and get that knowledge in the shape of books﻿—then this world will indeed be at our feet.”
===Chinese===
在与掠奴队的一次小规模冲突中，我们的一些萨里人抓了一批萨戈思俘虏，其中两人正是当初富特拉那座囚禁我们的楼里的看守。他们告诉我们，玛哈尔人发现楼内地窖里发生的事情之后，暴跳如雷。萨戈思人只知道他们的主人遭了大殃，但玛哈尔人千方百计不让这一致命祸患的真实性质泄露到本族之外。这个种族还要多久才彻底灭绝，连猜也无法猜；但这结局看来势所难免。
玛哈尔人悬了天价赏格，要活捉我们中的任何一个；同时又威胁说，谁若伤害我们，必受最酷烈的惩罚。萨戈思人理解不了这些看似自相矛盾的指令，其用意我却一清二楚。玛哈尔人要的是大秘密（the Great Secret），而他们知道，能把它交到他们手里的只有我们。
佩里制造火药和打造步枪的试验进展不如我们期望的那样快——这两门学问里有太多东西佩里并不在行。我们俩都确信，解决了这些问题，佩卢西达尔的文明事业将一步跨越几千年。此外还有种种别的技艺和科学想引进来，但我们的知识加在一起，也不包含那些能使它们具有商业或实用价值的机械细节。
「戴维，」佩里说——他那回造出的火药连燃烧都不肯——「咱们俩必须有一个回外层世界去，把咱们欠缺的知识带回来。这里有的是劳力和材料，足以复制外层出产过的任何东西——咱们缺的是知识。让咱们回去，把知识装成书本带回来——到那时，这个世界才真正是咱们的囊中之物。」

===Original===
And so it was decided that I should return in the prospector, which still lay upon the edge of the forest at the point where we had first penetrated to the surface of the inner world. Dian would not listen to any arrangement for my going which did not include her, and I was not sorry that she wished to accompany me, for I wanted her to see my world, and I wanted my world to see her.
With a large force of men we marched to the great iron mole, which Perry soon had hoisted into position with its nose pointed back toward the outer crust. He went over all the machinery carefully. He replenished the air tanks, and manufactured oil for the engine. At last everything was ready, and we were about to set out when our pickets, a long, thin line of which had surrounded our camp at all times, reported that a great body of what appeared to be Sagoths and Mahars were approaching from the direction of Phutra.
Dian and I were ready to embark, but I was anxious to witness the first clash between two fair-sized armies of the opposing races of Pellucidar. I realized that this was to mark the historic beginning of a mighty struggle for possession of a world, and as the first emperor of Pellucidar I felt that it was not alone my duty, but my right, to be in the thick of that momentous struggle.
===Chinese===
于是议定由我乘地下探勘器返回。它还停在我们初次钻透地心世界表面的那片森林边上。凡是不带黛安同行的安排，她一概不听；而她愿意陪我同行，我也正求之不得，因为我要她看看我的世界，也要我的世界看看她。
我们率领大队人马开到那台大铁鼹鼠跟前，佩里很快把它吊装到位，鼻子直指外层地壳。他把全部机械仔细检查了一遍，给空气罐补足了气，又给发动机配制了油。最后一切就绪，我们正要动身，警戒哨——营区四周始终布着一圈细长的警戒线——来报，说一大队看模样是萨戈思人和玛哈尔人的队伍正从富特拉方向开来。
黛安和我已准备登程，但我渴望亲眼目睹佩卢西达尔对立两族两支像样军队的第一次交锋。我意识到这将标志一场争夺整个世界的宏大斗争的历史性开端；作为佩卢西达尔的第一位皇帝，我觉得置身这场重大斗争的最前沿，不独是我的本分，也是我的权利。

===Original===
As the opposing army approached we saw that there were many Mahars with the Sagoth troops﻿—an indication of the vast importance which the dominant race placed upon the outcome of this campaign, for it was not customary with them to take active part in the sorties which their creatures made for slaves﻿—the only form of warfare which they waged upon the lower orders.
Ghak and Dacor were both with us, having come primarily to view the prospector. I placed Ghak with some of his Sarians on the right of our battle line. Dacor took the left, while I commanded the center. Behind us I stationed a sufficient reserve under one of Ghak’s head men. The Sagoths advanced steadily with menacing spears, and I let them come until they were within easy bowshot before I gave the word to fire.
At the first volley of poison-tipped arrows the front ranks of the gorilla-men crumpled to the ground; but those behind charged over the prostrate forms of their comrades in a wild, mad rush to be upon us with their spears. A second volley stopped them for an instant, and then my reserve sprang through the openings in the firing line to engage them with sword and shield. The clumsy spears of the Sagoths were no match for the swords of the Sarian and Amozite, who turned the spear thrusts aside with their shields and leaped to close quarters with their lighter, handier weapons.
===Chinese===
敌军走近后，我们看到萨戈思军队里有许多玛哈尔人——这说明统治种族何等看重此役的胜负，因为他们一向不惯于亲自参与爪牙们的掠奴出击——那是他们对下等种族发动的唯一一种战争。
格哈克和达科尔都在我们这边，他们此来主要是想看看探勘器。我把格哈克和他的一部分萨里人布置在我方战线的右翼，达科尔居左，我亲自坐镇中路。后方我留了足够的后备队，交由格哈克的一名头人统领。萨戈思人端着威慑的长矛稳步进逼，我放他们进入到弓箭射程之内，才下令开火。
毒箭第一轮齐射，猿人的前排便纷纷栽倒在地；后面的踏着倒下同伴的躯体，狂呼怒吼着挺矛扑来。第二轮齐射把他们遏止了一瞬，随后我的后备队便从射击线的空隙里冲出，以刀盾接战。萨戈思人笨重的长矛根本敌不过萨里人和阿莫兹人的刀剑：他们用盾牌格开矛刺，跃身近战，轻巧顺手的兵刃大占上风。

===Original===
Ghak took his archers along the enemy’s flank, and while the swordsmen engaged them in front, he poured volley after volley into their unprotected left. The Mahars did little real fighting, and were more in the way than otherwise, though occasionally one of them would fasten its powerful jaw upon the arm or leg of a Sarian.
The battle did not last a great while, for when Dacor and I led our men in upon the Sagoth’s right with naked swords they were already so demoralized that they turned and fled before us. We pursued them for some time, taking many prisoners and recovering nearly a hundred slaves, among whom was Hooja the Sly One.
He told me that he had been captured while on his way to his own land; but that his life had been spared in hope that through him the Mahars would learn the whereabouts of their Great Secret. Ghak and I were inclined to think that the Sly One had been guiding this expedition to the land of Sari, where he thought that the book might be found in Perry’s possession; but we had no proof of this and so we took him in and treated him as one of us, although none liked him. And how he rewarded my generosity you will presently learn.
There were a number of Mahars among our prisoners, and so fearful were our own people of them that they would not approach them unless completely covered from the sight of the reptiles by a piece of skin. Even Dian shared the popular superstition regarding the evil effects of exposure to the eyes of angry Mahars, and though I laughed at her fears I was willing enough to humor them if it would relieve her apprehension in any degree, and so she sat apart from the prospector, near which the Mahars had been chained, while Perry and I again inspected every portion of the mechanism.
===Chinese===
格哈克带着弓箭手沿敌军侧翼推进，当剑士们在正面缠住敌人时，他一轮接一轮地把箭泼向敌军不设防的左翼。玛哈尔人没打什么真正的仗，多半是碍手碍脚，只是偶尔有一只咬住哪个萨里人的胳膊或腿，那腭骨力道惊人。
战斗为时不长。当达科尔和我挥着出鞘的剑率部冲向萨戈思人的右翼时，他们早已军心涣散，转身在我们面前溃逃了。我们追击了一阵，俘获甚众，还救回近一百名奴隶，其中就有「狡猾鬼」胡贾（Hooja the Sly One）。
他告诉我，他是在回自己国土的路上被掳的；但留他性命，是指望能借他探出他们那大秘密的下落。格哈克和我都倾向于认为，这「狡猾鬼」一直是在给这支远征队带路，往萨里之地去——他以为那本书或许就在佩里手里；但我们没有证据，便收留了他，待他如自己人，尽管谁都不喜欢他。他怎样报答我的宽厚，你稍后自会知道。
俘虏中有几名玛哈尔人，我们自己的人对他们怕得要命，不蒙上一块兽皮把爬虫的视线完全隔开，就不肯走近。连黛安也信这套流行的说法——惹怒的玛哈尔人的眼睛照到会有恶果；我虽笑她胆小，但只要能稍减她的忧惧，我也乐于将就。于是她坐在离探勘器有一段距离的地方——玛哈尔人就被拴在探勘器近旁——我和佩里则再次把机械的每个部件检查了一遍。

===Original===
At last I took my place in the driving seat, and called to one of the men without to fetch Dian. It happened that Hooja stood quite close to the doorway of the prospector, so that it was he who, without my knowledge, went to bring her; but how he succeeded in accomplishing the fiendish thing he did, I cannot guess, unless there were others in the plot to aid him. Nor can I believe that, since all my people were loyal to me and would have made short work of Hooja had he suggested the heartless scheme, even had he had time to acquaint another with it. It was all done so quickly that I may only believe that it was the result of sudden impulse, aided by a number of, to Hooja, fortuitous circumstances occurring at precisely the right moment.
All I know is that it was Hooja who brought Dian to the prospector, still wrapped from head to toe in the skin of an enormous cave lion which covered her since the Mahar prisoners had been brought into camp. He deposited his burden in the seat beside me. I was all ready to get under way. The goodbyes had been said. Perry had grasped my hand in the last, long farewell. I closed and barred the outer and inner doors, took my seat again at the driving mechanism, and pulled the starting lever.
As before on that far-gone night that had witnessed our first trial of the iron monster, there was a frightful roaring beneath us﻿—the giant frame trembled and vibrated﻿—there was a rush of sound as the loose earth passed up through the hollow space between the inner and outer jackets to be deposited in our wake. Once more the thing was off.
===Chinese===
最后我在驾驶座上坐定，叫外面的人去请黛安。恰巧胡贾就站在探勘器门口近旁，于是趁我不知情，去带她的正是他；至于他怎样做成那件伤天害理的事，我猜不出来，除非同谋另有其人。我也不信有同谋，因为我的人个个忠于我，胡贾纵然有时间串通旁人，只要吐露半句那狠毒的计谋，也早叫他们结果了。事情办得那样快，我只能相信这是一时冲动的产物，外加几桩对胡贾而言恰逢其时、不多不少凑在那要命关头的巧事。
我只知道，把黛安带到探勘器跟前的确实是胡贾。她自玛哈尔俘虏押进营地起，就从头到脚裹着一张巨狮的皮。他把这负担放到我身旁的座位上。我一切就绪，只待启程。道别的话已说完，佩里握过我的手，作了最后的、长长的诀别。我关上并闩牢外门和内门，重新在驾驶机件前坐好，扳动了启动杆。
正像很久以前那见证我们初次试驾这铁怪的夜晚一样，我们脚下传来可怕的轰鸣——巨大的骨架震颤抖动——松土嗖嗖上涌，穿过内外壳之间的空腔，抛撒在我们身后的路上。这家伙又一次上路了。

===Original===
But on the instant of departure I was nearly thrown from my seat by the sudden lurching of the prospector. At first I did not realize what had happened, but presently it dawned upon me that just before entering the crust the towering body had fallen through its supporting scaffolding, and that instead of entering the ground vertically we were plunging into it at a different angle. Where it would bring us out upon the upper crust I could not even conjecture. And then I turned to note the effect of this strange experience upon Dian. She still sat shrouded in the great skin.
“Come, come,” I cried, laughing, “come out of your shell. No Mahar eyes can reach you here,” and I leaned over and snatched the lion skin from her. And then I shrank back upon my seat in utter horror.
The thing beneath the skin was not Dian﻿—it was a hideous Mahar. Instantly I realized the trick that Hooja had played upon me, and the purpose of it. Rid of me, forever as he doubtless thought, Dian would be at his mercy. Frantically I tore at the steering wheel in an effort to turn the prospector back toward Pellucidar; but, as on that other occasion, I could not budge the thing a hair.
===Chinese===
可就在启程的一刹那，探勘器猛地一歪，差点把我从座位上甩出去。起初我不明白出了什么事，随后才恍然：就在钻入地壳之前，那高耸的机身从支撑架上塌落了下来，于是我们不是垂直入地，而是斜插进一个角度入地。它会把我们带上外层地壳的哪一点，我连猜也无处猜起。我这才回头去看这场怪异的变故对黛安的影响。她还坐在那儿，裹在那张大兽皮里。
「来呀，来呀，」我笑着喊道，「从你的壳里出来吧。这儿没有玛哈尔人的眼睛够得着你。」我探身过去，一把掀掉她身上的狮皮——随即满怀惊骇，缩回座位上。
兽皮底下的那东西不是黛安——是一只面目狰狞的玛哈尔人。我立刻明白了胡贾对我耍的花招，也明白了它的用意。甩掉了我——他无疑认定是永远甩掉了——黛安便任他摆布了。我发疯似的扳动舵轮，想让探勘器折回佩卢西达尔；可是正如上回一样，那东西纹丝不动，一毫也搬不动。

===Original===
It is needless to recount the horrors or the monotony of that journey. It varied but little from the former one which had brought us from the outer to the inner world. Because of the angle at which we had entered the ground the trip required nearly a day longer, and brought me out here upon the sand of the Sahara instead of in the United States as I had hoped.
For months I have been waiting here for a white man to come. I dared not leave the prospector for fear I should never be able to find it again﻿—the shifting sands of the desert would soon cover it, and then my only hope of returning to my Dian and her Pellucidar would be gone forever.
That I ever shall see her again seems but remotely possible, for how may I know upon what part of Pellucidar my return journey may terminate﻿—and how, without a north or south or an east or a west may I hope ever to find my way across that vast world to the tiny spot where my lost love lies grieving for me?
===Chinese===
那趟旅程的恐怖与单调，不必细述。它同把我们从外层世界带到内层世界的那一趟几乎没有分别。由于入地的角度偏斜，这趟多花了将近一天，把我送到了这里的撒哈拉沙漠的沙地上，而不是我希望的美国。
数月以来，我一直在这里等候白人来。我不敢离开探勘器，生怕再也找不到它——流沙很快就会把它埋掉，那时我重返我的黛安、重返她的佩卢西达尔的唯一指望，便永远完了。
我是否还能再见她一面，看来希望渺茫；因为我怎知道重返的旅程会在佩卢西达尔的哪一处终结？而没有了北、没有了南、没有了东和西，我又怎能指望穿越那茫茫世界，找到我那失恋的爱人为我悲伤垂泪的那一小点地方？

===Original===
---
That is the story as David Innes told it to me in the goatskin tent upon the rim of the great Sahara Desert. The next day he took me out to see the prospector﻿—it was precisely as he had described it. So huge was it that it could have been brought to this inaccessible part of the world by no means of transportation that existed there﻿—it could only have come in the way that David Innes said it came﻿—up through the crust of the earth from the inner world of Pellucidar.
I spent a week with him, and then, abandoning my lion hunt, returned directly to the coast and hurried to London where I purchased a great quantity of stuff which he wished to take back to Pellucidar with him. There were books, rifles, revolvers, ammunition, cameras, chemicals, telephones, telegraph instruments, wire, tools and more books﻿—books upon every subject under the sun. He said he wanted a library with which they could reproduce the wonders of the twentieth century in the Stone Age and if quantity counts for anything I got it for him.
I took the things back to Algeria myself, and accompanied them to the end of the railroad; but from here I was recalled to America upon important business. However, I was able to employ a very trustworthy man to take charge of the caravan﻿—the same guide, in fact, who had accompanied me on the previous trip into the Sahara﻿—and after writing a long letter to Innes in which I gave him my American address, I saw the expedition head south.
===Chinese===
---
这就是戴维·英尼斯在浩瀚撒哈拉沙漠边缘那顶山羊皮帐篷里讲给我听的故事。第二天他带我去看那台探勘器——同他描述的一模一样。它那么庞大，世界上现有的任何运输工具都无法把它弄到这个与世隔绝的地方来——它只能照戴维·英尼斯所说的那条路来——从佩卢西达尔这个内层世界，穿过地壳一路钻上来。
我陪了他一个星期，随后放弃猎狮，径直返回海岸，赶赴伦敦，在那里购置了他希望带回佩卢西达尔的大批物件：书籍、步枪、左轮枪、弹药、照相机、化学品、电话机、电报机、电线、工具，还有更多的书——天下万般学问，无所不有的书。他说他要一座图书馆，好让他们在石器时代重现二十世纪的种种奇迹；要说数量顶用的话，我可是给他弄到了。
我亲自把这些东西运回阿尔及利亚，一路押送到铁路的尽头；可从那里，我因要事被召回美国。不过我雇到了一个非常可靠的人来照管这支商队——事实上正是上回陪我进撒哈拉的那位向导——又给英尼斯写了封长信，留下我的美国地址，然后目送这支队伍向南进发。

===Original===
Among the other things which I sent to Innes was over five hundred miles of double, insulated wire of a very fine gauge. I had it packed on a special reel at his suggestion, as it was his idea that he could fasten one end here before he left and by paying it out through the end of the prospector lay a telegraph line between the outer and inner worlds. In my letter I told him to be sure to mark the terminus of the line very plainly with a high cairn, in case I was not able to reach him before he set out, so that I might easily find and communicate with him should he be so fortunate as to reach Pellucidar.
I received several letters from him after I returned to America﻿—in fact he took advantage of every northward-passing caravan to drop me word of some sort. His last letter was written the day before he intended to depart. Here it is.

> My Dear Friend:
> Tomorrow I shall set out in quest of Pellucidar and Dian. That is if the Arabs don’t get me. They have been very nasty of late. I don’t know the cause, but on two occasions they have threatened my life. One, more friendly than the rest, told me today that they intended attacking me tonight. It would be unfortunate should anything of that sort happen now that I am so nearly ready to depart.
> However, maybe I will be as well off, for the nearer the hour approaches, the slenderer my chances for success appear.
===Chinese===
我送给英尼斯的物件中，有五百多英里双股绝缘的细号线。照他的建议，我把线绕在一个特制的线轴上；因为他的主意是，动身前把线的一端固定在这边，让线从探勘器尾部一路放出，在外层与内层两个世界之间架起一条电报线。我在信里叮嘱他，万一起程前我赶不到，一定要在线的末端立一座高高的石冢，标记得清清楚楚，这样，倘若他福星高照、真到了佩卢西达尔，我也能轻易找到他、同他通信。
我回美国后，收到他好几封信——事实上，每一支北上的商队路过，他都要托他们捎个口信。他的最后一封信写在他打算动身的前一天。信就在这里。

> 我亲爱的朋友：
> 明天我就要动身去寻访佩卢西达尔和黛安了——只要阿拉伯人不来堵我。他们近来很不友善，原因我不清楚，但已有两回威胁我的性命。今天有个比旁人友善些的告诉我，他们打算今夜来袭。眼下我行装将齐，偏偏这时出这种事，可就糟了。
> 不过，兴许我横竖都是一样；因为动身时辰越近，我成功的指望看上去就越纤细。

===Original===
> Here is the friendly Arab who is to take this letter north for me, so goodbye, and God bless you for your kindness to me.
> The Arab tells me to hurry, for he sees a cloud of sand to the south﻿—he thinks it is the party coming to murder me, and he doesn’t want to be found with me. So goodbye again.
> Yours,
> David Innes

A year later found me at the end of the railroad once more, headed for the spot where I had left Innes. My first disappointment was when I discovered that my old guide had died within a few weeks of my return, nor could I find any member of my former party who could lead me to the same spot.
For months I searched that scorching land, interviewing countless desert sheiks in the hope that at last I might find one who had heard of Innes and his wonderful iron mole. Constantly my eyes scanned the blinding waste of sand for the rocky cairn beneath which I was to find the wires leading to Pellucidar﻿—but always was I unsuccessful.
===Chinese===
> 那位肯帮忙的阿拉伯人就要替我把这封信带往北边了，那么再会吧，愿上帝保佑你，报答你对我的种种好处。
> 那阿拉伯人催我快点，他看见南边扬起了一团沙尘——他估摸那是来杀我的一伙人到了，他可不想让人发现他同我在一起。那么再一次道别。
> 你的，
> 戴维·英尼斯

一年之后，我又来到铁路的尽头，朝我留下英尼斯的那个地点进发。第一重失望是，我发现老向导在我回去后不出几个星期便死了，原先那一队人里，也再找不出一个能领我到原地的人。
好几个月，我都在那片灼热的土地上搜寻，会见了数不清的沙漠舍赫，指望终究能遇到一个听说过英尼斯和他那台奇妙铁鼹鼠的人。我的眼睛不断扫视那片晃眼的荒沙，寻找那座石头堆的标塔——塔下应当埋着通往佩卢西达尔的电线——然而始终一无所获。

===Original===
And always do these awful questions harass me when I think of David Innes and his strange adventures.
Did the Arabs murder him, after all, just on the eve of his departure? Or, did he again turn the nose of his iron monster toward the inner world? Did he reach it, or lies he somewhere buried in the heart of the great crust? And if he did come again to Pellucidar was it to break through into the bottom of one of her great island seas, or among some savage race far, far from the land of his heart’s desire?
Does the answer lie somewhere upon the bosom of the broad Sahara, at the end of two tiny wires, hidden beneath a lost cairn? I wonder.
===Chinese===
而每当想起戴维·英尼斯和他的奇异历险，这几个可怕的问题总在纠缠我。
阿拉伯人到底还是没让他走到动身前夜？还是，他又把那铁怪的鼻子掉向了内层世界？他到达了吗，还是横躺在巨壳深处某处，埋骨沙石？倘若他真的又回到了佩卢西达尔，他是破水而出，钻进某座海上大岛的湖底呢，还是落在某个蛮族之中，离他心之所系的国土千里之遥？
答案会不会就藏在这片浩瀚撒哈拉的某处怀抱里，在两根细导线的尽头，埋在一座无人知晓的石冢之下？我不知道。

## IV Dian the Beautiful / 四 美人黛安

===Original===
When our guards aroused us from sleep we were much refreshed. They gave us food. Strips of dried meat it was, but it put new life and strength into us, so that now we too marched with high-held heads, and took noble strides. At least I did, for I was young and proud; but poor Perry hated walking. On earth I had often seen him call a cab to travel a square﻿—he was paying for it now, and his old legs wobbled so that I put my arm about him and half carried him through the balance of those frightful marches.
The country began to change at last, and we wound up out of the level plain through mighty mountains of virgin granite. The tropical verdure of the lowlands was replaced by hardier vegetation, but even here the effects of constant heat and light were apparent in the immensity of the trees and the profusion of foliage and blooms. Crystal streams roared through their rocky channels, fed by the perpetual snows which we could see far above us. Above the snowcapped heights hung masses of heavy clouds. It was these, Perry explained, which evidently served the double purpose of replenishing the melting snows and protecting them from the direct rays of the sun.
By this time we had picked up a smattering of the bastard language in which our guards addressed us, as well as making good headway in the rather charming tongue of our co-captives. Directly ahead of me in the chain gang was a young woman. Three feet of chain linked us together in a forced companionship which I, at least, soon rejoiced in. For I found her a willing teacher, and from her I learned the language of her tribe, and much of the life and customs of the inner world﻿—at least that part of it with which she was familiar.
She told me that she was called Dian the Beautiful, and that she belonged to the tribe of Amoz, which dwells in the cliffs above the Darel Az, or shallow sea.
“How came you here?” I asked her.
===Chinese===
当看守们把我们从睡梦中唤醒时，我们已恢复了许多精神。他们给了我们食物——干肉条，却给我们注入了新的生命与力气，于是如今我们也能昂着头颅、迈着堂堂的步伐行军了。至少我是如此，因为我年轻气盛；可怜的佩里却最恨走路。在地球上，我常常见他为一个街区的路程就雇马车——如今这笔债可要偿还了，他两条老腿抖得厉害，我只得伸手揽住他，在余下那些可怕行军中半搀半抱地架着他走。
地势终于开始变化，我们蜿蜒爬出平坦的平原，穿过一座座原生花岗岩的巍峨大山。低地的热带草木换成了更为耐寒的植物，但即便在这里，持续炎热与光照的影响依然显见于树木的高大、枝叶花果的繁茂。晶莹的溪流在岩石河道里轰鸣，水源便是我们头顶远处望得见的终年积雪。雪峰之上悬着大团沉重的云。佩里解释说，正是这些云显然起着双重作用：补充消融的积雪，又使积雪免遭太阳光的直射。
这时我们已能听懂几句看守们同我们讲话的那种混杂语言，对同囚的俘虏们那颇为悦耳的语言也进步很快。锁链队列里，走在我正前方的是个年轻女子。三英尺长的锁链把我们连在一起，结成一种强制的伙伴关系——至少我很快就为之庆幸了。因为我发现她是个热心的小老师，我从她那里学到了她部族的语言，也学到许多内心世界的生活与风俗——至少是她所熟悉的那一部分。
她告诉我，她叫美人黛安（Dian the Beautiful），属于阿莫兹（Amoz）部落，那部落住在达雷尔阿兹（Darel Az）——也就是浅海——上方的悬崖上。
“你怎么会到这儿来的？”我问她。

===Original===
“I was running away from Jubal the Ugly One,” she answered, as though that was explanation quite sufficient.
“Who is Jubal the Ugly One?” I asked. “And why did you run away from him?”
She looked at me in surprise.
“Why *does* a woman run away from a man?” she answered my question with another.
“They do not, where I come from,” I replied. “Sometimes they run after them.”
But she could not understand. Nor could I get her to grasp the fact that I was of another world. She was quite as positive that creation was originated solely to produce her own kind and the world she lived in as are many of the outer world.
===Chinese===
“我是从丑八怪朱巴尔（Jubal the Ugly One）那里逃出来的。”她答道，仿佛这一句解释便绰绰有余了。
“丑八怪朱巴尔是谁？”我问，“你为什么要从他那里逃走？”
她诧异地看着我。
“女人为什么*要*从男人身边逃走呢？”她用另一个问题来回答我的问题。
“在我来的地方，女人不逃，”我答道，“有时候，她们倒是追男人。”
可她无法理解。我也没法让她明白我来自另一个世界。她深信，造物之所以被创造出来，就是为了产出她自己的族类和她所生活的这个世界——其笃信的程度，同外部世界的许多人一模一样。

===Original===
“But Jubal,” I insisted. “Tell me about him, and why you ran away to be chained by the neck and scourged across the face of a world.”
“Jubal the Ugly One placed his trophy before my father’s house. It was the head of a mighty *tandor*. It remained there and no greater trophy was placed beside it. So I knew that Jubal the Ugly One would come and take me as his mate. None other so powerful wished me, or they would have slain a mightier beast and thus have won me from Jubal. My father is not a mighty hunter. Once he was, but a *sadok* tossed him, and never again had he the full use of his right arm. My brother, Dacor the Strong One, had gone to the land of Sari to steal a mate for himself. Thus there was none, father, brother, or lover, to save me from Jubal the Ugly One, and I ran away and hid among the hills that skirt the land of Amoz. And there these Sagoths found me and made me captive.”
“What will they do with you?” I asked. “Where are they taking us?”
Again she looked her incredulity.
“I can almost believe that you are of another world,” she said, “for otherwise such ignorance were inexplicable. Do you really mean that you do not know that the Sagoths are the creatures of the Mahars﻿—the mighty Mahars who think they own Pellucidar and all that walks or grows upon its surface, or creeps or burrows beneath, or swims within its lakes and oceans, or flies through its air? Next you will be telling me that you never before heard of the Mahars!”
I was loath to do it, and further incur her scorn; but there was no alternative if I were to absorb knowledge, so I made a clean breast of my pitiful ignorance as to the mighty Mahars. She was shocked. But she did her very best to enlighten me, though much that she said was as Greek would have been to her. She described the Mahars largely by comparisons. In this way they were like unto *thipdars*, in that to the hairless *lidi*.
===Chinese===
“说说朱巴尔吧，”我坚持道，“告诉我他的事，还有你为什么宁可逃出来，被人用链子锁着脖子、鞭打着驱过一个世界。”
“丑八怪朱巴尔把他的战利品放在了我父亲的屋前。那是一头巨大的*坦多尔*（*tandor*）的头。它一直摆在那里，旁边不曾摆出过更大的战利品。于是我明白，丑八怪朱巴尔终归要来把我娶去作他的配偶。别的心慕我的人没有他那样强大；若有，他们就会宰一头更强大的野兽，从而把我从朱巴尔手里赢走。我父亲不是强大的猎手。他从前是，可一头*萨多克*（*sadok*）把他掀翻过，从此他的右臂就不听使唤了。我哥哥强者达科尔（Dacor the Strong One），到萨里的地界给自己抢配偶去了。就这样，父亲也好，哥哥也好，情人也好，没有一个能把我从丑八怪朱巴尔手里救出来，我便逃走，藏进了阿莫兹地界边上的群山里。就在那儿，这些萨戈思人发现了我，把我捉了来。”
“他们会拿你怎么处置？”我问，“他们要把我们带到哪里去？”
她又露出难以置信的神色。
“我几乎要相信你是另一个世界来的了，”她说，“不然这样的无知简直没法解释。你当真不知道萨戈思人是玛哈尔人的造物吗——那些强大的玛哈尔人，自以为佩卢西达尔归他们所有，连同地面上行走生长的一切、地底下爬行掘穴的一切、湖泊海洋里游动的一切、天空中飞翔的一切？再往下，你恐怕要告诉我，你从来没听说过玛哈尔人！”
我很不愿这样做，再招她的讥诮；可要想学到知识，别无他法，于是我把自己对强大的玛哈尔人那可怜的无知和盘托出。她大为震惊。但她尽了全力来开导我，尽管她说的许多话，对她而言不啻天书。她多半用打比方来描述玛哈尔人：说他们在某方面像*提普达*（*thipdar*），在另一方面则像无毛的*利迪*（*lidi*）。

===Original===
About all I gleaned of them was that they were quite hideous, had wings, and webbed feet; lived in cities built beneath the ground; could swim under water for great distances, and were very, very wise. The Sagoths were their weapons of offense and defense, and the races like herself were their hands and feet﻿—they were the slaves and servants who did all the manual labor. The Mahars were the heads﻿—the brains﻿—of the inner world. I longed to see this wondrous race of supermen.
Perry learned the language with me. When we halted, as we occasionally did, though sometimes the halts seemed ages apart, he would join in the conversation, as would Ghak the Hairy One, he who was chained just ahead of Dian the Beautiful. Ahead of Ghak was Hooja the Sly One. He too entered the conversation occasionally. Most of his remarks were directed toward Dian the Beautiful.
