It didn’t take half an eye to see that he had developed a bad case; but the girl appeared totally oblivious to his thinly veiled advances. Did I say thinly veiled? There is a race of men in New Zealand, or Australia, I have forgotten which, who indicate their preference for the lady of their affections by banging her over the head with a bludgeon. By comparison with this method Hooja’s lovemaking might be called thinly veiled. At first it caused me to blush violently although I have seen several Old Years out at Rectors, and in other less fashionable places off Broadway, and in Vienna, and Hamburg.
But the girl! She was magnificent. It was easy to see that she considered herself as entirely above and apart from her present surroundings and company. She talked with me, and with Perry, and with the taciturn Ghak because we were respectful; but she couldn’t even see Hooja the Sly One, much less hear him, and that made him furious. He tried to get one of the Sagoths to move the girl up ahead of him in the slave gang, but the fellow only poked him with his spear and told him that he had selected the girl for his own property﻿—that he would buy her from the Mahars as soon as they reached Phutra. Phutra, it seemed, was the city of our destination.
After passing over the first chain of mountains we skirted a salt sea, upon whose bosom swam countless horrid things. Seal-like creatures there were with long necks stretching ten and more feet above their enormous bodies and whose snake heads were split with gaping mouths bristling with countless fangs. There were huge tortoises too, paddling about among these other reptiles, which Perry said were Plesiosaurs of the Lias. I didn’t question his veracity﻿—they might have been most anything.
Dian told me they were *tandorazes*, or *tandors* of the sea, and that the other, and more fearsome reptiles, which occasionally rose from the deep to do battle with them, were *azdyryths*, or sea-*dyryths*﻿—Perry called them Ichthyosaurs. They resembled a whale with the head of an alligator.
I had forgotten what little geology I had studied at school﻿—about all that remained was an impression of horror that the illustrations of restored prehistoric monsters had made upon me, and a well-defined belief that any man with a pig’s shank and a vivid imagination could “restore” most any sort of paleolithic monster he saw fit, and take rank as a first class paleontologist. But when I saw these sleek, shiny carcasses shimmering in the sunlight as they emerged from the ocean, shaking their giant heads; when I saw the waters roll from their sinuous bodies in miniature waterfalls as they glided hither and thither, now upon the surface, now half submerged; as I saw them meet, open-mouthed, hissing and snorting, in their titanic and interminable warring I realized how futile is man’s poor, weak imagination by comparison with Nature’s incredible genius.
===Chinese===
关于他们，我打听到的全部情况就是：他们生得相当丑陋，有翅膀和蹼足；住在地下建造的城里；能在水下长距离游泳；而且非常、非常聪明。萨戈思人是他们攻防的武器，而像她这样的种族则是他们的手脚——是干一切体力活的奴隶和仆役。玛哈尔人是内心世界的头颅——是脑子。我渴望见一见这个了不起的超人种族。
佩里同我一起学语言。每逢队伍停下歇息——虽然间歇有时恍如隔世——他便会加入谈话，一起加入的还有多毛人格哈克（Ghak the Hairy One），他就锁在美人黛安前面；格哈克前面是狡猾鬼胡贾（Hooja the Sly One）。他偶尔也插进来说几句，多半是说给美人黛安听的。不用半只眼睛也看得出，他害了很重的相思病；可姑娘对他那层薄纱都谈不上的示好全然视若无睹。我说薄纱？新西兰还是澳大利亚——我忘了是哪处——有一个人种，看中了心上哪位女子，便用大棒敲她的头来表达心意。同这种方法一比，胡贾的求爱倒真算得上含蓄了。起初这害得我满脸通红，虽说我在雷克托饭店（Rectors）、在百老汇之外其他不那么时兴的地方、在维也纳和汉堡，都守过好几个除夕。
再说这姑娘！她真是气度非凡。一眼便看得出，她全然把自己当作高踞于眼下处境和同伴之上、置身事外的人。她同我谈，同佩里谈，也同沉默寡言的格哈克谈，因为我们敬重她；可狡猾鬼胡贾她连看都看不见，更别说听见了，这叫他勃然大怒。他想让一个萨戈思人把姑娘调到队伍里他前面去，那家伙却只用矛捅了他一下，说他已把姑娘挑中作了自己的私产——说一到富特拉，他就向玛哈尔人把她买下来。富特拉看来就是我们此行的目的地。
翻过第一道山脉之后，我们沿着一片盐海的岸边走，海面上游着数不清的可怕东西。有海豹似的生物，长长的脖子从庞大的身躯上伸出十几英尺，蛇一样的头颅裂开大口，口里密密排满无数獠牙。还有巨大的龟鳖，在这些别的爬虫中间划水游动——佩里说它们是里阿斯期的蛇颈龙。我没有质疑他的权威——它们几乎什么都能是。
黛安告诉我，它们叫*坦多拉泽斯*（*tandorazes*），就是海里的*坦多尔*；另外那些更可怕的、不时从深处浮上来同它们搏斗的爬虫，是*阿兹德里思斯*（*azdyryths*），也就是海*德里思*——佩里管它们叫鱼龙。它们像一头长着鳄鱼脑袋的鲸。
我把在学校里学的那点地质学忘光了——剩下的只有往日复原图上那些史前怪物给我留下的恐怖印象，以及一个清清楚楚的信念：随便哪个手里有块猪腿骨、想象力又够活跃的人，都能“复原”出他看中的几乎任何一种旧石器时代的怪物，从而跻身一流古生物学家的行列。可是，当我看见这些油光水滑的庞大躯体从海里冒出来、在阳光下闪闪发亮、摇着巨大的头颅；当我看见水从它们蜿蜒的身躯上滚落，宛如一道道小小的瀑布，它们四处游动，时而浮在水面，时而半沉半浮；当我看见它们张着大嘴相遇，嘶嘶吼吼，进行那巨人之间无休无止的战争——我才明白，同大自然不可思议的天才相比，人类贫乏软弱的想象力是多么徒劳。

===Original===
And Perry! He was absolutely flabbergasted. He said so himself.
“David,” he remarked, after we had marched for a long time beside that awful sea. “David, I used to teach geology, and I thought that I believed what I taught; but now I see that I did not believe it﻿—that it is impossible for man to believe such things as these unless he sees them with his own eyes. We take things for granted, perhaps, because we are told them over and over again, and have no way of disproving them﻿—like religions, for example; but we don’t believe them, we only think we do. If you ever get back to the outer world you will find that the geologists and paleontologists will be the first to set you down a liar, for they know that no such creatures as they restore ever existed. It is all right to *imagine* them as existing in an equally imaginary epoch﻿—but now? poof!”
At the next halt Hooja the Sly One managed to find enough slack chain to permit him to worm himself back quite close to Dian. We were all standing, and as he edged near the girl she turned her back upon him in such a truly earthly feminine manner that I could scarce repress a smile; but it was a short-lived smile for on the instant the Sly One’s hand fell upon the girl’s bare arm, jerking her roughly toward him.
I was not then familiar with the customs or social ethics which prevailed within Pellucidar; but even so I did not need the appealing look which the girl shot to me from her magnificent eyes to influence my subsequent act. What the Sly One’s intention was I paused not to inquire; but instead, before he could lay hold of her with his other hand, I placed a right to the point of his jaw that felled him in his tracks.
A roar of approval went up from those of the other prisoners and the Sagoths who had witnessed the brief drama; not, as I later learned, because I had championed the girl, but for the neat and, to them, astounding method by which I had bested Hooja.
And the girl? At first she looked at me with wide, wondering eyes, and then she dropped her head, her face half averted, and a delicate flush suffused her cheek. For a moment she stood thus in silence, and then her head went high, and she turned her back upon me as she had upon Hooja. Some of the prisoners laughed, and I saw the face of Ghak the Hairy One go very black as he looked at me searchingly. And what I could see of Dian’s cheek went suddenly from red to white.
===Chinese===
还有佩里！他简直是目瞪口呆。这是他自己说的。
“戴维，”我们沿着那片可怕的海走了许久之后，他说道，“戴维，我从前是教地质学的，我自以为相信自己所教的东西；可现在我明白，我并不相信——这类事情，人若非亲眼看见，是不可能相信的。我们把许多事当成理所当然，或许因为我们听人翻来覆去讲了无数遍，又无从反驳——比如宗教，就是个例子；可我们并不相信它们，只是自以为相信罢了。你若有一天回到地表，会发现地质学家和古生物学家头一个把你当成骗子，因为他们知道，他们复原出来的那些生物从来就不曾存在过。把它们*想象*成存在于一个同样想象出来的年代里，那没什么不行——可现在是亲眼看着？呸！”
下一次歇脚时，狡猾鬼胡贾总算从锁链里凑出足够的富余，把自己一点点蹭到离黛安很近的地方。我们都站着，他朝姑娘身边挨过去，她把脊背转向他——那副做派同地表的女人一模一样，害我差点憋不住笑；可这笑意转瞬即逝，因为刹那间狡猾鬼的手落到了姑娘裸露的手臂上，猛地把她朝自己这边一拽。
我当时还不熟悉佩卢西达尔的风俗和社会伦理；可即便如此，也用不着姑娘用她那双明丽的眼睛向我投来恳求的一瞥，来促成我接下来的举动。狡猾鬼意欲何为，我没停下来打听；相反，趁他另一只手还没抓稳她，我一记右拳正中他的下巴，把他当场撂倒在原地。
目睹了这出短短戏码的其他俘虏和萨戈思人齐声吼出一片喝彩；后来我才知道，倒不是因为我护了姑娘，而是因为我打倒胡贾的手段干净利落，令他们叹为观止。
那姑娘呢？起初她睁大眼睛，惊讶地望着我，随后垂下头，脸偏向一边，一抹红晕染上她的脸颊。她那样默默站了片刻，接着头一扬，像方才对胡贾那样，把脊背转向了我。有些俘虏笑了起来，我看见多毛人格哈克打量我时，脸色阴沉得吓人。而黛安那半边脸颊，也眼看着由红转白。

===Original===
Immediately after we resumed the march, and though I realized that in some way I had offended Dian the Beautiful I could not prevail upon her to talk with me that I might learn wherein I had erred﻿—in fact I might quite as well have been addressing a sphinx for all the attention I got. At last my own foolish pride stepped in and prevented my making any further attempts, and thus a companionship that without my realizing it had come to mean a great deal to me was cut off. Thereafter I confined my conversation to Perry. Hooja did not renew his advances toward the girl, nor did he again venture near me.
Again the weary and apparently interminable marching became a perfect nightmare of horrors to me. The more firmly fixed became the realization that the girl’s friendship had meant so much to me, the more I came to miss it; and the more impregnable the barrier of silly pride. But I was very young and would not ask Ghak for the explanation which I was sure he could give, and that might have made everything all right again.
On the march, or during halts, Dian refused consistently to notice me﻿—when her eyes wandered in my direction she looked either over my head or directly through me. At last I became desperate, and determined to swallow my self-esteem, and again beg her to tell me how I had offended, and how I might make reparation. I made up my mind that I should do this at the next halt. We were approaching another range of mountains at the time, and when we reached them, instead of winding across them through some high-flung pass we entered a mighty natural tunnel﻿—a series of labyrinthine grottoes, dark as Erebus.
The guards had no torches or light of any description. In fact we had seen no artificial light or sign of fire since we had entered Pellucidar. In a land of perpetual noon there is no need of light above ground, yet I marveled that they had no means of lighting their way through these dark, subterranean passages. So we crept along at a snail’s pace, with much stumbling and falling﻿—the guards keeping up a singsong chant ahead of us, interspersed with certain high notes which I found always indicated rough places and turns.
Halts were now more frequent, but I did not wish to speak to Dian until I could see from the expression of her face how she was receiving my apologies. At last a faint glow ahead forewarned us of the end of the tunnel, for which I for one was devoutly thankful. Then at a sudden turn we emerged into the full light of the noonday sun.
But with it came a sudden realization of what meant to me a real catastrophe﻿—Dian was gone, and with her a half-dozen other prisoners. The guards saw it too, and the ferocity of their rage was terrible to behold. Their awesome, bestial faces were contorted in the most diabolical expressions, as they accused each other of responsibility for the loss. Finally they fell upon us, beating us with their spear shafts, and hatchets. They had already killed two near the head of the line, and were like to have finished the balance of us when their leader finally put a stop to the brutal slaughter. Never in all my life had I witnessed a more horrible exhibition of bestial rage﻿—I thanked God that Dian had not been one of those left to endure it.
===Chinese===
队伍立刻重新开拔。我明白自己不知怎么得罪了美人黛安，却怎么也劝不动她同我说话，好让我知道自己错在哪里——事实上，我得到的理会之少，简直同对着斯芬克斯讲话差不多。最后，我自己那点愚蠢的自尊心占了上风，叫我不再做进一步的尝试，就这样，一段我未曾察觉已对我意义重大的情谊被掐断了。此后我便只同佩里说话。胡贾没有再向姑娘献殷勤，也不敢再靠近我。
那疲惫不堪、似乎永无尽头的行军，再次成了我一场噩梦般的恐怖。姑娘的情谊对我意义之大，认识得越清楚，我就越觉失落；那道愚蠢自尊筑成的壁垒，也就越发不可逾越。可我那时太年轻，不肯去问格哈克——我确信他能给我解释，而那本可以让一切重归于好。
行军也好，歇脚也好，黛安全然拒不理会我——她的眼睛即使朝我这个方向望来，也是越过我的头顶，或者径直穿透我这个人。我终于急了，决意咽下自尊，再次恳求她告诉我，我如何冒犯了她，我又怎样才能弥补。我打定主意，下一次歇脚就这么办。当时我们正逼近又一道山脉；到达之后，我们不是从什么高悬的垭口盘山而过，而是钻进了一条巨大的天然隧道——一连串迷宫似的岩洞，黑得如同厄瑞玻斯。
看守们没有火把，也没有任何可以照明的东西。事实上，自打进入佩卢西达尔，我们没见过一星人造的光或火的痕迹。在这永昼的地方，地面上用不着光，可他们竟没有任何照亮这些黑暗地下通道的办法，令我暗暗称奇。于是我们蜗牛似的往前蹭，跌跌撞撞，不断摔跤——看守们在前头领唱着一支单调的歌，中间夹着几个高音，我发现那总是预示着崎岖之处和拐弯。
歇脚越来越频繁，可我要等到能从黛安脸上的神情看出她如何接受我的道歉，才肯同她说话。终于，前方一点微光预示着隧道到了尽头，为此我打心底里感谢上苍。接着，一个急转弯之后，我们一头钻进了正午太阳的满目光亮里。
可随之而来的，是一个对我而言不啻真正灾祸的骤然醒悟——黛安不见了，同她一起不见的还有六七个俘虏。看守们也发现了，他们的狂怒凶残得骇人。那些可怖的兽脸上扭曲出最狰恶的表情，他们相互指责，推诿失人的罪责。最后他们扑到我们身上，用矛杆和斧头打我们。队伍前头已经有两个人被打死，若不是他们的头目终于叫停这场屠杀，余下的人也快没命了。我一生从未见过比这更可怕的兽性狂怒——我感谢上帝，黛安没有落在留下来承受这一切的人中间。

===Original===
Of the twelve prisoners who had been chained ahead of me each alternate one had been freed commencing with Dian. Hooja was gone. Ghak remained. What could it mean? How had it been accomplished? The commander of the guards was investigating. Soon he discovered that the rude locks which had held the neckbands in place had been deftly picked.
“Hooja the Sly One,” murmured Ghak, who was now next to me in line. “He has taken the girl that you would not have,” he continued, glancing at me.
“That I would not have!” I cried. “What do you mean?”
He looked at me closely for a moment.
“I have doubted your story that you are from another world,” he said at last, “but yet upon no other grounds could your ignorance of the ways of Pellucidar be explained. Do you really mean that you do not know that you offended the Beautiful One, and how?”
“I do not know, Ghak,” I replied.
===Chinese===
原先锁在我前面的十二名俘虏，从黛安起每隔一个就被人放走了一个。胡贾跑了。格哈克还在。这意味着什么？这是怎么得手的？警卫队长正在查。很快他发现，固定颈圈的粗陋锁具被人巧妙地撬开了。
“是狡猾鬼胡贾，”如今排在我旁边的格哈克喃喃道，“他把你不要的姑娘带走了。”他接着说，眼睛瞥了我一眼。
“我不要的！”我叫起来，“你这话什么意思？”
他紧盯着我看了片刻。
“我本来疑心你那套来自另一个世界的说法，”他终于开口，“可若不是这样，你对佩卢西达尔规矩的无知又作何解释。你当真不知道你冒犯了美人，也不知道是怎么冒犯的？”
“我不知道，格哈克。”我答道。

===Original===
“Then shall I tell you. When a man of Pellucidar intervenes between another man and the woman the other man would have, the woman belongs to the victor. Dian the Beautiful belongs to you. You should have claimed her or released her. Had you taken her hand, it would have indicated your desire to make her your mate, and had you raised her hand above her head and then dropped it, it would have meant that you did not wish her for a mate and that you released her from all obligation to you. By doing neither you have put upon her the greatest affront that a man may put upon a woman. Now she is your slave. No man will take her as mate, or may take her honorably, until he shall have overcome you in combat, and men do not choose slave women as their mates﻿—at least not the men of Pellucidar.”
“I did not know, Ghak,” I cried. “I did not know. Not for all Pellucidar would I have harmed Dian the Beautiful by word, or look, or act of mine. I do not want her as my slave. I do not want her as my﻿—” but here I stopped. The vision of that sweet and innocent face floated before me amidst the soft mists of imagination, and where I had on the second believed that I clung only to the memory of a gentle friendship I had lost, yet now it seemed that it would have been disloyalty to her to have said that I did not want Dian the Beautiful as my mate. I had not thought of her except as a welcome friend in a strange, cruel world. Even now I did not think that I loved her.
I believe Ghak must have read the truth more in my expression than in my words, for presently he laid his hand upon my shoulder.
“Man of another world,” he said, “I believe you. Lips may lie, but when the heart speaks through the eyes it tells only the truth. Your heart has spoken to me. I know now that you meant no affront to Dian the Beautiful. She is not of my tribe; but her mother is my sister. She does not know it﻿—her mother was stolen by Dian’s father who came with many others of the tribe of Amoz to battle with us for our women﻿—the most beautiful women of Pellucidar. Then was her father king of Amoz, and her mother was daughter of the king of Sari﻿—to whose power I, his son, have succeeded. Dian is the daughter of kings, though her father is no longer king since the *sadok* tossed him and Jubal the Ugly One wrested his kingship from him. Because of her lineage the wrong you did her was greatly magnified in the eyes of all who saw it. She will never forgive you.”
I asked Ghak if there was not some way in which I could release the girl from the bondage and ignominy I had unwittingly placed upon her.
“If ever you find her, yes,” he answered. “Merely to raise her hand above her head and drop it in the presence of others is sufficient to release her; but how may you ever find her, you who are doomed to a life of slavery yourself in the buried city of Phutra?”
===Chinese===
“那我说给你听。在佩卢西达尔，一个男人插到另一个男人与他想要的女人之间，那女人便属于胜者。美人黛安属于你。你本该认领她，或者放走她。你若握住她的手，便表明你想娶她为配偶；你若把她的手举过她的头顶然后放开，便意味着你不想娶她，把她对你的一切义务尽数解除。你两样都没做，便是把一个男人能给一个女人的最大羞辱加在了她身上。如今她是你的奴隶。没有男人会娶她做配偶，或者说没法光明正大地娶，除非他先在决斗中打败你——而男人们是不挑女奴做配偶的——至少佩卢西达尔的男人不。”
“我不知道啊，格哈克，”我叫道，“我不知道。哪怕拿整个佩卢西达尔来换，我也决不愿凭我的言语、眼神或举动伤害美人黛安。我不要她做我的奴隶。我不要她做我的——”话到这里，我停住了。那张甜美纯真的面容，在想象柔和的雾霭中浮现在我眼前；方才一瞬间，我还以为自己紧抓不放的只是一段失去的温柔情谊的记忆，可此刻我才明白，倘若说我不想娶美人黛安做配偶，那便是对她的不忠。我一直只把她当作这陌生残酷世界里一位受欢迎的朋友，从未有过别的想法。即便是现在，我也不认为我爱上了她。
我相信，格哈克与其说是从我的话语里、不如说是从我的神情里读出了真相，因为不多时他便把手搭在了我的肩上。
“异世界的人，”他说，“我相信你。嘴唇会说谎，可当心借着眼睛说话时，道出的只能是真话。你的心已经对我说了话。我现在明白，你无意冒犯美人黛安。她不是我族的人；可她母亲是我妹妹。她本人并不知道——当年她母亲被黛安的父亲掳走，那人带着阿莫兹部族的许多人为我们的女人来同我们开战——为了佩卢西达尔最美的女人们。那时她父亲是阿莫兹的王，她母亲是萨里王的女儿——他的权柄，如今由我这个儿子继承。黛安是王者的女儿，虽说自打那头*萨多克*把他掀翻、丑八怪朱巴尔从他手里夺走王位之后，她父亲已不再是王。因为她这样的血统，你冒犯她的这桩过错，在所有目击者眼里便被放大了许多倍。她永远不会原谅你。”
我问格哈克，有没有什么法子能把我无意间加在姑娘身上的奴役和耻辱解除。
“你若再找到她，有的，”他答道，“只需当着旁人的面，把她的手举过头顶再放开，便足以解救她；可你到哪儿再去找她呢？你自己还要在埋藏地下的富特拉城里当一辈子奴隶。”

===Original===
“Is there no escape?” I asked.
“Hooja the Sly One escaped and took the others with him,” replied Ghak. “But there are no more dark places on the way to Phutra, and once there it is not so easy﻿—the Mahars are very wise. Even if one escaped from Phutra there are the *thipdars*﻿—they would find you, and then﻿—” the Hairy One shuddered. “No, you will never escape the Mahars.”
It was a cheerful prospect. I asked Perry what he thought about it; but he only shrugged his shoulders and continued a longwinded prayer he had been at for some time. He was wont to say that the only redeeming feature of our captivity was the ample time it gave him for the improvisation of prayers﻿—it was becoming an obsession with him. The Sagoths had begun to take notice of his habit of declaiming throughout entire marches. One of them asked him what he was saying﻿—to whom he was talking. The question gave me an idea, so I answered quickly before Perry could say anything.
“Do not interrupt him,” I said. “He is a very holy man in the world from which we come. He is speaking to spirits which you cannot see﻿—do not interrupt him or they will spring out of the air upon you and rend you limb from limb﻿—like that,” and I jumped toward the great brute with a loud “Boo!” that sent him stumbling backward.
I took a long chance, I realized, but if we could make any capital out of Perry’s harmless mania I wanted to make it while the making was prime. It worked splendidly. The Sagoths treated us both with marked respect during the balance of the journey, and then passed the word along to their masters, the Mahars.
Two marches after this episode we came to the city of Phutra. The entrance to it was marked by two lofty towers of granite, which guarded a flight of steps leading to the buried city. Sagoths were on guard here as well as at a hundred or more other towers scattered about over a large plain.
===Chinese===
“没有逃跑的路子吗？”我问。
“狡猾鬼胡贾逃了，还带走了其余的人，”格哈克答道，“可去富特拉的路上再没有黑暗的地方了，而一旦到了那儿，就绝不容易——玛哈尔人非常精明。就算有人从富特拉逃出去，还有*提普达*——它们会找到你，那时候——”多毛人打了个寒噤。“不，你们永远逃不出玛哈尔人的手心。”
前景真叫人振奋。我问佩里有何高见；他只耸耸肩，接着做他那篇已经做了半天的冗长祷告。他常说，我们被俘这桩事唯一可取之处，就是给他留出了充裕的时间来即兴创作祷文——这快成了他的执念。萨戈思人已开始留意他整段行军都念念有词的习惯。有一个萨戈思人问他嘴里念的是什么——在同谁说话。这个问题给了我一个主意，我便抢在佩里开口之前飞快地答道：
“别打岔。他是我们来的那个世界里一位非常神圣的人。他在同你们看不见的神灵说话——别打搅他，不然神灵会从空中扑到你们身上，把你们撕成碎块——像这样，”说着我朝那大块头猛地扑过去，大喝一声“呸！”，吓得他踉跄倒退。
我知道这是冒了天大的险，可要是能从佩里这无害的痴念里捞一点资本，我只想趁热打铁。效果妙极了。余下的路程里，萨戈思人对我们两个都敬重有加，随后又把话传给了他们的主人——玛哈尔人。
这件事过后又行军两程，我们来到了富特拉城。城的入口以两座高耸的花岗岩塔为标志，塔间守卫着一段通向这座地下之城的台阶。这里同散布在大平原上一百多座其他塔楼一样，都有萨戈思人把守。

## XI Four Dead Mahars / 四个死去的玛哈尔人

===Original===
A moment later I was standing before a dozen Mahars﻿—the social investigators of Phutra. They asked me many questions, through a Sagoth interpreter. I answered them all truthfully. They seemed particularly interested in my account of the outer earth and the strange vehicle which had brought Perry and me to Pellucidar. I thought that I had convinced them, and after they had sat in silence for a long time following my examination, I expected to be ordered returned to my quarters.
During this apparent silence they were debating through the medium of strange, unspoken language the merits of my tale. At last the head of the tribunal communicated the result of their conference to the officer in charge of the Sagoth guard.
“Come,” he said to me, “you are sentenced to the experimental pits for having dared to insult the intelligence of the mighty ones with the ridiculous tale you have had the temerity to unfold to them.”
“Do you mean that they do not believe me?” I asked, totally astonished.

===Chinese===
片刻之后，我已站在十几只玛哈尔人（Mahars）面前——富特拉（Phutra）的社会调查官们。它们通过一个萨戈思人（Sagoth）通译，问了我许多问题。我一一如实作答。它们对我讲述的外层地球，以及把佩里（Perry）和我带到佩卢西达尔（Pellucidar）的那台奇怪载具，似乎格外感兴趣。我原以为已经说服了它们；它们在我受审之后又静坐了很久，随后我等着被押回住处去。
在这表面的沉默中，它们正用那种古怪的无声语言，辩论我这番话的价值。最后，法庭的主审把合议的结果，传达给了统领萨戈思卫兵的军官。
「来吧，」他对我说，「你被判投入实验坑，因为你竟敢用你有胆量向强者们编造的荒唐故事，侮辱他们的智力。」
「你是说，他们不相信我？」我全然愕然地问。

===Original===
“Believe you!” he laughed. “Do you mean to say that you expected anyone to believe so impossible a lie?”
It was hopeless, and so I walked in silence beside my guard down through the dark corridors and runways toward my awful doom. At a low level we came upon a number of lighted chambers in which we saw many Mahars engaged in various occupations. To one of these chambers my guard escorted me, and before leaving they chained me to a side wall. There were other humans similarly chained. Upon a long table lay a victim even as I was ushered into the room. Several Mahars stood about the poor creature holding him down so that he could not move. Another, grasping a sharp knife with her three-toed fore foot, was laying open the victim’s chest and abdomen. No anesthetic had been administered and the shrieks and groans of the tortured man were terrible to hear. This, indeed, was vivisection with a vengeance. Cold sweat broke out upon me as I realized that soon my turn would come. And to think that where there was no such thing as time I might easily imagine that my suffering was enduring for months before death finally released me!
The Mahars had paid not the slightest attention to me as I had been brought into the room. So deeply immersed were they in their work that I am sure they did not even know that the Sagoths had entered with me. The door was close by. Would that I could reach it! But those heavy chains precluded any such possibility. I looked about for some means of escape from my bonds. Upon the floor between me and the Mahars lay a tiny surgical instrument which one of them must have dropped. It looked not unlike a buttonhook, but was much smaller, and its point was sharpened. A hundred times in my boyhood days had I picked locks with a buttonhook. Could I but reach that little bit of polished steel I might yet effect at least a temporary escape.

===Chinese===
「相信你！」他笑道，「你是想说，你还指望有谁会相信这么一个不可能的谎言？」
事情已无望，于是我在卫兵身旁默默走着，穿过黑暗的走廊和甬道，走向我可怕的厄运。在较低的一层，我们经过许多亮着灯的房间，里面有许多玛哈尔人在各行其事。卫兵把我押进其中一间，离开前把我锁到了侧墙上。还有别的人类也被同样锁着。我被带进房间时，一张长长的台上正躺着一个受难者。几只玛哈尔人围着那可怜的家伙，把他按得动弹不得。另一只用三趾的前脚攥着一把利刃，正剖开受难者的胸膛和腹腔。没有施任何麻醉，那受刑人的惨叫和呻吟，听得人毛骨悚然。这可真是登峰造极的活体解剖。我一想到转眼就要轮到自己，冷汗便冒了出来。再想想，在一个没有时间这回事的地方，我很容易想象，我的苦刑要熬上好几个月，死亡才终于放我解脱！
我被带进房间时，玛哈尔人对我不屑一顾。它们埋头干活干得太深，我敢说它们甚至不知道萨戈思人是同我一起进来的。门就在近旁。但愿我能够到它！可那些沉重的锁链断了这种可能。我四下寻找脱身之具。在我和玛哈尔人之间的地板上，躺着一件小小的外科器械，准是哪只掉落的。它看上去很像个纽扣钩，但要小得多，尖端磨得很锋利。少年时代，我用纽扣钩捅开过上百把锁。只要够得着那一小片擦亮的钢，我兴许至少能暂且脱身。

===Original===
Crawling to the limit of my chain, I found that by reaching one hand as far out as I could my fingers still fell an inch short of the coveted instrument. It was tantalizing! Stretch every fiber of my being as I would, I could not quite make it.
At last I turned about and extended one foot toward the object. My heart came to my throat! I could just touch the thing! But suppose that in my effort to drag it toward me I should accidentally shove it still farther away and thus entirely out of reach! Cold sweat broke out upon me from every pore. Slowly and cautiously I made the effort. My toes dropped upon the cold metal. Gradually I worked it toward me until I felt that it was within reach of my hand and a moment later I had turned about and the precious thing was in my grasp.
Assiduously I fell to work upon the Mahar lock that held my chain. It was pitifully simple. A child might have picked it, and a moment later I was free. The Mahars were now evidently completing their work at the table. One already turned away and was examining other victims, evidently with the intention of selecting the next subject.

===Chinese===
我爬到锁链的极限，发现一只手伸到最长，指尖离那件心痒的器械还差一英寸。真急死人！任凭我把全身每一根筋都抻直，就是差那么一点。
最后我转过身，朝那东西伸出一只脚。我的心提到了嗓子眼！我刚好能碰到它！可要是往回扒的时候一失手，把它推得更远、彻底够不着，那可怎么办！每个毛孔都渗出了冷汗。我慢慢吞吞、小心翼翼地用劲。脚趾落上冰凉的金属。我一点一点把它扒近，直到觉得它已进入手的范围，片刻之后我转过身，那宝贝已攥在掌心。
我勤勤恳恳地对付起锁住我锁链的玛哈尔锁。它简单得可怜。小孩子也捅得开，不多时我便自由了。玛哈尔人这时显然快在台边完活了。一只已经转过身去，查看别的受难者，显然是在挑选下一个对象。

===Original===
Those at the table had their backs toward me. But for the creature walking toward us I might have escaped that moment. Slowly the thing approached me, when its attention was attracted by a huge slave chained a few yards to my right. Here the reptile stopped and commenced to go over the poor devil carefully, and as it did so its back turned toward me for an instant, and in that instant I gave two mighty leaps that carried me out of the chamber into the corridor beyond, down which I raced with all the speed I could command.
Where I was, or whither I was going, I knew not. My only thought was to place as much distance as possible between me and that frightful chamber of torture.
Presently I reduced my speed to a brisk walk, and later realizing the danger of running into some new predicament, were I not careful, I moved still more slowly and cautiously. After a time I came to a passage that seemed in some mysterious way familiar to me, and presently, chancing to glance within a chamber which led from the corridor I saw three Mahars curled up in slumber upon a bed of skins. I could have shouted aloud in joy and relief. It was the same corridor and the same Mahars that I had intended to have lead so important a role in our escape from Phutra. Providence had indeed been kind to me, for the reptiles still slept.

===Chinese===
台边那几只都背对着我。若不是那只朝我们走来的家伙，我那一刻就逃掉了。那东西慢慢向我走近，注意力却被锁在我右边几码开外的一个高大奴隶吸引。爬虫在那里停下，动手仔细查看那可怜鬼，这一查，它的背朝我转过来一瞬，就在这一瞬，我猛力连跃两步，蹿出房间进了外面的走廊，撒开腿能跑多快跑多快。
我在哪里，要往哪里去，我一概不知。唯一的念头，就是在我和那间骇人的酷刑室之间，隔开尽可能远的距离。
不久我把速度放慢成疾走，再后来意识到，一个不小心就可能撞进新的险境，我便走得愈发缓慢谨慎。过了一阵，我来到一条似乎莫名眼熟的通道，不久，恰巧往走廊旁的一间屋里瞟了一眼，只见三只玛哈尔人蜷在一铺兽皮上酣睡。我险些喜极放声欢呼。正是那条走廊，正是那三只玛哈尔——我本打算让它们在我们逃出富特拉的行动里担纲重任。天意着实眷顾我，因为这几只爬虫还在睡。

===Original===
My one great danger now lay in returning to the upper levels in search of Perry and Ghak, but there was nothing else to be done, and so I hastened upward. When I came to the frequented portions of the building, I found a large burden of skins in a corner and these I lifted to my head, carrying them in such a way that ends and corners fell down about my shoulders completely hiding my face. Thus disguised I found Perry and Ghak together in the chamber where we had been wont to eat and sleep.
Both were glad to see me, it was needless to say, though of course they had known nothing of the fate that had been meted out to me by my judges. It was decided that no time should now be lost before attempting to put our plan of escape to the test, as I could not hope to remain hidden from the Sagoths long, nor could I forever carry that bale of skins about upon my head without arousing suspicion. However it seemed likely that it would carry me once more safely through the crowded passages and chambers of the upper levels, and so I set out with Perry and Ghak﻿—the stench of the illy cured pelts fairly choking me.
Together we repaired to the first tier of corridors beneath the main floor of the buildings, and here Perry and Ghak halted to await me. The buildings are cut out of the solid limestone formation. There is nothing at all remarkable about their architecture. The rooms are sometimes rectangular, sometimes circular, and again oval in shape. The corridors which connect them are narrow and not always straight. The chambers are lighted by diffused sunlight reflected through tubes similar to those by which the avenues are lighted. The lower the tiers of chambers, the darker. Most of the corridors are entirely unlighted. The Mahars can see quite well in semidarkness.

===Chinese===
眼下我唯一的巨大风险，是回到上层去找佩里和格哈克（Ghak），可又别无他法，于是我便匆匆向上走。走到建筑中人来人往的地段，我在墙角发现一大摞兽皮，便把它们顶到头上，让皮子的边边角角垂在肩头四周，把脸完全遮住。我就这样化了装，找到佩里和格哈克，就在我们惯常吃喝睡觉的那间屋里。
两人见了我自然高兴，不必多说，当然，法官们判给我的下场，他们一无所知。我们商定，立刻着手检验我们的越狱计划，一刻也不耽搁，因为我在萨戈思人眼皮底下藏不了多久，那捆兽皮也不可能永远顶在头上而不惹人起疑。不过看来，它多半还能再护送我平安穿过上层的拥挤通道一次，于是我便同佩里和格哈克一道出发——那鞣制不良的皮子的恶臭，简直要把我呛死。
我们一同下到主层下面第一层走廊，佩里和格哈克停在这里等我。这些建筑开凿在整层石灰岩里。其建筑式样毫无出奇之处。房间时而长方，时而正圆，时而又呈椭圆。连接它们的走廊狭窄，又并非总是笔直。房间靠漫射的阳光照明，光经由管道反射进来，与大道的照明同理。房间的层级越低越暗。大多数走廊完全没有照明。玛哈尔人在半明半暗里看东西相当自如。

===Original===
Down to the main floor we encountered many Mahars, Sagoths, and slaves; but no attention was paid to us as we had become a part of the domestic life of the building. There was but a single entrance leading from the place into the avenue and this was well guarded by Sagoths﻿—this doorway alone were we forbidden to pass. It is true that we were not supposed to enter the deeper corridors and apartments except on special occasions when we were instructed to do so; but as we were considered a lower order without intelligence there was little reason to fear that we could accomplish any harm by so doing, and so we were not hindered as we entered the corridor which led below.
Wrapped in a skin I carried three swords, and the two bows, and the arrows which Perry and I had fashioned. As many slaves bore skin-wrapped burdens to and fro my load attracted no comment. Where I left Ghak and Perry there were no other creatures in sight, and so I withdrew one sword from the package, and leaving the balance of the weapons with Perry, started on alone toward the lower levels.

===Chinese===
下到主层的一路，我们遇到许多玛哈尔人、萨戈思人和奴隶；但没人理会我们，因为我们已成了这栋建筑日常生活的一部分。从这里通往大道的入口只有一个，由萨戈思人把守森严——唯独这扇门，我们被禁止通行。诚然，我们不该进入更深处的走廊和房间，除非有特殊差遣；但我们既被视作没有智能的下等货，也犯不上担心我们这样能干出什么祸事，于是当我们走进通往下面的走廊时，无人阻拦。
我用一张兽皮裹了三把剑、两张弓，还有佩里和我制作的箭。奴隶们扛着皮子包裹的来来去去多得是，我这一担没有惹起任何议论。我撇下格哈克和佩里的地方，视野里没有别的生物，于是我从包裹里抽出一把剑，把其余兵刃留给佩里，独自一人朝下层走去。

===Original===
Having come to the apartment in which the three Mahars slept I entered silently on tiptoe, forgetting that the creatures were without the sense of hearing. With a quick thrust through the heart I disposed of the first but my second thrust was not so fortunate, so that before I could kill the next of my victims it had hurled itself against the third, who sprang quickly up, facing me with wide-distended jaws. But fighting is not the occupation which the race of Mahars loves, and when the thing saw that I already had dispatched two of its companions, and that my sword was red with their blood, it made a dash to escape me. But I was too quick for it, and so, half hopping, half flying, it scurried down another corridor with me close upon its heels.
Its escape meant the utter ruin of our plan, and in all probability my instant death. This thought lent wings to my feet; but even at my best I could do no more than hold my own with the leaping thing before me.
Of a sudden it turned into an apartment on the right of the corridor, and an instant later as I rushed in I found myself facing two of the Mahars. The one who had been there when we entered had been occupied with a number of metal vessels, into which had been put powders and liquids as I judged from the array of flasks standing about upon the bench where it had been working. In an instant I realized what I had stumbled upon. It was the very room for the finding of which Perry had given me minute directions. It was the buried chamber in which was hidden the Great Secret of the race of Mahars. And on the bench beside the flasks lay the skin-bound book which held the only copy of the thing I was to have sought, after dispatching the three Mahars in their sleep.

===Chinese===
来到那三只玛哈尔人睡觉的房间，我踮着脚悄声进去——竟忘了这些生物没有听觉。一剑迅捷穿心，我结果了第一只；可第二剑不那么走运，没等杀掉下一个祭品，它已扑到第三只身上，那第三只一跃而起，张着大口冲我而来。但搏斗并非玛哈尔人这个种族喜爱的营生，那东西见我已结果了它的两个同伴，我的剑还染着它们的血，便一头猛冲想躲开我。可我比它更快，于是它半跳半飞，窜进另一条走廊，我紧追在它的脚后跟。
它逃脱，便意味着我们的计划彻底完蛋，而且十有八九我当场就得死。这个念头给我脚底添了翅膀；但纵然使出全力，我至多同眼前这跳跃的东西打个平手。
它突然拐进走廊右边的一个房间，片刻后我冲进去，发现自己正面对两只玛哈尔人。我们进屋时原在房里的那一只，正忙着一批金属器皿，从台子上摆着的一排烧瓶判断，里面已放入了粉末和液体。一瞬间我明白了撞见的是什么。这正是佩里给我详细指点、要我找到的那个房间。正是那个埋藏的密室，里面藏着玛哈尔人种族的大秘密。而在台子上烧瓶旁边，摊着那本皮面书，装着我本该在睡梦中结果那三只玛哈尔人之后前来寻取的东西的唯一抄本。

===Original===
There was no exit from the room other than the doorway in which I now stood facing the two frightful reptiles. Cornered, I knew that they would fight like demons, and they were well equipped to fight if fight they must. Together they launched themselves upon me, and though I ran one of them through the heart on the instant, the other fastened its gleaming fangs about my sword arm above the elbow, and then with her sharp talons commenced to rake me about the body, evidently intent upon disemboweling me. I saw that it was useless to hope that I might release my arm from that powerful, viselike grip which seemed to be severing my arm from my body. The pain I suffered was intense, but it only served to spur me to greater efforts to overcome my antagonist.
Back and forth across the floor we struggled﻿—the Mahar dealing me terrific, cutting blows with her fore feet, while I attempted to protect my body with my left hand, at the same time watching for an opportunity to transfer my blade from my now useless sword hand to its rapidly weakening mate. At last I was successful, and with what seemed to me my last ounce of strength I ran the blade through the ugly body of my foe.
Soundless, as it had fought, it died, and though weak from pain and loss of blood, it was with an emotion of triumphant pride that I stepped across its convulsively stiffening corpse to snatch up the most potent secret of a world. A single glance assured me it was the very thing that Perry had described to me.

===Chinese===
房间除我此刻站着的这扇门，再无别的出口，我正面对着这两只可怖的爬虫。被逼到角落，我知道它们会像恶魔一样搏斗，而一旦非斗不可，它们也装备齐全。它们一齐朝我扑来，虽然我当即一剑洞穿其中一只的心脏，另一只却用它亮晃晃的獠牙咬住了我持剑臂的肘上，又用尖利的钩爪开始朝我身上乱抓，分明是想把我开膛破肚。我明白，想从那铁钳般的死握里抽出手臂，是无望的——那力量像要把我的胳膊从身体上撕下来。我痛得钻心，可这痛只鞭策我使出更大的劲，去制服我的对手。
我们在地板上翻来滚去地缠斗——那玛哈尔人用前脚给我一下下可怕的开裂重击，我则用左手护住身体，同时瞅机会把剑从已经不中用的持剑手，换到那只迅速乏力的手上。我终于得手，用自觉是最后一丝的力气，把剑捅穿了我仇敌那丑陋的躯体。
它像搏斗时一样无声无息地死了。我虽然又痛又失血、浑身发虚，却怀着一种得胜的自豪，跨过它抽搐僵直的尸身，一把抓起一个世界最强大的秘密。只一眼我便确信，这正是佩里向我描述过的那样东西。

===Original===
And as I grasped it did I think of what it meant to the human race of Pellucidar﻿—did there flash through my mind the thought that countless generations of my own kind yet unborn would have reason to worship me for the thing that I had accomplished for them? I did not. I thought of a beautiful oval face, gazing out of limpid eyes, through a waving mass of jet-black hair. I thought of red, red lips, God-made for kissing. And of a sudden, apropos of nothing, standing there alone in the secret chamber of the Mahars of Pellucidar, I realized that I loved Dian the Beautiful.

===Chinese===
我抓住它的时候，可曾想到它对佩卢西达尔的人类意味着什么——可曾有念头闪过脑海：我族中尚未出世的千千万万代后人，会为我为他们成就的这件事，有理由把我奉若神明？我没有。我想起的是一张美丽的鹅蛋脸，从一双清澈的眼睛里向外凝望，透过一头乌黑波浪般的长发。我想起那红艳艳的双唇，上帝造它们就是为着亲吻的。突然之间，无端无由地，独自站在佩卢西达尔玛哈尔人的密室里，我意识到，我爱上了美人黛安（Dian the Beautiful）。

## VII Freedom / 自由

===Original===
Once out of the direct path of the animal, fear of it left me, but another emotion as quickly gripped me﻿—hope of escape that the demoralized condition of the guards made possible for the instant.
I thought of Perry, and but for the hope that I might better encompass his release if myself free I should have put the thought of freedom from me at once. As it was I hastened on toward the right searching for an exit toward which no Sagoths were fleeing, and at last I found it﻿—a low, narrow aperture leading into a dark corridor.
Without thought of the possible consequence, I darted into the shadows of the tunnel, feeling my way along through the gloom for some distance. The noises of the amphitheater had grown fainter and fainter until now all was as silent as the tomb about me. Faint light filtered from above through occasional ventilating and lighting tubes, but it was scarce sufficient to enable my human eyes to cope with the darkness, and so I was forced to move with extreme care, feeling my way along step by step with a hand upon the wall beside me.
===Chinese===
一脱离那畜生直冲的路线，我对它的恐惧便消失了；但另一种情绪随即攫住了我——趁看守们军心涣散的这一瞬逃跑的希望。
我想到了佩里；若不是指望自己脱身后更能设法救他，我早把自由的念头撇开了。于是我只管朝右边赶，寻一个没有萨戈思人奔逃的出口，终于找到了——一道低矮狭窄的孔洞，通进一条黑暗的走廊。
我不假思索后果，一头扎进隧道的暗影里，在昏黑中摸索着走了一程。竞技场的喧声愈来愈弱，此刻四下静得像坟墓。微光从头顶偶尔的通气管和采光管滤下来，却不够我这双人类的眼睛对付黑暗，我只得格外小心地挪动，一手扶着身旁的墙，一步一步探着走。

===Original===
Presently the light increased and a moment later, to my delight, I came upon a flight of steps leading upward, at the top of which the brilliant light of the noonday sun shone through an opening in the ground.
Cautiously I crept up the stairway to the tunnel’s end, and peering out saw the broad plain of Phutra before me. The numerous lofty, granite towers which mark the several entrances to the subterranean city were all in front of me﻿—behind, the plain stretched level and unbroken to the nearby foothills. I had come to the surface, then, beyond the city, and my chances for escape seemed much enhanced.
My first impulse was to await darkness before attempting to cross the plain, so deeply implanted are habits of thought; but of a sudden I recollected the perpetual noonday brilliance which envelops Pellucidar, and with a smile I stepped forth into the daylight.
===Chinese===
不久光线亮了起来，片刻之后，令我欣喜的是，我遇到一道向上的石阶，阶顶上正午灿烂的阳光从地面的一个洞口照进来。
我小心翼翼爬上台阶，到隧道尽头探头一望，富特拉的辽阔平原展现在我面前。标志着地下之城各处入口的那些高耸的花岗岩塔，全在我前方——身后，平原平坦无阻，一直铺到近处的山麓。这么说，我已出到城外地面，逃脱的机会似乎大增。
我的头一个冲动是等天黑再设法穿越平原——思维的习惯就是这样根深蒂固；可我突然想起笼罩佩卢西达尔的是永不消逝的正午光辉，便微笑着迈进了白昼。

===Original===
Rank grass, waist high, grows upon the plain of Phutra﻿—the gorgeous flowering grass of the inner world, each particular blade of which is tipped with a tiny, five-pointed blossom﻿—brilliant little stars of varying colors that twinkle in the green foliage to add still another charm to the weird, yet lovely, landscape.
But then the only aspect which attracted me was the distant hills in which I hoped to find sanctuary, and so I hastened on, trampling the myriad beauties beneath my hurrying feet. Perry says that the force of gravity is less upon the surface of the inner world than upon that of the outer. He explained it all to me once, but I was never particularly brilliant in such matters and so most of it has escaped me. As I recall it the difference is due in some part to the counterattraction of that portion of the earth’s crust directly opposite the spot upon the face of Pellucidar at which one’s calculations are being made. Be that as it may, it always seemed to me that I moved with greater speed and agility within Pellucidar than upon the outer surface﻿—there was a certain airy lightness of step that was most pleasing, and a feeling of bodily detachment which I can only compare with that occasionally experienced in dreams.
And as I crossed Phutra’s flower-bespangled plain that time I seemed almost to fly, though how much of the sensation was due to Perry’s suggestion and how much to actuality I am sure I do not know. The more I thought of Perry the less pleasure I took in my newfound freedom. There could be no liberty for me within Pellucidar unless the old man shared it with me, and only the hope that I might find some way to encompass his release kept me from turning back to Phutra.
===Chinese===
齐腰高的茂草长满富特拉平原——那是内层世界绚丽的开花草，每一片叶尖都顶着一朵小小的五瓣花——五色缤纷的小星星在绿丛中闪烁，给这片诡异而又可爱的景色更添一分妩媚。
但那时唯一吸引我的，只是我希望于其中寻得庇护的远山；于是我只管赶路，把万般美景都踩在匆匆的双脚下。佩里说，内层世界表面的重力比外层的小。他有一回给我解释过一整套，可惜我在这些事情上向来不聪明，多半都忘了。依稀记得，这差异部分来自地壳上与佩卢西达尔计算点正相对的那一部分的反向引力。不管怎样，我在佩卢西达尔行动总比在外层表面更快更灵——脚步有一种说不出的轻飘，煞是惬意；身体有一种超然之感，我只能拿梦中偶有的体验来相比。
那一回穿过富特拉繁花铺缀的平原时，我简直像在飞，尽管这感觉几分来自佩里的说法、几分来自实情，我自己也说不清。越是想念佩里，我这新得的自由便越乏味。佩卢西达尔之内，老人不同享，便没有我的自由；唯有盼望能寻出办法救他脱身，才使我压住折回富特拉的念头。

===Original===
Just how I was to help Perry I could scarce imagine, but I hoped that some fortuitous circumstance might solve the problem for me. It was quite evident however that little less than a miracle could aid me, for what could I accomplish in this strange world, naked and unarmed? It was even doubtful that I could retrace my steps to Phutra should I once pass beyond view of the plain, and even were that possible, what aid could I bring to Perry no matter how far I wandered?
The case looked more and more hopeless the longer I viewed it, yet with a stubborn persistency I forged ahead toward the foothills. Behind me no sign of pursuit developed, before me I saw no living thing. It was as though I moved through a dead and forgotten world.
I have no idea, of course, how long it took me to reach the limit of the plain, but at last I entered the foothills, following a pretty little canyon upward toward the mountains. Beside me frolicked a laughing brooklet, hurrying upon its noisy way down to the silent sea. In its quieter pools I discovered many small fish, of four- or five-pound weight I should imagine. In appearance, except as to size and color, they were not unlike the whale of our own seas. As I watched them playing about I discovered, not only that they suckled their young, but that at intervals they rose to the surface to breathe as well as to feed upon certain grasses and a strange, scarlet lichen which grew upon the rocks just above the water line.
===Chinese===
我究竟怎样帮佩里，简直无从想象，只盼某个偶然的机缘替我解决这道难题。然而很显然，非有近于奇迹的机缘不能助我：在这陌生世界里，赤身裸体、手无寸铁，我能做成什么？一旦走出能望见平原的范围，我能否循原路回富特拉都成问题；就算回得去，任我走得再远，又能给佩里带去什么帮助？
这局面越想越无望，我却执拗地一味朝山麓赶去。身后没有追兵的影子，眼前不见一个活物，仿佛我在一个死寂而被遗忘的世界里行走。
走了多久才到平原尽头，我自然说不上来；但最后我终于进了山麓，沿一条秀美的小峡谷向山上去。身边一条欢笑的小溪嬉闹着，喧哗着赶它喧哗的路，流向沉默的海。在它平静的水潭里我发现许多小鱼，估摸每条有四五磅重。论模样，除开大小和颜色，同我们海里的鲸相去不远。看它们嬉游时我发现，它们不但哺乳幼崽，还不时浮上水面呼吸，也啃食某些水草和一种长在略高出水线的岩石上的猩红色地衣。

===Original===
It was this last habit that gave me the opportunity I craved to capture one of these herbivorous cetaceans﻿—that is what Perry calls them﻿—and make as good a meal as one can on raw, warm-blooded fish; but I had become rather used, by this time, to the eating of food in its natural state, though I still balked on the eyes and entrails, much to the amusement of Ghak, to whom I always passed these delicacies.
Crouching beside the brook, I waited until one of the diminutive purple whales rose to nibble at the long grasses which overhung the water, and then, like the beast of prey that man really is, I sprang upon my victim, appeasing my hunger while he yet wriggled to escape.
Then I drank from the clear pool, and after washing my hands and face continued my flight. Above the source of the brook I encountered a rugged climb to the summit of a long ridge. Beyond was a steep declivity to the shore of a placid, inland sea, upon the quiet surface of which lay several beautiful islands.
===Chinese===
正是这后一种习性，给了我渴望的机会，逮住一头这类食草的鲸类——佩里这么叫它们——饱餐了一顿生的温血鱼，算是能吃到的美餐了；到这时我已相当习惯吃原模原样的食物了，虽然眼睛和内脏我仍旧下不去口，多半递给格哈克，惹他好一阵取笑。
我蹲在小溪旁，等一头小小的紫色鲸鱼浮上来啃悬在水上的长草，随后，像人这头真正的野兽那样，猛扑上我的猎物，趁它还在挣扎，填饱了我的肚子。
然后我从清澈的水潭里喝了水，洗净手脸，继续奔逃。在小溪源头之上，我攀上一段崎岖的路，登上一道长长的山脊。山脊那边是一道陡坡，直落到一片平静的内陆海之滨，安静的海面上卧着几座美丽的岛。

===Original===
The view was charming in the extreme, and as no man or beast was to be seen that might threaten my newfound liberty, I slid over the edge of the bluff, and half sliding, half falling, dropped into the delightful valley, the very aspect of which seemed to offer a haven of peace and security.
The gently sloping beach along which I walked was thickly strewn with strangely shaped, colored shells; some empty, others still housing as varied a multitude of mollusks as ever might have drawn out their sluggish lives along the silent shores of the antediluvian seas of the outer crust. As I walked I could not but compare myself with the first man of that other world, so complete the solitude which surrounded me, so primal and untouched the virgin wonders and beauties of adolescent nature. I felt myself a second Adam wending my lonely way through the childhood of a world, searching for my Eve, and at the thought there rose before my mind’s eye the exquisite outlines of a perfect face surmounted by a loose pile of wondrous, raven hair.
As I walked, my eyes were bent upon the beach so that it was not until I had come quite upon it that I discovered that which shattered all my beautiful dream of solitude and safety and peace and primal overlordship. The thing was a hollowed log drawn upon the sands, and in the bottom of it lay a crude paddle.
===Chinese===
景色美到极处；眼看既没有人也没有兽会威胁我新得的自由，我便滑下断崖的边，半滑半跌，落进那可喜的山谷——单看它的模样，就像一处和平与安稳的庇护所。
我沿岸走过的缓坡海滩上，密密铺满了形状古怪、五颜六色的贝壳：有的空着，有的还住着软体动物，其种类之繁，不亚于当年在外层洪荒海岸上拖着懒散一生的那芸芸众生。一路走，我不禁把自己比作另一个世界的第一个人：四周的孤独这样彻底，青春大自然的万般处女奇景这样原始而未经触犯。我自觉是第二个亚当，孤身跋涉在一个世界的童年里，寻找我的夏娃；这念头一起，我心灵的眼前便浮起一张完美脸庞的清丽轮廓，上面松松堆着一头美妙的乌发。
我一路走，眼睛只顾盯着海滩，直到几乎撞上那样东西，才发现了它——它把我这孤独、安全、和平与原始君主权的美梦砸了个粉碎。那是一拖到沙滩上的挖空的木头，底里横着一支粗糙的桨。

===Original===
The rude shock of awakening to what doubtless might prove some new form of danger was still upon me when I heard a rattling of loose stones from the direction of the bluff, and turning my eyes in that direction I beheld the author of the disturbance, a great copper-colored man, running rapidly toward me.
There was that in the haste with which he came which seemed quite sufficiently menacing, so that I did not need the added evidence of brandishing spear and scowling face to warn me that I was in no safe position, but whither to flee was indeed a momentous question.
The speed of the fellow seemed to preclude the possibility of escaping him upon the open beach. There was but a single alternative﻿—the rude skiff﻿—and with a celerity which equaled his, I pushed the thing into the sea and as it floated gave a final shove and clambered in over the end.
===Chinese===
这一记惊醒的震骇还未过去——那东西多半意味着某种新的危险——忽听得断崖那边碎石滚动，我把眼一转，望见了肇事的源头：一个高大的铜色人，正飞快朝我奔来。
他来势那般急切，光这已够凶多吉少，更不必说挥舞的长矛和愠怒的脸色来警告我处境不妙；可是往哪里逃，倒真是个天大的难题。
那家伙的速度，看来叫我在开阔的海滩上脱身无望。只剩一条路——那条破筏子；我以不输于他的迅捷把那东西推进海里，趁它浮起，最后一推，翻身从尾端爬了进去。

===Original===
A cry of rage rose from the owner of the primitive craft, and an instant later his heavy, stone-tipped spear grazed my shoulder and buried itself in the bow of the boat beyond. Then I grasped the paddle, and with feverish haste urged the awkward, wobbly thing out upon the surface of the sea.
A glance over my shoulder showed me that the copper-colored one had plunged in after me and was swimming rapidly in pursuit. His mighty strokes bade fair to close up the distance between us in short order, for at best I could make but slow progress with my unfamiliar craft, which nosed stubbornly in every direction but that which I desired to follow, so that fully half my energy was expended in turning its blunt prow back into the course.
I had covered some hundred yards from shore when it became evident that my pursuer must grasp the stern of the skiff within the next half-dozen strokes. In a frenzy of despair, I bent to the grandfather of all paddles in a hopeless effort to escape, and still the copper giant behind me gained and gained.
===Chinese===
这原始独木舟的主人发出一声狂怒的吼叫，转瞬间他那沉重的石尖矛擦过我的肩膀，深深扎进船头的木头里。我抓起桨，拼命把这件笨拙、摇晃的东西往海面上划。
我回头一瞥，那铜色人已纵身跳进海里追来，游得飞快。他那有力的划水眼看顷刻间就要追上我们之间的距离，因为我这条不驯服的破船至多只能慢慢挪——它固执地朝一切方向乱指，独独不肯朝我要去的方向，害得我一半力气都花在把那钝船头拨回航道上面。
我离岸约莫一百码时，眼看追我的人不出六七下划水就要抓住船尾了。绝望使我发狂般弯腰去摇那桨中之王的老祖宗，作徒劳的逃命挣扎；可身后那铜色巨人还是一寸一寸逼近。

===Original===
His hand was reaching upward for the stern when I saw a sleek, sinuous body shoot from the depths below. The man saw it too, and the look of terror that overspread his face assured me that I need have no further concern as to him, for the fear of certain death was in his look.
And then about him coiled the great, slimy folds of a hideous monster of that prehistoric deep﻿—a mighty serpent of the sea, with fanged jaws, and darting forked tongue, with bulging eyes, and bony protuberances upon head and snout that formed short, stout horns.
As I looked at that hopeless struggle my eyes met those of the doomed man, and I could have sworn that in his I saw an expression of hopeless appeal. But whether I did or not there swept through me a sudden compassion for the fellow. He was indeed a brother-man, and that he might have killed me with pleasure had he caught me was forgotten in the extremity of his danger.
===Chinese===
他的手正向上够那船尾，我忽见一条光滑柔韧的身躯从深处射出。那人也看见了；他脸上布满的恐怖向我保证，我不必再为他操心——那神色里有必死无疑的恐惧。
随即，那史前深水里一头丑恶巨怪的黏滑巨躯便缠上了他——一条大海蛇，腭间生着獠牙，吐着叉状的舌头，眼睛暴突，头和吻上生着骨质的突起，构成短而粗壮的角。
望着那场无望的挣扎，我的眼睛同那注定丧命的人的眼睛相遇了；我可以发誓，我在他眼里看到一种绝望的哀求。且不论看没看到，一阵突如其来的恻隐涌遍我全身。他终究是个人类的兄弟；他方才若抓住我，原会乐得杀了我——这在眼前这灭顶的危险里，全被忘掉了。

===Original===
Unconsciously I had ceased paddling as the serpent rose to engage my pursuer, so now the skiff still drifted close beside the two. The monster seemed to be but playing with his victim before he closed his awful jaws upon him and dragged him down to his dark den beneath the surface to devour him. The huge, snakelike body coiled and uncoiled about its prey. The hideous, gaping jaws snapped in the victim’s face. The forked tongue, lightning-like, ran in and out upon the copper skin.
Nobly the giant battled for his life, beating with his stone hatchet against the bony armor that covered that frightful carcass; but for all the damage he inflicted he might as well have struck with his open palm.
At last I could endure no longer to sit supinely by while a fellowman was dragged down to a horrible death by that repulsive reptile. Embedded in the prow of the skiff lay the spear that had been cast after me by him whom I suddenly desired to save. With a wrench I tore it loose, and standing upright in the wobbly log drove it with all the strength of my two arms straight into the gaping jaws of the hydrophidian.
With a loud hiss the creature abandoned its prey to turn upon me, but the spear, imbedded in its throat, prevented it from seizing me though it came near to overturning the skiff in its mad efforts to reach me.
===Chinese===
蛇升起缠住追我的人时，我不知不觉停了桨，于是那小筏子仍旧漂在两者近旁。那怪物似乎先拿它的猎物耍弄一番，才合上那可怕的腭把他拖下去，拖进水面下它黑暗的巢穴里吞吃。那巨蛇似的躯体在猎物身上一盘一松；那丑恶的血盆大口对着猎物的脸猛咬；那叉状的舌头闪电般在铜色皮肤上进进出出。
那巨人英勇地为性命搏斗，用石斧砍击盖住那可怕躯体的骨甲；可他砍出的那点损伤，还不如张开手掌拍打。
终于，我再也不能眼睁睁坐着，看一个同胞被那讨厌的爬虫拖下去不得好死。筏头里嵌着那支矛——正是我突然想救的这个人掷来掷我的。我使劲一拧把它拔了出来，在那摇晃的木舟里挺身直立，双臂使出全部力气，把矛笔直捅进那海蛇张开的大口。
那怪物嘶的一声大响，丢下猎物转身来对付我；可那矛深深扎在它喉咙里，使它抓不到我，虽然它发疯般够我，险些把筏子掀翻。

## X Phutra Again / 重返富特拉

===Original===
I hastened to the cliff edge above Ja and helped him to a secure footing. He would not listen to any thanks for his attempt to save me, which had come so near miscarrying.
“I had given you up for lost when you tumbled into the Mahar temple,” he said, “for not even I could save you from their clutches, and you may imagine my surprise when on seeing a canoe dragged up upon the beach of the mainland I discovered your own footprints in the sand beside it.
“I immediately set out in search of you, knowing as I did that you must be entirely unarmed and defenseless against the many dangers which lurk upon the mainland both in the form of savage beasts and reptiles, and men as well. I had no difficulty in tracking you to this point. It is well that I arrived when I did.”
===Chinese===
我急忙赶到崖边，俯身把雅扶到稳固的立足处。他方才为救我险些坏了事，却不肯听我半句道谢。
「你跌进玛哈尔人神庙的时候，我已经当你没命了，」他说，「因为连我也救不了你，让你逃出他们的魔掌。所以你可想而知，当我看见一艘独木舟被拖上大陆的沙滩，又在旁边发现你自己的脚印时，我是多么惊讶。
「我立刻动身来找你，因为我明知你手无寸铁，毫无自卫之力，而大陆上处处潜伏着危险——有凶猛的野兽和爬虫，也有人类。我一路追踪你到这儿，毫不费事。幸亏我来得正是时候。」

===Original===
“But why did you do it?” I asked, puzzled at this show of friendship on the part of a man of another world and a different race and color.
“You saved my life,” he replied; “from that moment it became my duty to protect and befriend you. I would have been no true Mezop had I evaded my plain duty; but it was a pleasure in this instance for I like you. I wish that you would come and live with me. You shall become a member of my tribe. Among us there is the best of hunting and fishing, and you shall have, to choose a mate from, the most beautiful girls of Pellucidar. Will you come?”
I told him about Perry then, and Dian the Beautiful, and how my duty was to them first. Afterward I should return and visit him﻿—if I could ever find his island.
===Chinese===
「可你为什么要这样做？」我问。此人来自另一个世界，种族肤色都与我迥异，竟对我表现出这般情谊，令我大惑不解。
「你救过我的命，」他答道，「从那一刻起，保护和善待你就成了我的本分。我若逃避这份明摆着的责任，就算不上一个真正的梅佐普人（Mezop）；不过这一回我甘之如饴，因为我喜欢你。我盼着你来同我一起生活，做我部落的一员。我们这里有最好的渔猎，你还可以从佩卢西达尔最美的姑娘们当中挑一个做伴侣。你来不来？」
于是我把佩里和美人黛安的事讲给他听，说明我首先对他们二人负有责任，日后自当回去看望他——只要我找得到他的海岛。

===Original===
“Oh, that is easy, my friend,” he said. “You need merely to come to the foot of the highest peak of the Mountains of the Clouds. There you will find a river which flows into the Lural Az. Directly opposite the mouth of the river you will see three large islands far out, so far that they are barely discernible, the one to the extreme left as you face them from the mouth of the river is Anoroc, where I rule the tribe of Anoroc.”
“But how am I to find the Mountains of the Clouds?” I asked. “Men say that they are visible from half Pellucidar,” he replied.
“How large is Pellucidar?” I asked, wondering what sort of theory these primitive men had concerning the form and substance of their world.
===Chinese===
「哦，那容易，我的朋友，」他说，「你只消走到云中山脉（Mountains of the Clouds）最高峰的脚下。那里有一条河，注入卢拉尔阿兹（Lural Az）。正对河口望去，远处的海上能看见三座大岛，远得几乎辨不出来；你从河口朝它们望过去，最左端那座就是阿诺罗克（Anoroc），我统治着阿诺罗克部落。」
「可我怎么找得到云中山脉呢？」我问。「据说隔着半个佩卢西达尔都望得见那山。」他答道。
「佩卢西达尔有多大？」我问，心里琢磨着这些原始人对他们这个世界的形状与构造有着怎样的看法。

===Original===
“The Mahars say it is round, like the inside of a tola shell,” he answered, “but that is ridiculous, since, were it true, we should fall back were we to travel far in any direction, and all the waters of Pellucidar would run to one spot and drown us. No, Pellucidar is quite flat and extends no man knows how far in all directions. At the edges, so my ancestors have reported and handed down to me, is a great wall that prevents the earth and waters from escaping over into the burning sea whereon Pellucidar floats; but I never have been so far from Anoroc as to have seen this wall with my own eyes. However, it is quite reasonable to believe that this is true, whereas there is no reason at all in the foolish belief of the Mahars. According to them Pellucidarians who live upon the opposite side walk always with their heads pointed downward!” and Ja laughed uproariously at the very thought.
It was plain to see that the human folk of this inner world had not advanced far in learning, and the thought that the ugly Mahars had so outstripped them was a very pathetic one indeed. I wondered how many ages it would take to lift these people out of their ignorance even were it given to Perry and me to attempt it. Possibly we would be killed for our pains as were those men of the outer world who dared challenge the dense ignorance and superstitions of the earth’s younger days. But it was worth the effort if the opportunity ever presented itself.
And then it occurred to me that here was an opportunity﻿—that I might make a small beginning upon Ja, who was my friend, and thus note the effect of my teaching upon a Pellucidarian.
===Chinese===
「玛哈尔人说它是圆的，像托拉（tola）贝壳的里面，」他答道，「可这也太荒唐了：倘若当真如此，我们朝任何方向走远了都得倒翻回去，佩卢西达尔的全部水也会流到一处，把我们淹死。不，佩卢西达尔完全是平的，向四面八方延伸出去不知多远。据我的先人们代代相传，在世界的边缘有一道大墙，拦住大地和众水，不让它们溢出去，落进托着佩卢西达尔的那片燃烧之海。只是我从来没有离开阿诺罗克那么远，不曾亲眼见过那道墙。然而这说法倒还讲得通，玛哈尔人那套愚蠢的信念却毫无道理。照他们的说法，住在正对面的佩卢西达尔人走路时脑袋永远朝下！」雅一想到这个便纵声大笑。
不难看出，这个地心世界的人类在学问上进步甚微；而丑陋的玛哈尔人竟把他们远远抛在了后面，这实在可悲。我不禁思量，纵然让佩里和我放手去做，要多少个世代才能把这些同胞从蒙昧里提升出来。说不定我们还会白白赔上性命，正如外层世界那些胆敢挑战蒙昧与迷信的先人一样。但只要机会来临，这份努力总是值得的。
接着我忽然想到，眼前不正是一个机会么——我可以先在雅这个朋友身上做个小小的开端，看看我的教导在一个佩卢西达尔人身上效果如何。

===Original===
“Ja,” I said, “what would you say were I to tell you that in so far as the Mahars’ theory of the shape of Pellucidar is concerned it is correct?”
“I would say,” he replied, “that either you are a fool, or took me for one.”
“But, Ja,” I insisted, “if their theory is incorrect how do you account for the fact that I was able to pass through the earth from the outer crust to Pellucidar. If your theory is correct all is a sea of flame beneath us, wherein no peoples could exist, and yet I come from a great world that is covered with human beings, and beasts, and birds, and fishes in mighty oceans.”
===Chinese===
「雅，」我说，「倘若我告诉你：就佩卢西达尔的形状而论，玛哈尔人的理论是对的，你会怎么说？」
「我会说，」他答道，「你要么是个傻瓜，要么就是拿我当傻瓜。」
「可是，雅，」我坚持道，「倘若他们的理论不对，你怎么解释我能从外层地壳穿透地球来到佩卢西达尔？倘若你的理论是对的，我们脚下就该是一片火海，那里面不可能存在任何人群；而我偏偏来自一个伟大的世界，那上面遍布人类和走兽飞禽，浩瀚的海洋里游满了鱼。」

===Original===
“You live upon the under side of Pellucidar, and walk always with your head pointed downward?” he scoffed. “And were I to believe that, my friend, I should indeed be mad.”
I attempted to explain the force of gravity to him, and by the means of the dropped fruit to illustrate how impossible it would be for a body to fall off the earth under any circumstances. He listened so intently that I thought I had made an impression, and started the train of thought that would lead him to a partial understanding of the truth. But I was mistaken.
“Your own illustration,” he said finally, “proves the falsity of your theory.” He dropped a fruit from his hand to the ground. “See,” he said, “without support even this tiny fruit falls until it strikes something that stops it. If Pellucidar were not supported upon the flaming sea it too would fall as the fruit falls﻿—you have proven it yourself!” He had me, that time﻿—you could see it in his eye.
===Chinese===
「你住在佩卢西达尔的底面，走路永远头朝下？」他讥讽道，「我的朋友，我若信了这个，那才真是疯了。」
我试着向他解释重力的作用，又借丢下的果子作演示，说明无论如何物体都不可能跌离地球。他听得那样专注，我还以为自己打动了它，启动了一串终将引他部分领悟真相的思路。可我错了。
「你自己举的例子，」他末了说道，「恰恰证明你的理论站不住。」他让一枚果子从手中落到地上。「瞧，」他说，「失去了支撑，连这小小的果子也要一直往下掉，直到撞上什么挡住它的东西。倘若佩卢西达尔不是浮在燃烧之海上面，它也会像果子一样坠落——这是你自己证明的！」这一回可让他问住了我——你从他眼色里就看得出来。

===Original===
It seemed a hopeless job and I gave it up, temporarily at least, for when I contemplated the necessity explanation of our solar system and the universe I realized how futile it would be to attempt to picture to Ja or any other Pellucidarian the sun, the moon, the planets, and the countless stars. Those born within the inner world could no more conceive of such things than can we of the outer crust reduce to factors appreciable to our finite minds such terms as space and eternity.
“Well, Ja,” I laughed, “whether we be walking with our feet up or down, here we are, and the question of greatest importance is not so much where we came from as where we are going now. For my part I wish that you could guide me to Phutra where I may give myself up to the Mahars once more that my friends and I may work out the plan of escape which the Sagoths interrupted when they gathered us together and drove us to the arena to witness the punishment of the slaves who killed the guardsman. I wish now that I had not left the arena for by this time my friends and I might have made good our escape, whereas this delay may mean the wrecking of all our plans, which depended for their consummation upon the continued sleep of the three Mahars who lay in the pit beneath the building in which we were confined.”
“You would return to captivity?” cried Ja.
===Chinese===
这似乎是件毫无指望的差事，我便作罢了——至少暂且作罢。因为一想到还得解释我们的太阳系和宇宙，我就明白，要向雅或任何别的佩卢西达尔人描画太阳、月亮、行星和数不清的星辰，是何等徒劳。生在地心世界的人没法构想这些东西，正如我们这些外层地壳的人，也没法把「空间」「永恒」这类字眼化归为有限心智所能理解的因数。
「好吧，雅，」我笑道，「不管咱们走路是脚朝上还是脚朝下，反正人已经在这儿了；眼下最要紧的，与其说是咱们从哪儿来，不如说是咱们这就要往哪儿去。就我而论，我倒是希望你能领我到富特拉去，让我重新把自己交到玛哈尔人手里，好让我和同伴们把那越狱计划继续推行下去——当初萨戈思人把我们集中起来赶进竞技场、去看惩处杀死卫兵的奴隶时，恰好打断了它。如今我倒懊悔离开了竞技场，因为到这时我们本可顺利脱身；而这一耽搁，或许会砸了我们全部的计划——那计划的成败，全指着关我们的那座楼底下坑里睡着的三个玛哈尔人一直睡下去。」
「你要重回枷锁？」雅叫道。

===Original===
“My friends are there,” I replied, “the only friends I have in Pellucidar, except yourself. What else may I do under the circumstances?”
He thought for a moment in silence. Then he shook his head sorrowfully.
“It is what a brave man and a good friend should do,” he said; “yet it seems most foolish, for the Mahars will most certainly condemn you to death for running away, and so you will be accomplishing nothing for your friends by returning. Never in all my life have I heard of a prisoner returning to the Mahars of his own free will. There are but few who escape them, though some do, and these would rather die than be recaptured.”
===Chinese===
「我的朋友们在那儿，」我答道，「除了你，他们是我在佩卢西达尔仅有的朋友。事到如今，我还能怎么办？」
他默默想了一会儿，随即悲伤地摇了摇头。
「这正是一个勇士兼好朋友该做的事，」他说，「可又实在愚蠢至极，因为玛哈尔人必定会因为你逃跑而判你死刑，你回去不但帮不了朋友一点忙，反而白白送死。我平生从未听说有哪个俘虏自愿回到玛哈尔人那里去。能从他们手里逃脱的没有几个——虽说也有——而那些人情愿死也不愿再度被擒。」

===Original===
“I see no other way, Ja,” I said, “though I can assure you that I would rather go to Sheol after Perry than to Phutra. However, Perry is much too pious to make the probability at all great that I should ever be called upon to rescue him from the former locality.”
Ja asked me what Sheol was, and when I explained, as best I could, he said, “You are speaking of Molop Az, the flaming sea upon which Pellucidar floats. All the dead who are buried in the ground go there. Piece by piece they are carried down to Molop Az by the little demons who dwell there. We know this because when graves are opened we find that the bodies have been partially or entirely borne off. That is why we of Anoroc place our dead in high trees where the birds may find them and bear them bit by bit to the Dead World above the Land of Awful Shadow. If we kill an enemy we place his body in the ground that it may go to Molop Az.”
As we talked we had been walking up the canyon down which I had come to the great ocean and the sithic. Ja did his best to dissuade me from returning to Phutra, but when he saw that I was determined to do so, he consented to guide me to a point from which I could see the plain where lay the city. To my surprise the distance was but short from the beach where I had again met Ja. It was evident that I had spent much time following the windings of a tortuous canyon, while just beyond the ridge lay the city of Phutra near to which I must have come several times.
===Chinese===
「我别无他路，雅，」我说，「不过我大可以向你保证：与其去富特拉，我倒宁愿跟着佩里下希奥耳（Sheol）。好在佩里虔诚得很，要我到前一个地方去救他，这可能性实在小得很。」
雅问我希奥耳是什么，我尽力解释了一番，他说：「你说的是莫洛普阿兹（Molop Az），托着佩卢西达尔的那片燃烧之海。凡是埋进土里的死者都往那里去，被住在那里的个小妖一块一块地搬下莫洛普阿兹。这个我们知道，因为坟墓扒开时，我们发现尸身总被搬走了一部分或者整个没了。所以我们阿诺罗克人把死者安放在高树上，让飞鸟去找他们，一口一口把他们衔到可怖之影之地（Land of Awful Shadow）上方的死亡世界去。要是杀了仇敌，我们就把他的尸身埋进土里，好叫他下莫洛普阿兹。」
我们说着话，已沿着峡谷往上走——我先前正是从这条峡谷下到大洋和那只西西克（*sithic*）那里去的。雅竭力劝我别回富特拉，但见我心意已决，便答应把我送到一处能望见那座城所在平原的地方。令我吃惊的是，从我再遇雅的沙滩到那里路途很短。显然，我先前尽在曲折峡谷的弯道里兜圈子，费去许多时光，而山脊那一面便是富特拉城，我必定几次三番走到离它很近的地方了。

===Original===
As we topped the ridge and saw the granite gate towers dotting the flowered plain at our feet Ja made a final effort to persuade me to abandon my mad purpose and return with him to Anoroc, but I was firm in my resolve, and at last he bid me goodbye, assured in his own mind that he was looking upon me for the last time.
I was sorry to part with Ja, for I had come to like him very much indeed. With his hidden city upon the island of Anoroc as a base, and his savage warriors as escort Perry and I could have accomplished much in the line of exploration, and I hoped that were we successful in our effort to escape we might return to Anoroc later.
There was, however, one great thing to be accomplished first﻿—at least it was the great thing to me﻿—the finding of Dian the Beautiful. I wanted to make amends for the affront I had put upon her in my ignorance, and I wanted to﻿—well, I wanted to see her again, and to be with her.
===Chinese===
我们登上山脊，望见花岗岩的门塔星星点点缀在脚下开满鲜花的平原上，雅最后一次努力劝我撇下这疯狂的念头，随他回阿诺罗克去；但我决心已定，他终于向我道别，心里认定这是最后一次见我了。
与雅分手令我难过，因为我实在已经非常喜欢他了。有他那座藏匿在阿诺罗克岛上的城作根据地，再有他的野蛮武士们护卫，佩里和我在探险一途本可大有作为；我巴望越狱成功之后，日后还能重返阿诺罗克。
不过，首先还有一件大事要办——至少对我是头等大事——那就是找到美人黛安。我要为自己出于无知加诸她的冒犯作出补偿；我还想——唉，我想再见她，同她在一起。

===Original===
Down the hillside I made my way into the gorgeous field of flowers, and then across the rolling land toward the shadowless columns that guard the ways to buried Phutra. At a quarter-mile from the nearest entrance I was discovered by the Sagoth guard, and in an instant four of the gorilla-men were dashing toward me.
Though they brandished their long spears and yelled like wild Comanches I paid not the slightest attention to them, walking quietly toward them as though unaware of their existence. My manner had the effect upon them that I had hoped, and as we came quite near together they ceased their savage shouting. It was evident that they had expected me to turn and flee at sight of them, thus presenting that which they most enjoyed, a moving human target at which to cast their spears.
“What do you here?” shouted one, and then as he recognized me, “Ho! It is the slave who claims to be from another world﻿—he who escaped when the *thag* ran amuck within the amphitheater. But why do you return, having once made good your escape?”
===Chinese===
我顺山坡下到那片绚烂的花田里，再穿过起伏的丘陵，朝守卫着通往地下富特拉各条道路的无影石柱走去。离最近的入口还有四分之一英里，萨戈思卫兵便发现了我，转眼间四个人猿直朝我扑来。
他们虽然挥舞长矛、像发狂的科曼奇人（Comanches）一般喊杀，我却毫不在意，只管安步当车朝他们走去，仿佛不曾察觉他们的存在。我的姿态收到了预期的效果：双方凑得颇近时，他们止住了野蛮的呐喊。显然，他们本以为我一见他们就会转身逃跑，好奉上他们最津津乐道的东西——一个移动的活人靶子，供他们投矛取乐。
「你来干什么？」一个喝道，随即认出了我，「嗬！这不是那个自称来自另一个世界的奴隶么——竞技场里*塔格*（*thag*）发狂时跑掉的那个。可你既然已经脱身，干吗还要回来？」

===Original===
“I did not ‘escape,’ ” I replied. “I but ran away to avoid the *thag*, as did others, and coming into a long passage I became confused and lost my way in the foothills beyond Phutra. Only now have I found my way back.”
“And you come of your free will back to Phutra!” exclaimed one of the guardsmen.
“Where else might I go?” I asked. “I am a stranger within Pellucidar and know no other where than Phutra. Why should I not desire to be in Phutra? Am I not well fed and well treated? Am I not happy? What better lot could man desire?”
===Chinese===
「我并没有『逃走』，」我答道，「我不过跟别人一样，是躲那头*塔格*才跑开的；后来误入一条长廊，在富特拉以外的山麓间迷了路，直到此刻才找回来。」
「你竟是心甘情愿回富特拉来的！」一名卫兵失声喊道。
「我还能上哪儿去？」我说，「我在佩卢西达尔人地两生，除了富特拉再不知有别处。我怎么会不愿待在富特拉？我在这里不是吃得饱、待得好么？我不快活么？人还能指望比这更好的命运么？」

===Original===
The Sagoths scratched their heads. This was a new one on them, and so being stupid brutes they took me to their masters whom they felt would be better fitted to solve the riddle of my return, for riddle they still considered it.
I had spoken to the Sagoths as I had for the purpose of throwing them off the scent of my purposed attempt at escape. If they thought that I was so satisfied with my lot within Phutra that I would voluntarily return when I had once had so excellent an opportunity to escape, they would never for an instant imagine that I could be occupied in arranging another escape immediately upon my return to the city.
So they led me before a slimy Mahar who clung to a slimy rock within the large room that was the thing’s office. With cold, reptilian eyes the creature seemed to bore through the thin veneer of my deceit and read my inmost thoughts. It heeded the story which the Sagoths told of my return to Phutra, watching the gorilla-men’s lips and fingers during the recital. Then it questioned me through one of the Sagoths.
===Chinese===
萨戈思人挠起了头。这可是桩新鲜事，于是这帮蠢笨的畜生便把我押去见他们的主人，觉得主人更配来解我回归之谜——他们始终拿它当个谜。
我先前对萨戈思人那样说话，正是为了引开他们的鼻子，别嗅出我图谋越狱的踪迹。他们若以为我对富特拉的境遇心满意足，以至有过那般绝佳的逃跑机会还要自愿回来，就绝不会有一瞬想到，我一回城便要立刻着手布置第二次逃跑。
于是他们把我押到一只黏滑的玛哈尔人面前，它趴在一间大屋里一块同样黏滑的岩石上——那大屋便是这东西的公署。这生物用冷冷的爬虫眼睛仿佛要钻透我那层单薄的欺骗贴面，直读出我内心最深处的念头。它听萨戈思人讲述我回富特拉的经过，叙述时眼睛盯着这些猿人的嘴唇和手指。随后它借一名萨戈思人之口盘问我。

===Original===
“You say that you returned to Phutra of your own free will, because you think yourself better off here than elsewhere﻿—do you not know that you may be the next chosen to give up your life in the interests of the wonderful scientific investigations that our learned ones are continually occupied with?”
I hadn’t heard of anything of that nature, but I thought best not to admit it.
“I could be in no more danger here,” I said, “than naked and unarmed in the savage jungles or upon the lonely plains of Pellucidar. I was fortunate, I think, to return to Phutra at all. As it was I barely escaped death within the jaws of a huge *sithic*. No, I am sure that I am safer in the hands of intelligent creatures such as rule Phutra. At least such would be the case in my own world, where human beings like myself rule supreme. There the higher races of man extend protection and hospitality to the stranger within their gates, and being a stranger here I naturally assumed that a like courtesy would be accorded me.”
===Chinese===
「你说你自愿回到富特拉，是因为自以为这里比别处强——你可知道，我们博学之士们不断忙着种种了不起的科学研究，下一个被选中交出性命的，说不定就是你？」
这一类事情我从未听说过，但我想还是不认账为妙。
「我在这里能有什么危险，」我说，「总不至于大过赤身露体、手无寸铁地闯荡佩卢西达尔的蛮荒丛林或孤寂平原吧。我想，我能回到富特拉已属万幸；实情是我险些死在一头巨大*西西克*的腭间。不，我敢肯定，在这班统治富特拉的智慧生物手中我更安全。至少在我自己的世界里情形如此：在那里，如我这般的人类君临一切，较高的人种对入界的外乡人一概给予保护和款待；我在此地既是外乡人，自然以为会受到同等的礼遇。」

===Original===
The Mahar looked at me in silence for some time after I ceased speaking and the Sagoth had translated my words to his master. The creature seemed deep in thought. Presently he communicated some message to the Sagoth. The latter turned, and motioning me to follow him, left the presence of the reptile. Behind and on either side of me marched the balance of the guard.
“What are they going to do with me?” I asked the fellow at my right.
“You are to appear before the learned ones who will question you regarding this strange world from which you say you come.”
===Chinese===
我住口之后，萨戈思人又把我的话译给它的主人，那玛哈尔人便默不作声地打量了我好一阵。这生物似乎陷入了深思。片刻后它向萨戈思人传达了几句什么。那萨戈思人转过身，示意我跟上，领我退出了这爬虫的面前。其余的卫兵在我身后和左右押行。
「他们要拿我怎么样？」我问右边那个家伙。
「你要去见那些博学之士，他们将审问你自称来自的那个怪世界。」

===Original===
After a moment’s silence he turned to me again.
“Do you happen to know,” he asked, “what the Mahars do to slaves who lie to them?”
“No,” I replied, “nor does it interest me, as I have no intention of lying to the Mahars.”
===Chinese===
沉默片刻之后，他又转向我。
「你可知道，」他问，「玛哈尔人怎样处置对他们撒谎的奴隶？」
「不知道，」我答道，「也不关心，因为我压根不打算对玛哈尔人撒谎。」

===Original===
“Then be careful that you don’t repeat the impossible tale you told Sol-to-to just now﻿—another world, indeed, where human beings rule!” he concluded in fine scorn.
“But it is the truth,” I insisted. “From where else then did I come? I am not of Pellucidar. Anyone with half an eye could see that.”
“It is your misfortune then,” he remarked dryly, “that you may not be judged by one with but half an eye.”
===Chinese===
「那你可当心，别再把刚才对索尔托托（Sol-to-to）讲的那套不可能的故事重复一遍——居然还有什么另一个世界，人类当政！」他极尽轻蔑地收了尾。
「可那是实话，」我坚持道，「不然我打哪儿来？我不是佩卢西达尔人，随便哪个长着半只眼的人都看得出来。」
「那可就是你的不幸了，」他冷冷地说，「偏偏没长半只眼的家伙来审你。」

===Original===
“What will they do with me,” I asked, “if they do not have a mind to believe me?”
“You may be sentenced to the arena, or go to the pits to be used in research work by the learned ones,” he replied.
“And what will they do with me there?” I persisted.
===Chinese===
「要是他们不肯相信我，」我问，「他们会怎么处置我？」
「你可能被判进竞技场，也可能下坑里去，供博学之士做研究之用。」他答道。
「在那儿他们会把我怎么样？」我追问。

===Original===
“No one knows except the Mahars and those who go to the pits with them, but as the latter never return, their knowledge does them but little good. It is said that the learned ones cut up their subjects while they are yet alive, thus learning many useful things. However I should not imagine that it would prove very useful to him who was being cut up; but of course this is all but conjecture. The chances are that ere long you will know much more about it than I,” and he grinned as he spoke. The Sagoths have a well-developed sense of humor.
“And suppose it is the arena,” I continued; “what then?”
“You saw the two who met the *tarag* and the *thag* the time that you escaped?” he said.
===Chinese===
「除了玛哈尔人和随他们下坑的人，谁也不知道；可下去的人从没回来过，他们的见识对他们自己也没多大用处。据说博学之士解剖那些人时，人都还活着，这样能学到许多有用的东西。不过我想，这对挨解剖的那位未必很有用；当然，这一切全是猜测。多半要不了多久，你对此就知道得比我多得多了。」他说话时咧着嘴笑。萨戈思人的幽默感可谓发达。
「假如进的是竞技场呢，」我接着说，「那会怎样？」
「你逃跑那回，看见那两个遇上*塔拉格*（*tarag*）和*塔格*的人了吧？」他说。

===Original===
“Yes.”
“Your end in the arena would be similar to what was intended for them,” he explained, “though of course the same kinds of animals might not be employed.”
“It is sure death in either event?” I asked.
===Chinese===
「看见了。」
「你若进竞技场，下场便同他们原本的下场相仿，」他解释道，「当然，未必用同几种兽。」
「反正两下里都是死定了？」我问。

===Original===
“What becomes of those who go below with the learned ones I do not know, nor does any other,” he replied; “but those who go to the arena may come out alive and thus regain their liberty, as did the two whom you saw.”
“They gained their liberty? And how?”
“It is the custom of the Mahars to liberate those who remain alive within the arena after the beasts depart or are killed. Thus it has happened that several mighty warriors from far distant lands, whom we have captured on our slave raids, have battled the brutes turned in upon them and slain them, thereby winning their freedom. In the instance which you witnessed the beasts killed each other, but the result was the same﻿—the man and woman were liberated, furnished with weapons, and started on their homeward journey. Upon the left shoulder of each a mark was burned﻿—the mark of the Mahars﻿—which will forever protect these two from slaving parties.”
===Chinese===
「随博学之士下坑的人落个什么结局，我不知道，旁人也一样不知道，」他答道，「但进竞技场的人有可能活着出来，从而恢复自由，就像你见到的那两个。」
「他们恢复了自由？怎么恢复的？」
「玛哈尔人的规矩是：野兽散去或被杀死之后，竞技场里还活着的人一律释放。因此发生过这样的事：几次掠奴远征中我们从远方掳来的几位骁勇武士，同放进场的野兽搏斗并将它们杀死，从而赢得了自由。你亲眼所见的那一例，野兽自相残杀而死，结果也一样——那一男一女获得释放，领了武器，踏上归途。两人左肩上各烙了一个印记——玛哈尔人的印记——这印记将永远保他们不被掠奴队掳掠。」

===Original===
“There is a slender chance for me then if I be sent to the arena, and none at all if the learned ones drag me to the pits?”
“You are quite right,” he replied; “but do not felicitate yourself too quickly should you be sent to the arena, for there is scarce one in a thousand who comes out alive.”
To my surprise they returned me to the same building in which I had been confined with Perry and Ghak before my escape. At the doorway I was turned over to the guards there.
===Chinese===
「这么说，我被送进竞技场还有一线生机；若是被博学之士拖下坑去，就毫无指望了？」
「你说得对，」他答道，「不过就算进的是竞技场，也别忙着庆幸，因为一千人里头也难得有一个活着出来。」
出乎我的意料，他们把我押回了逃跑之前我和佩里、格哈克一同被关的那座建筑。在门口，我被移交给了那里的看守。

===Original===
“He will doubtless be called before the investigators shortly,” said he who had brought me back, “so have him in readiness.”
The guards in whose hands I now found myself, upon hearing that I had returned of my own volition to Phutra evidently felt that it would be safe to give me liberty within the building as had been the custom before I had escaped, and so I was told to return to whatever duty had been mine formerly.
My first act was to hunt up Perry, whom I found poring as usual over the great tomes that he was supposed to be merely dusting and rearranging upon new shelves.
===Chinese===
「他八成儿很快就会奉传去见审讯官，」押我回来的人说，「随时让他准备着。」
如今看管我的这些卫兵听说我是出于自愿返回富特拉，显然觉得不妨照我逃跑前的老规矩，容我在楼内自由行动，于是吩咐我仍旧去做先前分派的差事。
我头一桩事便是去找佩里，只见他照旧埋头苦读那几大部头——那些书原本只派他掸灰、再摆上新书架的。

===Original===
As I entered the room he glanced up and nodded pleasantly to me, only to resume his work as though I had never been away at all. I was both astonished and hurt at his indifference. And to think that I was risking death to return to him purely from a sense of duty and affection!
“Why, Perry!” I exclaimed, “haven’t you a word for me after my long absence?”
“Long absence!” he repeated in evident astonishment. “What do you mean?”
===Chinese===
我进屋时，他抬眼朝我愉快地点了点头，便又接着干他的活，仿佛我从来不曾离开过。他这般漠然，令我又是惊讶又是伤心。想想吧，我纯粹出于责任和情义，才冒着死亡的危险回到他身边！
「哎呀，佩里！」我喊道，「我离开了这么久，你竟没有一句话对我说么？」
「离开了这么久！」他分明惊讶地重复道，「你这是什么意思？」

===Original===
“Are you crazy, Perry? Do you mean to say that you have not missed me since that time we were separated by the charging *thag* within the arena?”
“ ‘That time,’ ” he repeated. “Why man, I have but just returned from the arena! You reached here almost as soon as I. Had you been much later I should indeed have been worried, and as it is I had intended asking you about how you escaped the beast as soon as I had completed the translation of this most interesting passage.”
“Perry, you *are* mad,” I exclaimed. “Why, the Lord only knows how long I have been away. I have been to other lands, discovered a new race of humans within Pellucidar, seen the Mahars at their worship in their hidden temple, and barely escaped with my life from them and from a great *labyrinthodon* that I met afterward, following my long and tedious wanderings across an unknown world. I must have been away for months, Perry, and now you barely look up from your work when I return and insist that we have been separated but a moment. Is that any way to treat a friend? I’m surprised at you, Perry, and if I’d thought for a moment that you cared no more for me than this I should not have returned to chance death at the hands of the Mahars for your sake.”
===Chinese===
「你疯了么，佩里？你是说，从竞技场里那头*塔格*冲散我们那时起，你就一直不曾惦记过我？」
「『那时』，」他重复道，「老兄，我自己也才从竞技场回来不久！你到这儿几乎跟我同时。你若再晚来些，我才真要着急呢；就这样，我还打算一译完这段顶有趣的文章，就问问你是怎样从那畜生爪下脱身的。」
「佩里，你真是疯了，」我喊道，「嗐，天知道我离开了多久！我到过别的地界，在佩卢西达尔发现了一个新的人类种族，见过玛哈尔人在他们隐秘的神庙里礼拜，九死一生才逃出他们的手心，随后在漫长而艰辛的漫游、穿越一个未知世界之后，又险些丧命于遇到的一头巨大的*迷齿螈*（*labyrinthodon*）。我准是走开了好几个月，佩里！可我一回来，你竟几乎连头也不抬，硬说我们不过分开了一小会儿。这是对待朋友的法子么？佩里，你真让我寒心；早知你待我不过如此，我才不会为了你回来，拿性命到玛哈尔人手里去碰运气呢。」

===Original===
The old man looked at me for a long time before he spoke. There was a puzzled expression upon his wrinkled face, and a look of hurt sorrow in his eyes.
“David, my boy,” he said, “how could you for a moment doubt my love for you? There is something strange here that I cannot understand. I know that I am not mad, and I am equally sure that you are not; but how in the world are we to account for the strange hallucinations that each of us seems to harbor relative to the passage of time since last we saw each other. You are positive that months have gone by, while to me it seems equally certain that not more than an hour ago I sat beside you in the amphitheater. Can it be that both of us are right and at the same time both are wrong? First tell me what time is, and then maybe I can solve our problem. Do you catch my meaning?”
I didn’t and said so.
===Chinese===
老人望了我好一会儿才开口。他那张布满皱纹的脸上带着困惑的神情，眼里含着受了伤似的悲戚。
「戴维，我的孩子，」他说，「你怎么会有一时一刻怀疑我对你的爱？这里头有桩我参不透的怪事。我明白我自己没有疯，也同样确信你没有疯；可是，对于上次分别以来光阴的流逝，咱们俩似乎各抱着一套古怪的幻觉，这到底该怎样解释？你咬定过去了几个月，而我同样确信，不出一个钟头之前我还坐在竞技场里你的身旁。会不会咱们俩都对，同时又都错了？先告诉我时间是什么，然后我或许就能解开咱们的难题。你听懂我的意思了么？」
我不懂，也照直说了。

===Original===
“Yes,” continued the old man, “we are both right. To me, bent over my book here, there has been no lapse of time. I have done little or nothing to waste my energies and so have required neither food nor sleep, but you, on the contrary, have walked and fought and wasted strength and tissue which must needs be rebuilt by nutriment and food, and so, having eaten and slept many times since last you saw me you naturally measure the lapse of time largely by these acts. As a matter of fact, David, I am rapidly coming to the conviction that there is no such thing as time﻿—surely there can be no time here within Pellucidar, where there are no means for measuring or recording time. Why, the Mahars themselves take no account of such a thing as time. I find here in all their literary works but a single tense, the present. There seems to be neither past nor future with them. Of course it is impossible for our outer-earthly minds to grasp such a condition, but our recent experiences seem to demonstrate its existence.”
It was too big a subject for me, and I said so, but Perry seemed to enjoy nothing better than speculating upon it, and after listening with interest to my account of the adventures through which I had passed he returned once more to the subject, which he was enlarging upon with considerable fluency when he was interrupted by the entrance of a Sagoth.
“Come!” commanded the intruder, beckoning to me. “The investigators would speak with you.”
===Chinese===
「不错，」老人接着说，「咱们俩都对。对我而言，伏在这儿读书，时间并没有流逝：我几乎不曾耗费精力，因此既不需要吃，也不需要睡。你却相反，一路行走、搏斗，耗损了气力和血肉，必须靠养分和食物重新造出来；于是自上次见我以来，你吃了睡、睡了吃不知多少回，自然便主要拿这些来计量光阴的流逝了。说实在的，戴维，我正迅速趋向一个信念：根本没有时间这种东西——在佩卢西达尔这个无法计量、无法记录时间的地方，肯定没有时间。你看，连玛哈尔人自己也不把时间当一回事。我在此地他们全部的书文里只找到一种时态——现在时。在他们看来既没有过去，也没有将来。当然，要我们这些外层地球的头脑去把握这样一种状态是不可能的，但咱们近来的经历，似乎恰恰证明它确乎存在。」
这个题目对我太大了，我也这么说了；可佩里似乎最爱琢磨它不过。他饶有兴味地听完我讲述自己经历的种种冒险，又回到这个题目上来，正发挥得滔滔不绝，一个萨戈思人进来把他打断了。
「来！」这不速之客向我招手，喝令道，「审讯官要同你说话。」

===Original===
“Goodbye, Perry!” I said, clasping the old man’s hand. “There may be nothing but the present and no such thing as time, but I feel that I am about to take a trip into the hereafter from which I shall never return. If you and Ghak should manage to escape I want you to promise me that you will find Dian the Beautiful and tell her that with my last words I asked her forgiveness for the unintentional affront I put upon her, and that my one wish was to be spared long enough to right the wrong that I had done her.”
Tears came to Perry’s eyes.
“I cannot believe but that you will return, David,” he said. “It would be awful to think of living out the balance of my life without you among these hateful and repulsive creatures. If you are taken away I shall never escape, for I feel that I am as well off here as I should be anywhere within this buried world.
