Some time in past ages an earthquake had produced a fault at this point, so that beyond the spot where I stood the strata had slipped down a matter of twenty feet. The result was that the continuation of my ledge lay twenty feet below me, where it ended as abruptly as did the end upon which I stood.
And here, evidently halted in flight by this insurmountable break in the ledge, stood the object of the creature’s attack﻿—a girl cowering upon the narrow platform, her face buried in her arms, as though to shut out the sight of the frightful death which hovered just above her.
The dragon was circling lower, and seemed about to dart in upon its prey. There was no time to be lost, scarce an instant in which to weigh the possible chances that I had against the awfully armed creature; but the sight of that frightened girl below me called out to all that was best in me, and the instinct for protection of the other sex, which nearly must have equaled the instinct of self-preservation in primeval man, drew me to the girl’s side like an irresistible magnet.

===Chinese===
远古的某个时候，一场地震在这里造成了一处断层，我站立之处再往前，岩层便下滑了约莫二十英尺。结果就是，我这条岩架的延续段落落在我下方二十英尺处，并且同我脚下这一段一样，到得突兀。
而在这里，显然是被岩架上这处无法逾越的断裂截断了逃路，立着那怪物袭击的目标——一个瑟缩在狭窄平台上的姑娘，脸埋在臂弯里，仿佛要把就在头顶盘旋的可怖死亡挡在视线之外。
巨龙盘旋得越来越低，眼看就要朝猎物扑击。分秒不容耽误，几乎来不及掂量我面对这武装可怖的家伙能有几分胜算；但下方那受惊姑娘的身影，唤醒了我心中一切至善的东西，而保护异性的本能——它在原始人身上几乎同自我保全的本能一样强烈——像一块不可抗拒的磁石，把我吸到了姑娘身边。

===Original===
Almost thoughtless of the consequences, I leaped from the end of the ledge upon which I stood, for the tiny shelf twenty feet below. At the same instant the dragon darted in toward the girl, but my sudden advent upon the scene must have startled him for he veered to one side, and then rose above us once more.
The noise I made as I landed beside her convinced the girl that the end had come, for she thought I was the dragon; but finally when no cruel fangs closed upon her she raised her eyes in astonishment. As they fell upon me the expression that came into them would be difficult to describe; but her feelings could scarcely have been one whit more complicated than my own﻿—for the wide eyes that looked into mine were those of Dian the Beautiful.
“Dian!” I cried. “Dian! Thank God that I came in time.”
“You?” she whispered, and then she hid her face again; nor could I tell whether she were glad or angry that I had come.

===Chinese===
我几乎顾不上考虑后果，便从所站岩架的尽头，朝下方二十英尺处那一小方平台纵身跃下。同一瞬间，巨龙朝姑娘扑去，但我突然现身战场，想必吓了它一跳，它偏向一侧，随后又升回我们头顶。
我落在她身旁的声响，让姑娘深信末日已经降临，因为她以为我就是那条龙；可残忍的獠牙迟迟没有合拢上来，她终于惊讶地抬起了眼睛。目光落到我身上时，她眼中浮现的神情难以形容；但她的感受，也未必比我自己的复杂分毫——因为那双望进我眼睛里来的、睁大的眼睛，属于美人黛安（Dian the Beautiful）。
「黛安！」我喊道，「黛安！感谢上帝，我来得及时。」
「是你？」她低声说，随即又把脸藏了起来；我的到来究竟是让她欢喜还是恼怒，我无从判断。

===Original===
Once more the dragon was sweeping toward us, and so rapidly that I had no time to unsling my bow. All that I could do was to snatch up a rock, and hurl it at the thing’s hideous face. Again my aim was true, and with a hiss of pain and rage the reptile wheeled once more and soared away.
Quickly I fitted an arrow now that I might be ready at the next attack, and as I did so I looked down at the girl, so that I surprised her in a surreptitious glance which she was stealing at me; but immediately, she again covered her face with her hands.
“Look at me, Dian,” I pleaded. “Are you not glad to see me?”

===Chinese===
巨龙再度朝我们扫来，快得我来不及摘下弓。我唯一来得及做的，就是抓起一块石头，朝那东西的丑脸狠狠掷去。这一次我的准头依然不差，那爬虫发出一声痛苦暴怒的嘶鸣，又一次转身，高飞远去。
我赶紧搭上一支箭，好迎接下一次攻击；搭箭时我低头看了姑娘一眼，正好逮住她偷偷瞟我的一眼；但她立刻又用双手把脸捂住了。
「看着我，黛安，」我恳求道，「见到我，你难道不高兴吗？」

===Original===
She looked straight into my eyes.
“I hate you,” she said, and then, as I was about to beg for a fair hearing she pointed over my shoulder. “The *thipdar* comes,” she said, and I turned again to meet the reptile.
So this was a *thipdar*. I might have known it. The cruel bloodhound of the Mahars. The long-extinct pterodactyl of the outer world. But this time I met it with a weapon it never had faced before. I had selected my longest arrow, and with all my strength had bent the bow until the very tip of the shaft rested upon the thumb of my left hand, and then as the great creature darted toward us I let drive straight for that tough breast.
Hissing like the escape valve of a steam engine, the mighty creature fell turning and twisting into the sea below, my arrow buried completely in its carcass. I turned toward the girl. She was looking past me. It was evident that she had seen the *thipdar* die.

===Chinese===
她直直地望进我的眼睛。
「我恨你。」她说。我正要请求她给我一个申辩的机会，她却指向我的肩后。「提普达（thipdar）来了。」她说。我转过身去，再度迎战那爬虫。
原来这就是提普达。我本该想到的。玛哈尔人（Mahars）的残忍猎犬。外层世界早已绝迹的翼手龙。不过这一次，我用一件它从未领教过的武器迎战。我选了最长的一支箭，使出全身力气把弓拉弯，直到箭杆尾端抵在我左手的拇指上；随后，趁那巨兽扑向我们之际，我照着它那坚韧的胸膛径直射去。
那庞然大物像蒸汽机的泄气阀一样嘶嘶作响，翻着滚、拧着身栽进下方的大海，我的箭整个没进它的躯壳。我转向姑娘。她的目光越过我，望向别处。显然，提普达毙命的经过她已经看见了。

===Original===
“Dian,” I said, “won’t you tell me that you are not sorry that I have found you?”
“I hate you,” was her only reply; but I imagined that there was less vehemence in it than before﻿—yet it might have been but my imagination.
“Why do you hate me, Dian?” I asked, but she did not answer me.
“What are you doing here?” I asked, “and what has happened to you since Hooja freed you from the Sagoths?”

===Chinese===
「黛安，」我说，「你难道不愿意告诉我，被我找到，你并不觉得懊恼吗？」
「我恨你。」这是她唯一的回答；不过我觉得，这话里的狠劲比先前淡了些——当然，也可能只是我的想象。
「你为什么恨我，黛安？」我问，她却不答我。
「你在这里做什么？」我问，「自从『狡猾鬼』胡贾（Hooja the Sly One）把你从萨戈思人（Sagoths）手里放走之后，你都经历了些什么？」

===Original===
At first I thought that she was going to ignore me entirely, but finally she thought better of it.
“I was again running away from Jubal the Ugly One,” she said. “After I escaped from the Sagoths I made my way alone back to my own land; but on account of Jubal I did not dare enter the villages or let any of my friends know that I had returned for fear that Jubal might find out. By watching for a long time I found that my brother had not yet returned, and so I continued to live in a cave beside a valley which my race seldom frequents, awaiting the time that he should come back and free me from Jubal.
“But at last one of Jubal’s hunters saw me as I was creeping toward my father’s cave to see if my brother had yet returned and he gave the alarm and Jubal set out after me. He has been pursuing me across many lands. He cannot be far behind me now. When he comes he will kill you and carry me back to his cave. He is a terrible man. I have gone as far as I can go, and there is no escape,” and she looked hopelessly up at the continuation of the ledge twenty feet above us.

===Chinese===
起初，我以为她打算完全不理我，但她终于还是改变了主意。
「我又一次在从『丑八怪』朱巴尔（Jubal the Ugly One）手里逃命，」她说，「我从萨戈思人那里逃脱之后，独自一人回到了自己的家园；但因为朱巴尔的缘故，我不敢进村子，也不敢让任何朋友知道我回来了，生怕走漏风声让朱巴尔得知。经过长时间的守望，我发现哥哥还没有回来，于是我继续住在一个山谷旁的洞穴里——那山谷我的族人很少涉足——只等他回来，把我从朱巴尔手里解救出去。」
「可到了后来，朱巴尔的一个猎人瞧见了我，当时我正偷偷摸向父亲的洞穴，想看看哥哥回来没有。他一发警报，朱巴尔便动身追我。他一路追着我穿过一片片土地。他此刻离我肯定不远了。他一到，就会杀了你，把我掳回他的洞穴。他是个可怕的男人。我已经逃到了能逃的尽头，再没有出路了。」说着，她绝望地仰头望向我们上方二十英尺高处岩架的延续部分。

===Original===
“But he shall not have me,” she suddenly cried, with great vehemence. “The sea is there”﻿—she pointed over the edge of the cliff﻿—“and the sea shall have me rather than Jubal.”
“But I have you now Dian,” I cried; “nor shall Jubal, nor any other have you, for you are mine,” and I seized her hand, nor did I lift it above her head and let it fall in token of release.
She had risen to her feet, and was looking straight into my eyes with level gaze.

===Chinese===
「可他休想得到我！」她突然激动地高喊。「大海就在那里」——她指向悬崖边缘之外——「宁可让大海得到我，也绝不让朱巴尔得到。」
「可现在你在我手里，黛安，」我喊道，「朱巴尔得不到你，任何别人也得不到你，因为你是我的。」我一把攥住她的手，却没有把它举过她的头顶再松开落下，以示放她自由。
她已站起身来，目光平平地直望进我的眼睛。

===Original===
“I do not believe you,” she said, “for if you meant it you would have done this when the others were present to witness it﻿—then I should truly have been your mate; now there is no one to see you do it, for you know that without witnesses your act does not bind you to me,” and she withdrew her hand from mine and turned away.
I tried to convince her that I was sincere, but she simply couldn’t forget the humiliation that I had put upon her on that other occasion.
“If you mean all that you say you will have ample chance to prove it,” she said, “if Jubal does not catch and kill you. I am in your power, and the treatment you accord me will be the best proof of your intentions toward me. I am not your mate, and again I tell you that I hate you, and that I should be glad if I never saw you again.”

===Chinese===
「我不信你，」她说，「因为你要是当真的，当初旁人在场目睹的时候，你就会这样做了——那时我才真正算得你的伴侣；如今没有一个人看见你这样做，因为你知道，没有见证人，你这一举并不能将你约束于我。」她把手从我手中抽回，转身而去。
我竭力让她相信我是真心实意，可她就是忘不了我上回加在她身上的羞辱。
「如果你说的全是真心话，你会有充分的机会证明，」她说，「只要朱巴尔没有把你抓住杀掉。我在你的掌握之中，你怎样待我，就是你对我的心意的最好证明。我不是你的伴侣，我再一次告诉你，我恨你，要是从此再见不着你，我才高兴呢。」

===Original===
Dian certainly was candid. There was no gainsaying that. In fact I found candor and directness to be quite a marked characteristic of the cave men of Pellucidar. Finally I suggested that we make some attempt to gain my cave, where we might escape the searching Jubal, for I am free to admit that I had no considerable desire to meet the formidable and ferocious creature, of whose mighty prowess Dian had told me when I first met her. He it was who, armed with a puny knife, had met and killed a cave bear in a hand-to-hand struggle. It was Jubal who could cast his spear entirely through the armored carcass of the *sadok* at fifty paces. It was he who had crushed the skull of a charging *dyryth* with a single blow of his war club. No, I was not pining to meet the Ugly One﻿—and it was quite certain that I should not go out and hunt for him; but the matter was taken out of my hands very quickly, as is often the way, and I did meet Jubal the Ugly One face to face.
This is how it happened. I had led Dian back along the ledge the way she had come, searching for a path that would lead us to the top of the cliff, for I knew that we could then cross over to the edge of my own little valley, where I felt certain we should find a means of ingress from the cliff top. As we proceeded along the ledge I gave Dian minute directions for finding my cave against the chance of something happening to me. I knew that she would be quite safely hidden away from pursuit once she gained the shelter of my lair, and the valley would afford her ample means of sustenance.

===Chinese===
黛安的坦率毋庸置疑，这一点没得辩驳。事实上，我发现坦率与直截了当，正是佩卢西达尔穴居人十分鲜明的一种特质。最后我提议，我们设法回我的洞穴去，在那里或许能躲开四下搜索的朱巴尔——我坦白承认，我并不怎么渴望会见那可怕而凶残的家伙；黛安初见我时，曾对我讲过他那惊人的武艺。正是他，凭着一把小小的刀，在徒手相搏中会猎并杀死过一头洞熊。正是朱巴尔，能在五十步开外把长矛整个掷穿萨多克的披甲躯体。也正是他，只用战棒一击，便砸碎了迎面冲来的德里思（dyryth）的头骨。不，我并不渴望与「丑八怪」会面——而且可以肯定，我也绝不会外出寻他；可这件事很快就不再由我做主了——世事往往如此——我终究同丑陋的朱巴尔面对面遇上了。
事情是这样发生的。我领着黛安沿着她来时的岩架往回走，寻找一条能带我们登上崖顶的路，因为我知道，上去之后便可以横穿到我那小山谷的边上，我确信在那儿能找到从崖顶下谷的入口。沿岩架走的时候，我把找到我洞穴的详细路径一一告诉了黛安，以防我有个三长两短。我知道，她一旦进了我的巢穴藏身，追兵便休想找到她，而山谷也足以为她提供充裕的生计。

===Original===
Also, I was very much piqued by her treatment of me. My heart was sad and heavy, and I wanted to make her feel badly by suggesting that something terrible might happen to me﻿—that I might, in fact, be killed. But it didn’t work worth a cent, at least as far as I could perceive. Dian simply shrugged those magnificent shoulders of hers, and murmured something to the effect that one was not rid of trouble so easily as that.
For a while I kept still. I was utterly squelched. And to think that I had twice protected her from attack﻿—the last time risking my life to save hers. It was incredible that even a daughter of the Stone Age could be so ungrateful﻿—so heartless; but maybe her heart partook of the qualities of her epoch.
Presently we found a rift in the cliff which had been widened and extended by the action of the water draining through it from the plateau above. It gave us a rather rough climb to the summit, but finally we stood upon the level mesa which stretched back for several miles to the mountain range. Behind us lay the broad inland sea, curving upward in the horizonless distance to merge into the blue of the sky, so that for all the world it looked as though the sea lapped back to arch completely over us and disappear beyond the distant mountains at our backs﻿—the weird and uncanny aspect of the seascapes of Pellucidar balk description.

===Chinese===
另外，她待我的态度也让我颇为恼火。我的心又悲又沉，便想让她也难受难受，于是故意说什么我可能大难临头——说不定还会送命。可这一招连一分钱的效果都没有，至少我看不出半点。黛安只是耸了耸她那出色的肩膀，咕哝了句大意如此的话：人的麻烦可不会这样就轻易摆脱。
我沉默了好一阵。我彻底蔫了。想想看，我可是两次保护她免遭毒手——最近一次更是舍了性命救她。就连石器时代的女儿家，居然也能这般忘恩负义——这般狠心，简直叫人难以置信；不过，也许她的心本就带着她那个时代的品性吧。
不久，我们在崖壁上找到一道裂缝，是上方高原排泄的流水经年冲刷，把它加宽延深的。沿它爬上崖顶的路颇为崎岖，但我们终于站上了平坦的山顶台地，它向后绵延数英里，直抵山脉。我们身后横着广阔的内海，在没有地平线的远方向上弯曲，融入天空的湛蓝，看上去千真万确就像海水向后翻卷，在我们头顶完全拱成穹隆，隐没到背后远处的群山之外——佩卢西达尔海景那怪异诡谲的气象，非笔墨所能形容。

===Original===
At our right lay a dense forest, but to the left the country was open and clear to the plateau’s farther verge. It was in this direction that our way led, and we had turned to resume our journey when Dian touched my arm. I turned to her, thinking that she was about to make peace overtures; but I was mistaken.
“Jubal,” she said, and nodded toward the forest.
I looked, and there, emerging from the dense wood, came a perfect whale of a man. He must have been seven feet tall, and proportioned accordingly. He still was too far off to distinguish his features.
“Run,” I said to Dian. “I can engage him until you get a good start. Maybe I can hold him until you have gotten entirely away,” and then, without a backward glance, I advanced to meet the Ugly One. I had hoped that Dian would have a kind word to say to me before she went, for she must have known that I was going to my death for her sake; but she never even so much as bid me goodbye, and it was with a heavy heart that I strode through the flower-bespangled grass to my doom.

===Chinese===
我们右边是一片茂密的森林，左边却是开阔地，一直通透到高原的远缘。我们的路正通向那边，我们已转身准备继续赶路，黛安忽然碰了碰我的手臂。我转向她，以为她要递出讲和的橄榄枝；可我猜错了。
「朱巴尔。」她说着，朝森林点了点头。
我望去，只见那密林边缘正走出一个鲸鱼般魁伟的汉子。他准有七英尺高，身材也按这个头相应地魁伟。距离还太远，看不清他的相貌。
「快跑，」我对黛安说，「我可以缠住他，让你先跑出好一段路。说不定我能拖住他，直到你跑得无影无踪。」说罢，我头也不回，迎着「丑八怪」走了上去。我原本指望黛安临走前会对我说句好话，因为她一定知道，我是为了她去赴死的；可她竟然连一句道别都没有。我怀着沉重的心情，大步穿过缀满花朵的草地，走向我的厄运。

===Original===
When I had come close enough to Jubal to distinguish his features I understood how it was that he had earned the sobriquet of Ugly One. Apparently some fearful beast had ripped away one entire side of his face. The eye was gone, the nose, and all the flesh, so that his jaws and all his teeth were exposed and grinning through the horrible scar.
Formerly he may have been as good to look upon as the others of his handsome race, and it may be that the terrible result of this encounter had tended to sour an already strong and brutal character. However this may be it is quite certain that he was not a pretty sight, and now that his features, or what remained of them, were distorted in rage at the sight of Dian with another male, he was indeed most terrible to see﻿—and much more terrible to meet.
He had broken into a run now, and as he advanced he raised his mighty spear, while I halted and fitting an arrow to my bow took as steady aim as I could. I was somewhat longer than usual, for I must confess that the sight of this awful man had wrought upon my nerves to such an extent that my knees were anything but steady. What chance had I against this mighty warrior for whom even the fiercest cave bear had no terrors! Could I hope to best one who slaughtered the *sadok* and *dyryth* singlehanded! I shuddered; but, in fairness to myself, my fear was more for Dian than for my own fate.

===Chinese===
等我离朱巴尔近得能辨清相貌，我才明白他是怎么挣下「丑八怪」这个诨名的。显然，某只可怕的野兽曾撕去他整整半边脸。眼睛没了，鼻子没了，血肉全没了，于是他的下颌和满口牙齿尽数裸露，在那道可怕的疤痕后面龇牙咧嘴。
从前，他也许同他那英俊种族里的其他人一样耐看，也许这场遭遇战的可怕后果，让他本已强横残暴的性子愈发乖戾。不管怎样，可以肯定他绝不是个好看的景象；而此刻，他的五官——或者说残余的五官——一见黛安跟另一个男人在一起，便因狂怒而扭曲，他看上去实在骇人至极——真要交手，就更加可怕了。
这时他已撒腿飞奔，一边进逼一边举起他那根沉重的长矛。我站定脚，搭箭上弓，尽力稳住准头。我瞄准的工夫比平常长了不少，因为我必须承认，这个可怕家伙的模样把我的神经折腾得不轻，我的膝盖实在说不上稳当。面对连最凶猛的洞熊都吓不倒的勇士，我有什么胜算！我能指望胜过单枪匹马屠戮萨多克和德里思的人吗！我不寒而栗；不过，替自己说句公道话，我怕的多半是黛安，而不是自己的命运。

===Original===
And then the great brute launched his massive stone-tipped spear, and I raised my shield to break the force of its terrific velocity. The impact hurled me to my knees, but the shield had deflected the missile and I was unscathed. Jubal was rushing upon me now with the only remaining weapon that he carried﻿—a murderous-looking knife. He was too close for a careful bowshot, but I let drive at him as he came, without taking aim. My arrow pierced the fleshy part of his thigh, inflicting a painful but not disabling wound. And then he was upon me.
My agility saved me for the instant. I ducked beneath his raised arm, and when he wheeled to come at me again he found a sword’s point in his face. And a moment later he felt an inch or two of it in the muscles of his knife arm, so that thereafter he went more warily.
It was a duel of strategy now﻿—the great, hairy man maneuvering to get inside my guard where he could bring those giant thews to play, while my wits were directed to the task of keeping him at arm’s length. Thrice he rushed me, and thrice I caught his knife blow upon my shield. Each time my sword found his body﻿—once penetrating to his lung. He was covered with blood by this time, and the internal hemorrhage induced paroxysms of coughing that brought the red stream through the hideous mouth and nose, covering his face and breast with bloody froth. He was a most unlovely spectacle, but he was far from dead.

===Chinese===
接着，那大块头掷出了他沉重的石尖长矛，我举起盾牌，去卸掉那雷霆一掷的力道。冲力把我掀翻跪地，但盾牌偏转了飞矛，我毫发无损。朱巴尔此刻挥着他随身仅剩的武器——一把凶相毕露的刀——朝我猛冲过来。距离太近，已容不得从容放箭，但他冲上来时，我还是不加瞄准地照他放了一箭。箭射穿了他大腿的肉，伤得疼，却不妨碍行动。转眼间，他已经扑到我身上。
我的敏捷救了我一时。我一低头闪进他高举的手臂之下，等他转身再扑，一柄剑的剑尖已经指在他脸前。片刻之后，他又尝到一两英寸剑锋没入持刀手臂肌肉的滋味，从此他的动作便谨慎多了。
眼下成了一场斗智的决斗——那高大多毛的汉子使尽手段要钻进我的防守圈内，好叫他那一身巨力得以施展；而我的心智则全用在如何同他保持一臂开外。他三次猛扑，我三次都用盾牌架开他的刀。每一回合，我的剑都找上他的身体——有一次直透肺腑。这时他已浑身是血，内出血引发阵阵剧咳，把红色的血流从那张可怕的嘴和鼻子里咳出来，血沫糊满了他的脸和胸口。他那模样要多难看有多难看，可他离死还远得很。

===Original===
As the duel continued I began to gain confidence, for, to be perfectly candid, I had not expected to survive the first rush of that monstrous engine of ungoverned rage and hatred. And I think that Jubal, from utter contempt of me, began to change to a feeling of respect, and then in his primitive mind there evidently loomed the thought that perhaps at last he had met his master, and was facing his end.
At any rate it is only upon this hypothesis that I can account for his next act, which was in the nature of a last resort﻿—a sort of forlorn hope, which could only have been born of the belief that if he did not kill me quickly I should kill him. It happened on the occasion of his fourth charge, when, instead of striking at me with his knife, he dropped that weapon, and seizing my sword blade in both his hands wrenched the weapon from my grasp as easily as from a babe.
Flinging it far to one side he stood motionless for just an instant glaring into my face with such a horrid leer of malignant triumph as to almost unnerve me﻿—then he sprang for me with his bare hands. But it was Jubal’s day to learn new methods of warfare. For the first time he had seen a bow and arrows, never before that duel had he beheld a sword, and now he learned what a man who knows may do with his bare fists.

===Chinese===
决斗继续下去，我渐渐有了信心，因为坦白得彻底一点说，我本没指望能熬过那台狂怒与仇恨驱动的可怕机器的头一轮冲击。我想，朱巴尔对我的彻底轻蔑，也开始转变为一种敬意，随后，在他那原始的头脑里，显然浮现出一个念头：也许他终于遇到了压得住自己的人，正在面对自己的末日。
无论如何，只有依着这个假设，我才能解释他接下来的举动——那是孤注一掷的最后手段，一种近乎绝望的冒险，只能诞生于这样的信念：若不赶快杀了我，我便会杀了他。事情出在他第四次冲锋之时，他不用刀来砍我，反而丢开那件武器，双手攥住我的剑刃，像从一个婴儿手里夺东西一样轻易，把武器从我掌握中拧了出去。
他把剑远远甩到一旁，一动不动地站了片刻，用一种恶毒得意到极点的狞笑瞪着我的脸，几乎瓦解了我的胆气——随后他赤手空拳向我扑来。可这一天，注定是朱巴尔学习新战法的日子。他头一回见识了弓箭，那场决斗之前又从未见过剑，而现在，他领教了一个有本事的人，赤手空拳能干出什么来。

===Original===
As he came for me, like a great bear, I ducked again beneath his outstretched arm, and as I came up planted as clean a blow upon his jaw as ever you have seen. Down went that great mountain of flesh sprawling upon the ground. He was so surprised and dazed that he lay there for several seconds before he made any attempt to rise, and I stood over him with another dose ready when he should gain his knees.
Up he came at last, almost roaring in his rage and mortification; but he didn’t stay up﻿—I let him have a left fair on the point of the jaw that sent him tumbling over on his back. By this time I think Jubal had gone mad with hate, for no sane man would have come back for more as many times as he did. Time after time I bowled him over as fast as he could stagger up, until toward the last he lay longer on the ground between blows, and each time came up weaker than before.
He was bleeding very profusely now from the wound in his lungs, and presently a terrific blow over the heart sent him reeling heavily to the ground, where he lay very still, and somehow I knew at once that Jubal the Ugly One would never get up again. But even as I looked upon that massive body lying there so grim and terrible in death, I could not believe that I, single-handed, had bested this slayer of fearful beasts﻿—this gigantic ogre of the Stone Age.

===Chinese===
他像一头大熊般朝我扑来，我又一次低头闪过他张开的手臂，起身时，在他下巴上结结实实送上了一记干净利落的重拳，那样漂亮的拳，你未必亲眼见过。那座肉山轰然倒塌，四仰八叉瘫在地上。他又惊又懵，躺了好几秒才想起身，我便立在他上方，等他撑起膝盖，好再补他一剂。
他终于爬了起来，恼羞成怒，几乎要咆哮出声；可他没能站多久——我对准他下巴尖公平地赏了一记左拳，把他打得仰面翻倒。到这时，我想朱巴尔已被仇恨逼疯了，因为任何神志清醒的人，都不会像他那样一次次回来讨打。他刚摇摇晃晃爬起，我便把他再次打翻，如此周而复始，直到后来，他两拳之间躺在地上的时间越来越久，每一次爬起来都比前一次更加虚弱。
这时，他肺部的伤口血流如注，不久，对准心口的一记可怕重拳把他打得踉跄着重重倒地，躺在那里一动不动。不知怎的，我当下便明白，丑八怪朱巴尔再也起不来了。可即便望着那具庞大的躯体躺在那里，死状如此狰狞可怖，我仍难以相信，我凭一己之力，竟战胜了这位屠戮猛兽的杀手——这石器时代的巨人妖魔。

===Original===
Picking up my sword I leaned upon it, looking down on the dead body of my foeman, and as I thought of the battle I had just fought and won a great idea was born in my brain﻿—the outcome of this and the suggestion that Perry had made within the city of Phutra. If skill and science could render a comparative pygmy the master of this mighty brute, what could not the brute’s fellows accomplish with the same skill and science. Why all Pellucidar would be at their feet﻿—and I would be their king and Dian their queen.
Dian! A little wave of doubt swept over me. It was quite within the possibilities of Dian to look down upon me even were I king. She was quite the most superior person I ever had met﻿—with the most convincing way of letting you know that she was superior. Well, I could go to the cave, and tell her that I had killed Jubal, and then she might feel more kindly toward me, since I had freed her of her tormentor. I hoped that she had found the cave easily﻿—it would be terrible had I lost her again, and I turned to gather up my shield and bow to hurry after her, when to my astonishment I found her standing not ten paces behind me.
“Girl!” I cried, “what are you doing here? I thought that you had gone to the cave, as I told you to do.”

===Chinese===
我拾起剑，拄着它俯视仇敌的尸体。想到方才这场恶战——我打赢了——一个宏大的念头在我脑中诞生了：它是这场决斗的结局，再加上佩里（Perry）在富特拉（Phutra）城内向我说过的那项提议。倘若技艺与科学能叫一个相形见绌的矮子制服这庞大的蛮汉，那么凭着同样的技艺与科学，这蛮汉的同胞们又何事不可成就。到那时，整个佩卢西达尔都将拜倒在他们脚下——而我将是他们的国王，黛安则是他们的王后。
黛安！一阵疑虑的细浪掠过我的心头。哪怕我当上国王，黛安也完全有可能照样瞧不起我。她着实是我平生所见最自视甚高的人——而且自有一套让你心服口服地明白她高人一等的本事。罢了，我可以回洞穴去，告诉她我杀了朱巴尔；既然我替她除掉了折磨她的人，她对我的态度或许会好上一些。但愿她顺顺当当地找到了洞穴——要是再把她丢了，那可就糟了。我转身要拾起盾牌和弓赶紧去追她，却惊愕地发现，她就站在我身后不到十步的地方。
「姑娘！」我喊道，「你在这里做什么？我以为你照我的吩咐，到洞穴去了呢。」

===Original===
Up went her head, and the look that she gave me took all the majesty out of me, and left me feeling more like the palace janitor﻿—if palaces have janitors.
“As you told me to do!” she cried, stamping her little foot. “I do as I please. I am the daughter of a king, and furthermore, I hate you.”
I was dumbfounded﻿—this was my thanks for saving her from Jubal! I turned and looked at the corpse. “May be that I saved you from a worse fate, old man,” I said, but I guess it was lost on Dian, for she never seemed to notice it at all.

===Chinese===
她头一扬，赏我的那个眼神，登时把我满身的威仪剥了个精光，只让我觉得自己活像宫殿里的看门人——倘若宫殿也配有看门人的话。
「照你的吩咐！」她跺着小脚喊道，「我爱怎么做就怎么做。我是国王的女儿，而且，我还是恨你。」
我目瞪口呆——这便是我从朱巴尔手里救出她的谢礼！我转过身看看那具尸体。「老家伙，说不定我把你从更坏的命运里救出来了呢。」我说。不过我猜这话对黛安全然白说，她似乎压根儿没有留意。

===Original===
“Let us go to my cave,” I said, “I am tired and hungry.”
She followed along a pace behind me, neither of us speaking. I was too angry, and she evidently didn’t care to converse with the lower orders. I was mad all the way through, as I had certainly felt that at least a word of thanks should have rewarded me, for I knew that even by her own standards, I must have done a very wonderful thing to have killed the redoubtable Jubal in a hand-to-hand encounter.

===Chinese===
「去我的洞穴吧，」我说，「我又累又饿。」
她跟在我身后一步之遥的地方，两人都不说话。我气得说不出话，她呢，显然不屑同下等人交谈。我从头到脚一肚子火，因为我觉得，至少也该有一句道谢来酬报我——我知道，就算按她自己的标准，能在徒手相搏中杀死威名赫赫的朱巴尔，也必定是一件了不起的壮举。

===Original===
We had no difficulty in finding my lair, and then I went down into the valley and bowled over a small antelope, which I dragged up the steep ascent to the ledge before the door. Here we ate in silence. Occasionally I glanced at her, thinking that the sight of her tearing at raw flesh with her hands and teeth like some wild animal would cause a revulsion of my sentiments toward her; but to my surprise I found that she ate quite as daintily as the most civilized woman of my acquaintance, and finally I found myself gazing in foolish rapture at the beauties of her strong, white teeth. Such is love.
After our repast we went down to the river together and bathed our hands and faces, and then after drinking our fill went back to the cave. Without a word I crawled into the farthest corner and, curling up, was soon asleep.

===Chinese===
我们毫不费力就找到了我的巢穴。随后我下到山谷，打翻一只小羚羊，拖上陡坡，弄到门前的那块岩台上。我们就在这里默默用了餐。我不时瞥她一眼，心想看她像野兽似的用手和牙齿撕扯生肉，总会叫我心中生出反感；可出乎意料，我发现她吃得竟同我认识的最文明的女子一样斯文，到后来，我竟痴痴呆呆地望着她那口结实白牙的美丽出了神。爱情便是如此。
饭后，我们一同下到河边，洗净了手脸，又喝足了水，回到洞穴。我一言不发地爬进最里面的角落，蜷起身子，很快便睡着了。

===Original===
When I awoke I found Dian sitting in the doorway looking out across the valley. As I came out she moved to one side to let me pass, but she had no word for me. I wanted to hate her, but I couldn’t. Every time I looked at her something came up in my throat, so that I nearly choked. I had never been in love before, but I did not need any aid in diagnosing my case﻿—I certainly had it and had it bad. God, how I loved that beautiful, disdainful, tantalizing, prehistoric girl!
After we had eaten again I asked Dian if she intended returning to her tribe now that Jubal was dead, but she shook her head sadly, and said that she did not dare, for there was still Jubal’s brother to be considered﻿—his oldest brother.
“What has he to do with it?” I asked. “Does he too want you, or has the option on you become a family heirloom, to be passed on down from generation to generation?”

===Chinese===
醒来时，我见黛安坐在洞口，望着外面的山谷。我出来时，她挪到一旁让我过去，却没有一句话给我。我想恨她，却恨不起来。每次望她，总有什么东西涌上我的喉咙，噎得我几乎透不过气。我从前从未恋爱过，可诊断自己的病症，用不着旁人帮忙——我确实害上了这病，而且病得不轻。上帝啊，我多么爱这个美丽、冷傲、撩人的史前姑娘！
我们再次吃过饭，我问黛安，如今朱巴尔已死，她是否打算回部族去。她悲哀地摇了摇头，说不敢回去，因为还得顾及朱巴尔的兄弟——他的大哥。
「他跟这事有什么相干？」我问，「难道他也想要你？还是说，对你的这项优先权已经成了传家宝，要一代一代传下去？」

===Original===
She was not quite sure as to what I meant.
“It is probable,” she said, “that they all will want revenge for the death of Jubal﻿—there are seven of them﻿—seven terrible men. Someone may have to kill them all, if I am to return to my people.”
It began to look as though I had assumed a contract much too large for me﻿—about seven sizes, in fact.

===Chinese===
她不大明白我的意思。
「多半会这样，」她说，「他们个个都想为朱巴尔的死报仇——他们共有七个——七个可怕的男人。倘若我要回到族人身边，恐怕得有人把他们统统杀掉。」
看来，我揽下的这份合同对我而言未免太大了些——事实上，大了差不多七号。

===Original===
“Had Jubal any cousins?” I asked. It was just as well to know the worst at once.
“Yes,” replied Dian, “but they don’t count﻿—they all have mates. Jubal’s brothers have no mates because Jubal could get none for himself. He was so ugly that women ran away from him﻿—some have even thrown themselves from the cliffs of Amoz into the Darel Az rather than mate with the Ugly One.”

===Chinese===
「朱巴尔有堂兄弟吗？」我问。还是趁早把最坏的情况打听清楚为妙。
「有，」黛安答道，「但算不上数——他们全都有伴侣了。朱巴尔的兄弟们没有伴侣，是因为朱巴尔自己一个也弄不到。他丑得女人们见了他就逃——有的甚至宁可从阿莫兹（Amoz）的悬崖跳进达雷尔阿兹（Darel Az），也不愿同『丑八怪』结为伴侣。」

===Original===
“But what had that to do with his brothers?” I asked.
“I forget that you are not of Pellucidar,” said Dian, with a look of pity mixed with contempt, and the contempt seemed to be laid on a little thicker than the circumstance warranted﻿—as though to make quite certain that I shouldn’t overlook it. “You see,” she continued, “a younger brother may not take a mate until all his older brothers have done so, unless the older brother waives his prerogative, which Jubal would not do, knowing that as long as he kept them single they would be all the keener in aiding him to secure a mate.”

===Chinese===
「可那跟他的兄弟们有什么相干？」我问。
「我忘了你不是佩卢西达尔人，」黛安说，神情里怜悯中掺着轻蔑，而且那轻蔑似乎涂得比实情厚了些——像是唯恐我看不真切似的。「你要知道，」她接着说，「弟弟们在所有哥哥都成亲之前，不得自行择偶，除非哥哥放弃自己的优先权。朱巴尔绝不肯放弃，因为他心里明白，只要让弟弟们打着光棍，他们帮他夺取伴侣时就格外卖力。」

===Original===
Noticing that Dian was becoming more communicative I began to entertain hopes that she might be warming up toward me a bit, although upon what slender thread I hung my hopes I soon discovered.
“As you dare not return to Amoz,” I ventured, “what is to become of you since you cannot be happy here with me, hating me as you do?”
“I shall have to put up with you,” she replied coldly, “until you see fit to go elsewhere and leave me in peace, then I shall get along very well alone.”

===Chinese===
见黛安的话渐渐多了起来，我开始抱着希望，想她或许对我热络了一点，虽然我那希望悬在一根多么纤细的线上，我很快便领教了。
「你既然不敢回阿莫兹，」我试探着说，「又既然这样恨我，同我一起在这里不会快活，那你日后怎么办呢？」
「我只好将就着容忍你，」她冷冷地答道，「等你哪天高兴去别处，还我个清静，那我一个人自会过得很好。」

===Original===
I looked at her in utter amazement. It seemed incredible that even a prehistoric woman could be so cold and heartless and ungrateful. Then I arose.
“I shall leave you *now*,” I said haughtily, “I have had quite enough of your ingratitude and your insults,” and then I turned and strode majestically down toward the valley. I had taken a hundred steps in absolute silence, and then Dian spoke.
“I hate you!” she shouted, and her voice broke﻿—in rage, I thought.

===Chinese===
我全然惊愕地望着她。一个史前女人居然能如此冷酷、如此无情、如此忘恩负义，简直不可思议。于是我站起身来。
「我这*就*走，」我傲然说道，「你的忘恩负义和你的侮辱，我受够了。」说罢我转过身，威严地大步朝山谷走去。我一言不发地走了一百步，黛安开口了。
「我恨你！」她大喊，声音都劈了——我想，是气劈的。

===Original===
I was absolutely miserable, but I hadn’t gone too far when I began to realize that I couldn’t leave her alone there without protection, to hunt her own food amid the dangers of that savage world. She might hate me, and revile me, and heap indignity after indignity upon me, as she already had, until I should have hated her; but the pitiful fact remained that I loved her, and I couldn’t leave her there alone.
The more I thought about it the madder I got, so that by the time I reached the valley I was furious, and the result of it was that I turned right around and went up that cliff again as fast as I had come down. I saw that Dian had left the ledge and gone within the cave, but I bolted right in after her. She was lying upon her face on the pile of grasses I had gathered for her bed. When she heard me enter she sprang to her feet like a tigress.
“I hate you!” she cried.

===Chinese===
我悲惨透了，可没走多远，我便开始明白：我不能把她一个人撂在那里，无人保护，任她在那蛮荒世界的重重危险中自行觅食。她尽可以恨我、咒我、把屈辱一件件堆到我头上——她已经这样做了，做到我本该恨她的地步；可那令人心酸的事实依旧摆在那里：我爱她，我不能把她独自留在那里。
这件事越想越有气，走到谷底时，我已经怒火中烧，结果就是我立刻掉转头，以冲下来时同样快的速度重新爬上了那道崖。我看见黛安已离开岩台进了洞，可我照样紧跟着冲了进去。她正伏在我为她铺床搜集的那堆干草上。听见我进洞，她像母老虎般一跃而起。
「我恨你！」她喊道。

===Original===
Coming from the brilliant light of the noonday sun into the semidarkness of the cave I could not see her features, and I was rather glad, for I disliked to think of the hate that I should have read there.
I never said a word to her at first. I just strode across the cave and grasped her by the wrists, and when she struggled, I put my arm around her so as to pinion her hands to her sides. She fought like a tigress, but I took my free hand and pushed her head back﻿—I imagine that I had suddenly turned brute, that I had gone back a thousand million years, and was again a veritable cave man taking my mate by force﻿—and then I kissed that beautiful mouth again and again.
“Dian,” I cried, shaking her roughly, “I love you. Can’t you understand that I love you? That I love you better than all else in this world or my own? That I am going to have you? That love like mine cannot be denied?”

===Chinese===
从正午灿烂的日光下走进洞穴的半明半暗，我看不清她的眉目，我倒觉得庆幸，因为我不愿去想那上面本会读出怎样的恨意。
起初我对她一句话也没说。我只是大步穿过洞穴，攥住她的双腕；她挣扎，我便用一条胳膊箍住她，把她的双手按在身侧。她像母老虎一样搏斗，我却腾出空着的那只手，把她的头向后仰去——我想，那一刻我准是突然变回了野兽，倒退了十亿年，重新成了一个名副其实的穴居人，正以强力夺取自己的伴侣——随后，我一遍又一遍地吻那张美丽的嘴。
「黛安，」我一边粗鲁地摇着她，一边喊道，「我爱你。你难道不明白我爱你？不明白我爱你胜过这个世界或我那个世界里的一切？不明白我一定要得到你？不明白我这样的爱情，是拒绝不掉的吗？」

===Original===
I noticed that she lay very still in my arms now, and as my eyes became accustomed to the light I saw that she was smiling﻿—a very contented, happy smile. I was thunderstruck. Then I realized that, very gently, she was trying to disengage her arms, and I loosened my grip upon them so that she could do so. Slowly they came up and stole about my neck, and then she drew my lips down to hers once more and held them there for a long time. At last she spoke.
“Why didn’t you do this at first, David? I have been waiting so long.”
“What!” I cried. “You said that you hated me!”

===Chinese===
我察觉，她此刻在我怀里一动不动；等我的眼睛适应了光线，我看见她在微笑——一个非常满足、非常幸福的微笑。我如遭雷击。接着我意识到，她正非常轻柔地想抽回双臂，我便松开钳制，由她抽动。那双手臂慢慢抬起，悄悄环上我的脖颈；随后她把我的唇拉向她的唇，又一次久久贴在那里。终于，她开口了。
「你为什么不一开始就这样做，戴维（David）？我等了这么久。」
「什么！」我喊道，「你不是说过你恨我吗！」

===Original===
“Did you expect me to run into your arms, and say that I loved you before I knew that you loved me?” she asked.
“But I have told you right along that I love you,” I said. “Love speaks in acts,” she replied. “You could have made your mouth say what you wished it to say, but just now when you came and took me in your arms your heart spoke to mine in the language that a woman’s heart understands. What a silly man you are, David.”
“Then you haven’t hated me at all, Dian?” I asked.

===Chinese===
「难道你指望我扑进你的怀抱，在还不知道你爱不爱我之前，就说自己爱你？」她问。
「可我一直都在对你说，我爱你呀。」我说。「爱要用行动说话。」她答道，「你本可以让嘴巴说尽你想说的话，可方才你来把我搂进怀里，你的心便用女人的心听得懂的语言，对我的心说话了。你这个人多傻呀，戴维。」
「那么，你其实从来没有恨过我，黛安？」我问。

===Original===
“I have loved you always,” she whispered, “from the first moment that I saw you, although I did not know it until that time you struck down Hooja the Sly One, and then spurned me.”
“But I didn’t spurn you, dear,” I cried. “I didn’t know your ways﻿—I doubt if I do now. It seems incredible that you could have reviled me so, and yet have cared for me all the time.”
“You might have known,” she said, “when I did not run away from you that it was not hate which chained me to you. While you were battling with Jubal, I could have run to the edge of the forest, and when I learned the outcome of the combat it would have been a simple thing to have eluded you and returned to my own people.”

===Chinese===
「我一直爱着你，」她低声说，「从见到你的第一眼起就是，虽然我自己并不知道，直到你那次打倒『狡猾鬼』胡贾、随即又冷冷撇下我的时候，我才明白过来。」
「可我并没有撇下你呀，亲爱的，」我喊道，「我不懂你们的规矩——就是现在，我也未必真懂。你居然那样辱骂我，心里却一直恋着我，想想真叫人难以置信。」
「你本该想到的，」她说，「见我没有从你身边逃开，拴住我的就不是恨。你同朱巴尔搏斗的时候，我本可以跑到森林边上，等我知道了搏斗的结果，要避开你、回到自己族人那里去，本是轻而易举的事。」

===Original===
“But Jubal’s brothers﻿—and cousins﻿—” I reminded her, “how about them?”
She smiled, and hid her face on my shoulder.
“I had to tell you *something*, David,” she whispered. “I must needs have *some* excuse for remaining near you.”

===Chinese===
「可朱巴尔的兄弟们——还有堂兄弟们——」我提醒她，「他们怎么办？」
她嫣然一笑，把脸藏进我的肩膀。
「我总得跟你说*点什么*呀，戴维，」她低声说，「我总得找个*什么*借口，才能待在你身边。」

===Original===
“You little sinner!” I exclaimed. “And you have caused me all this anguish for nothing!”
“I have suffered even more,” she answered simply, “for I thought that you did not love me, and I was helpless. I couldn’t come to you and demand that my love be returned, as you have just come to me. Just now when you went away hope went with you. I was wretched, terrified, miserable, and my heart was breaking. I wept, and I have not done that before since my mother died,” and now I saw that there was the moisture of tears about her eyes. It was near to making me cry myself when I thought of all that poor child had been through. Motherless and unprotected; hunted across a savage, primeval world by that hideous brute of a man; exposed to the attacks of the countless fearsome denizens of its mountains, its plains, and its jungles﻿—it was a miracle that she had survived it all.
To me it was a revelation of the things my early forebears must have endured that the human race of the outer crust might survive. It made me very proud to think that I had won the love of such a woman. Of course she couldn’t read or write; there was nothing cultured or refined about her as you judge culture and refinement; but she was the essence of all that is best in woman, for she was good, and brave, and noble, and virtuous. And she was all these things in spite of the fact that their observance entailed suffering and danger and possible death.

===Chinese===
「你个小罪人！」我嚷道，「你害我白白受了这一场煎熬！」
「我受的苦只多不少，」她简单地答道，「因为我以为你不爱我，而我毫无办法。我可不能像你方才那样，跑到你面前去，要求你回报我的爱。方才你一走，希望也跟着你走了。我又凄惶，又惊惧，又痛苦，我的心都要碎了。我哭了——自打母亲死后，我还从来没有哭过。」这时我才看见，她的眼眶里还噙着泪痕。想到这可怜的姑娘经历过的一切，我自己也险些落泪。无母无靠，被那个人面兽心的丑类追得穿越蛮荒的原始世界亡命奔逃，暴露在山岭、平原和丛林中无数可怕生灵的攻击之下——她竟能全部熬过来，实在是个奇迹。
在我看来，这桩事揭示了我的远古祖先们为了外层地壳的人类得以延续，必定经受过的种种磨难。想到我赢得了这样一个女人的爱情，我自豪得很。当然，她不识字，也不会写字；若照你们评判教养与文雅的标准，她身上没有半点教养或文雅可言；可她是女人一切至善品性的精华，因为她善良、勇敢、高尚、贞洁。而她之所以兼有这些品德，全然不顾恪守它们意味着受苦、涉险、乃至可能的死亡。

===Original===
How much easier it would have been to have gone to Jubal in the first place! She would have been his lawful mate. She would have been queen in her own land﻿—and it meant just as much to the cave woman to be a queen in the Stone Age as it does to the woman of today to be a queen now; it’s all comparative glory any way you look at it, and if there were only half-naked savages on the outer crust today, you’d find that it would be considerable glory to be the wife of a Dahomey chief.
I couldn’t help but compare Dian’s action with that of a splendid young woman I had known in New York﻿—I mean splendid to look at and to talk to. She had been head over heels in love with a chum of mine﻿—a clean, manly chap﻿—but she had married a broken-down, disreputable old debauchee because he was a count in some dinky little European principality that was not even accorded a distinctive color by Rand McNally.

===Chinese===
当初若是干脆投奔朱巴尔，日子会好过多少啊！她本可以成为他名正言顺的伴侣。她本可以在自己的家园做王后——对石器时代的穴居女人来说，在那时做王后的分量，同今天的女人如今做王后一模一样；无论从哪个角度看，光荣都是相对而言的；倘若今天的外层地壳上只有些半裸的野人，你就会发现，做达荷美（Dahomey）酋长的妻子，也是相当了不起的荣耀。
我禁不住把黛安的所为，同我在纽约认识的一位出色姑娘的所为相比——我指的是模样出色、谈吐出色。她曾同我的一个好友爱得神魂颠倒——那是个干净爽利、有男子气的小伙子——可她到头来嫁给了一个颓唐潦倒、声名狼藉的老色鬼，只因为他是某个欧洲小得可怜的公国里的伯爵，那国家小得连兰德·麦克纳利（Rand McNally）的地图册都不曾给它单标一种颜色。

===Original===
Yes, I was mighty proud of Dian.
After a time we decided to set out for Sari, as I was anxious to see Perry, and to know that all was right with him. I had told Dian about our plan of emancipating the human race of Pellucidar, and she was fairly wild over it. She said that if Dacor, her brother, would only return he could easily be king of Amoz, and that then he and Ghak could form an alliance. That would give us a flying start, for the Sarians and the Amozites were both very powerful tribes. Once they had been armed with swords, and bows and arrows, and trained in their use we were confident that they could overcome any tribe that seemed disinclined to join the great army of federated states with which we were planning to march upon the Mahars.

===Chinese===
是啊，我为黛安感到无比骄傲。
过了一阵，我们决定动身去萨里，因为我急于见到佩里，想知道他一切安好。我把我们解放佩卢西达尔人类的计划讲给黛安听，她简直为之心醉神迷。她说，只要她的哥哥达科尔（Dacor the Strong One）肯回来，就轻而易举能做阿莫兹之王，到那时，他可以同格哈克（Ghak）结成同盟。这将给我们一个漂亮的起点，因为萨里人和阿莫兹人都是很强大的部落。一旦用剑、弓和箭把他们武装起来，并加以训练，我们便有把握：任何不大愿意加入联邦国家大军的部落，他们都能将其制服——我们正计划率领这支大军去讨伐玛哈尔人。

===Original===
I explained the various destructive engines of war which Perry and I could construct after a little experimentation﻿—gunpowder, rifles, cannon, and the like, and Dian would clap her hands, and throw her arms about my neck, and tell me what a wonderful thing I was. She was beginning to think that I was omnipotent although I really hadn’t done anything but talk﻿—but that is the way with women when they love. Perry used to say that if a fellow was one-tenth as remarkable as his wife or mother thought him, he would have the world by the tail with a downhill drag.
The first time we started for Sari I stepped into a nest of poisonous vipers before we reached the valley. A little fellow stung me on the ankle, and Dian made me come back to the cave. She said that I mustn’t exercise, or it might prove fatal﻿—if it had been a full-grown snake that struck me she said, I wouldn’t have moved a single pace from the nest﻿—I’d have died in my tracks, so virulent is the poison. As it was I must have been laid up for quite a while, though Dian’s poultices of herbs and leaves finally reduced the swelling and drew out the poison.
The episode proved most fortunate, however, as it gave me an idea which added a thousandfold to the value of my arrows as missiles of offense and defense. As soon as I was able to be about again, I sought out some adult vipers of the species which had stung me, and having killed them, I extracted their virus, smearing it upon the tips of several arrows. Later I shot a *hyaenodon* with one of these, and though my arrow inflicted but a superficial flesh wound the beast crumpled in death almost immediately after he was hit.
We now set out once more for the land of the Sarians, and it was with feelings of sincere regret that we bade goodbye to our beautiful Garden of Eden, in the comparative peace and harmony of which we had lived the happiest moments of our lives. How long we had been there I did not know, for as I have told you, time had ceased to exist for me beneath that eternal noonday sun﻿—it may have been an hour, or a month of earthly time; I do not know.

===Chinese===
我一一讲解了佩里和我稍加试验便能造出的种种毁灭性战争利器——火药、步枪、大炮等等；黛安听了便拍起手来，搂住我的脖子，夸我是多了不起的人物。她开始以为我无所不能了，虽然我实际上除了空谈什么也没做——不过女人一旦恋爱，便是这副样子。佩里常说，一个家伙倘若真有他老婆或老娘所想象的十分之一那么了不起，那他拽着世界的尾巴下坡，都毫不费力了。
我们头一回动身去萨里，还没走到谷口，我就一脚踩进了一个毒蛇窝。一条小蛇咬了我的脚踝，黛安硬是把我拉回了洞穴。她说我万万不可走动，否则可能有性命之虞——她说，若是条成年毒蛇咬的我，我会当场倒毙，一步也挪不动，那蛇毒就是这般剧烈。饶是如此，我也一定躺了许久，全靠黛安用草药和树叶敷的膏药，才终于消了肿、拔了毒。
不过，这场变故倒是因祸得福，它给了我一个灵感，让我那作为攻防武器的箭，价值翻了千倍。等我能起身走动，我便寻到几条咬过我的那种成年毒蛇，把它们杀死后取出蛇毒，涂在几支箭的箭头上。后来我用这样一支箭射中一头鬣齿兽（hyaenodon），虽然箭只造成一处皮肉轻伤，那野兽却几乎在中箭的同时便瘫软毙命。
于是我们再次出发，前往萨里人之地。向那座美丽的伊甸园道别时，我们心中怀着真诚的眷恋——在它相对的安宁与和谐之中，我们度过了一生中最幸福的时光。我们在那里住了多久，我并不知道，因为正如我告诉过你的，在那轮永恒的正午骄阳之下，时间于我早已不复存在——按地表的时间算，也许是一小时，也许是一个月；我说不上来。

## VIII The Mahar Temple / 八 玛哈尔神庙

===Original===
The aborigine, apparently uninjured, climbed quickly into the skiff, and seizing the spear with me helped to hold off the infuriated creature. Blood from the wounded reptile was now crimsoning the waters about us and soon from the weakening struggles it became evident that I had inflicted a death wound upon it. Presently its efforts to reach us ceased entirely, and with a few convulsive movements it turned upon its back quite dead.
And then there came to me a sudden realization of the predicament in which I had placed myself. I was entirely within the power of the savage man whose skiff I had stolen. Still clinging to the spear I looked into his face to find him scrutinizing me intently, and there we stood for some several minutes, each clinging tenaciously to the weapon the while we gazed in stupid wonderment at each other.
What was in his mind I do not know, but in my own was merely the question as to how soon the fellow would recommence hostilities.
Presently he spoke to me, but in a tongue which I was unable to translate. I shook my head in an effort to indicate my ignorance of his language, at the same time addressing him in the bastard tongue that the Sagoths use to converse with the human slaves of the Mahars.
To my delight he understood and answered me in the same jargon.
“What do you want of my spear?” he asked.
===Chinese===
那个土著人看上去并没有受伤，他迅速爬进小艇，同我一起抓住长矛，帮我抵挡那头被激怒的巨兽。受伤的爬虫流出的血正把我们周围的水染成殷红；从它渐渐虚弱的挣扎来看，很快就看得出，我已给了它致命的一击。不久，它想够到我们的努力便完全停止，又抽搐了几下，它翻过肚皮，彻底死透了。
接着我猛然意识到自己落入了怎样的处境。我偷了这个野人的小艇，如今全然处在他的掌握之中。我仍紧攥着长矛，抬眼望他，发现他也在目不转睛地打量我。我们就那样站了好几分钟，各自死死抓住那件武器，一边呆呆地、惊愕地彼此对望。
他心里想什么，我无从知晓；我心里只有一个念头：这家伙多久会重新动手。
不多时他开口对我说话，可那语言我译解不出。我摇了摇头，表示听不懂他的话，同时用萨戈思人同玛哈尔人的人类奴隶交谈时用的那种混杂土语对他说话。
令我高兴的是，他听懂了，并用同样的行话回答我。
“你要我的矛做什么？”他问。

===Original===
“Only to keep you from running it through me,” I replied.
“I would not do that,” he said, “for you have just saved my life,” and with that he released his hold upon it and squatted down in the bottom of the skiff.
“Who are you,” he continued, “and from what country do you come?”
I too sat down, laying the spear between us, and tried to explain how I came to Pellucidar, and wherefrom, but it was as impossible for him to grasp or believe the strange tale I told him as I fear it is for you upon the outer crust to believe in the existence of the inner world. To him it seemed quite ridiculous to imagine that there was another world far beneath his feet peopled by beings similar to himself, and he laughed uproariously the more he thought upon it. But it was ever thus. That which has never come within the scope of our really pitifully meager world-experience cannot be﻿—our finite minds cannot grasp that which may not exist in accordance with the conditions which obtain about us upon the outside of the insignificant grain of dust which wends its tiny way among the boulders of the universe﻿—the speck of moist dirt we so proudly call the World.
So I gave it up and asked him about himself. He said he was a Mezop, and that his name was Ja.
“Who are the Mezops?” I asked. “Where do they live?”
===Chinese===
“不过是防着你拿它捅我一个对穿。”我答道。
“我才不会那么干，”他说，“因为你方才救了我的命。”说着，他便松开手，在小艇底上蹲坐下来。
“你是谁？”他接着问，“从哪个国家来？”
我也坐了下来，把长矛放在两人中间，尽力解释我是怎样、又从何处来到佩卢西达尔的。可是，要他领会或相信我讲给他的这个离奇故事，恐怕就像要你们这些地表外壳上的人相信内心世界的存在一样，同样不可能。在他看来，自己脚下极深处居然还有另一个世界，住着同他一样的生灵，这念头实在荒唐；他越想越觉得好笑，索性放声大笑。但事情向来如此。凡是从未进入我们那实在少得可怜的世界经验范围之内的东西，便不可能存在——我们有限的心智，无法领会任何不能依照种种条件而存在的事物；而这些条件，只在我们这粒微尘之外才成立——这粒微尘在宇宙的巨砾之间迤逦而行，正是那滴被我们骄傲地称作“世界”的湿泥点。
于是我只好作罢，转而问起他的来历。他说自己是梅佐普人（Mezop），名叫雅（Ja）。
“梅佐普人是什么人？”我问，“他们住在哪儿？”

===Original===
He looked at me in surprise.
“I might indeed believe that you were from another world,” he said, “for who of Pellucidar could be so ignorant! The Mezops live upon the islands of the seas. In so far as I ever have heard no Mezop lives elsewhere, and no others than Mezops dwell upon islands, but of course it may be different in other far-distant lands. I do not know. At any rate in this sea and those near by it is true that only people of my race inhabit the islands.
“We are fishermen, though we be great hunters as well, often going to the mainland in search of the game that is scarce upon all but the larger islands. And we are warriors also,” he added proudly. “Even the Sagoths of the Mahars fear us. Once, when Pellucidar was young, the Sagoths were wont to capture us for slaves as they do the other men of Pellucidar, it is handed down from father to son among us that this is so; but we fought so desperately and slew so many Sagoths, and those of us that were captured killed so many Mahars in their own cities that at last they learned that it were better to leave us alone, and later came the time that the Mahars became too indolent even to catch their own fish, except for amusement, and then they needed us to supply their wants, and so a truce was made between the races. Now they give us certain things which we are unable to produce in return for the fish that we catch, and the Mezops and the Mahars live in peace.
“The great ones even come to our islands. It is there, far from the prying eyes of their own Sagoths, that they practice their religious rites in the temples they have builded there with our assistance. If you live among us you will doubtless see the manner of their worship, which is strange indeed, and most unpleasant for the poor slaves they bring to take part in it.”
As Ja talked I had an excellent opportunity to inspect him more closely. He was a huge fellow, standing I should say six feet six or seven inches, well developed and of a coppery red not unlike that of our own North American Indian, nor were his features dissimilar to theirs. He had the aquiline nose found among many of the higher tribes, the prominent cheek bones, and black hair and eyes, but his mouth and lips were better molded. All in all, Ja was an impressive and handsome creature, and he talked well too, even in the miserable makeshift language we were compelled to use.
During our conversation Ja had taken the paddle and was propelling the skiff with vigorous strokes toward a large island that lay some half-mile from the mainland. The skill with which he handled his crude and awkward craft elicited my deepest admiration, since it had been so short a time before that I had made such pitiful work of it.
===Chinese===
他诧异地望着我。
“我倒真要相信你是从另一个世界来的了，”他说，“佩卢西达尔之内还有谁能无知到这般地步！梅佐普人住在四海的岛屿上。据我所闻，没有一个梅佐普人住在别处，也没有别的部族住在岛上；当然，在别的那遥远地方或许不同，我不知道。无论如何，在这片海和附近几片海里，岛上住的的确只有我族之人，这一点不假。
“我们是打鱼的，不过同时也是了不起的猎人，常到大陆上去猎取野味——除了大岛，各处野味都稀少。我们还是武士，”他自豪地补充道，“连玛哈尔人的萨戈思人都怕我们。从前，佩卢西达尔还年轻时，萨戈思人惯于捉我们去做奴隶，就像他们对佩卢西达尔别的人类所做的那样——这是我们中间父子相传的说法；但我们打得太狠，杀了那么多萨戈思人，被掳去的同族又在他们自己的城里杀了许多玛哈尔人，于是他们终于明白，还是别惹我们为好。后来到了那样一个时期：玛哈尔人变得太懒，除了取乐，连自己的鱼都懒得捕了，于是便需要我们来供给他们的所需，两族之间就此立下休战之约。如今他们拿一些我们自己造不出的东西，换取我们捕到的鱼，梅佐普人和玛哈尔人便相安无事了。
“那些大人物们甚至到我们的岛上来。正是在那里，远离他们自己萨戈思人窥探的眼睛，他们在借我们之力建起的神庙里奉行他们的宗教仪式。你若住在我们中间，必定会见识到他们敬拜的方式——那真是古怪至极，对被带来参与仪式的可怜奴隶们来说，更是苦不堪言。”
雅说话时，我正好趁机把他细细打量一番。他是个大块头，依我看足有六英尺六七英寸高，体格健壮，肤色红铜，同我们的北美印第安人相去不远，相貌也同他们不无相似。他生着许多较开化部族里常见的鹰钩鼻、高耸的颧骨，头发和眼睛都是黑的，只是嘴和唇的轮廓长得更为端正。总而言之，雅是个仪表堂堂的俊美汉子；而且即便用我们不得不说那套糟糕的凑合语言，他讲起话来也颇为在行。
我们交谈的当口，雅已拿过桨，一阵猛划，小艇朝一座离大陆约半英里的大岛驶去。他操纵那条粗陋笨拙小船的娴熟本领，赢得我最深的钦佩——要知道，就在不久之前，我还在把这条船划得惨不忍睹呢。

===Original===
As we touched the pretty, level beach Ja leaped out and I followed him. Together we dragged the skiff far up into the bushes that grew beyond the sand.
“We must hide our canoes,” explained Ja, “for the Mezops of Luana are always at war with us and would steal them if they found them,” he nodded toward an island farther out at sea, and at so great a distance that it seemed but a blur hanging in the distant sky. The upward curve of the surface of Pellucidar was constantly revealing the impossible to the surprised eyes of the outer-earthly. To see land and water curving upward in the distance until it seemed to stand on edge where it melted into the distant sky, and to feel that seas and mountains hung suspended directly above one’s head required such a complete reversal of the perceptive and reasoning faculties as almost to stupefy one.
No sooner had we hidden the canoe than Ja plunged into the jungle, presently emerging into a narrow but well-defined trail which wound hither and thither much after the manner of the highways of all primitive folk, but there was one peculiarity about this Mezop trail which I was later to find distinguished them from all other trails that I ever have seen within or without the earth.
It would run on, plain and clear and well defined to end suddenly in the midst of a tangle of matted jungle, then Ja would turn directly back in his tracks for a little distance, spring into a tree, climb through it to the other side, drop onto a fallen log, leap over a low bush and alight once more upon a distinct trail which he would follow back for a short distance only to turn directly about and retrace his steps until after a mile or less this new pathway ended as suddenly and mysteriously as the former section. Then he would pass again across some media which would reveal no spoor, to take up the broken thread of the trail beyond.
As the purpose of this remarkable avenue dawned upon me I could not but admire the native shrewdness of the ancient progenitor of the Mezops who hit upon this novel plan to throw his enemies from his track and delay or thwart them in their attempts to follow him to his deep-buried cities.
To you of the outer earth it might seem a slow and tortuous method of traveling through the jungle, but were you of Pellucidar you would realize that time is no factor where time does not exist. So labyrinthine are the windings of these trails, so varied the connecting links and the distances which one must retrace one’s steps from the paths’ ends to find them that a Mezop often reaches man’s estate before he is familiar even with those which lead from his own city to the sea.
===Chinese===
小艇触到那片漂亮平整的沙滩，雅纵身跃下，我也跟着跳下。我们俩合力把小艇拖进沙滩那头生长的灌木丛深处。
“我们必须把船藏好，”雅解释道，“因为卢阿纳（Luana）的梅佐普人总在同我们打仗，让他们看见，船就得被偷走。”他朝海上更远处的一座岛点点头——那岛太远，看上去只是悬在天际的一抹模糊影子。佩卢西达尔的地面向上弯成弧形，不断在来自地表的眼睛前展现出种种不可能的景象。眼看陆地与海水在远处向上弯曲，直到仿佛竖立起来、融进遥远的天际；又感到大海与群山就悬在自己头顶正上方——这需要把感知与推理的能力整个颠倒过来，几乎要把人弄傻。
藏好小艇，雅便一头扎进丛林，不多时踏上一条狭窄却轮廓分明的小径。它曲折蜿蜒，同一切原始民族的大路颇为相像；但这条梅佐普小径有个特点——我后来才发现，正是它把梅佐普人的小径同我在这地球内外见过的所有其他道路区别开来。
这小径会一直延伸，清楚、明白、轮廓分明，却在盘结缠杂的密林深处陡然断掉；于是雅便循着原路倒退一小段，纵身跃上一棵树，从树里攀到另一边，落到一根倒木上，跳过一丛矮灌木，重新踏上另一条清晰的小径；他沿它往回走一小段，又蓦地掉头折返，直到一英里上下之后，这条新路也同前一段一样突然而神秘地到了尽头。随后他又会穿过某种不留行迹的介质，把小径中断的线头在那一边重新接上。
待我领会了这条奇径的用途，便不由得不佩服梅佐普人古代先祖的天生狡黠——他想出了这个新奇的主意，甩开敌人、拖住或挫败他们，不让他们循路找到自己深藏地底的城池。
对你们地表人来说，这样在丛林里赶路也许显得又慢又绕；可你若是佩卢西达尔人，就会明白：在时间不存在的地方，时间算不得什么因素。这些小径的弯道如此迷宫一般，连接环节和从路径尽头往回折返的距离又是如此变化多端，以致一个梅佐普人往往到了成年之龄，还认不全从自己城里通往大海的那几条路。

===Original===
In fact three-fourths of the education of the young male Mezop consists in familiarizing himself with these jungle avenues, and the status of an adult is largely determined by the number of trails which he can follow upon his own island. The females never learn them, since from birth to death they never leave the clearing in which the village of their nativity is situated except they be taken to mate by a male from another village, or captured in war by the enemies of their tribe.
After proceeding through the jungle for what must have been upward of five miles we emerged suddenly into a large clearing in the exact center of which stood as strange an appearing village as one might well imagine.
Large trees had been chopped down fifteen or twenty feet above the ground, and upon the tops of them spherical habitations of woven twigs, mud covered, had been built. Each ball-like house was surmounted by some manner of carven image, which Ja told me indicated the identity of the owner.
Horizontal slits, six inches high and two or three feet wide, served to admit light and ventilation. The entrances to the house were through small apertures in the bases of the trees and thence upward by rude ladders through the hollow trunks to the rooms above. The houses varied in size from two to several rooms. The largest that I entered was divided into two floors and eight apartments.
All about the village, between it and the jungle, lay beautifully cultivated fields in which the Mezops raised such cereals, fruits, and vegetables as they required. Women and children were working in these gardens as we crossed toward the village. At sight of Ja they saluted deferentially, but to me they paid not the slightest attention. Among them and about the outer verge of the cultivated area were many warriors. These too saluted Ja, by touching the points of their spears to the ground directly before them.
Ja conducted me to a large house in the center of the village﻿—the house with eight rooms﻿—and taking me up into it gave me food and drink. There I met his mate, a comely girl with a nursing baby in her arms. Ja told her of how I had saved his life, and she was thereafter most kind and hospitable toward me, even permitting me to hold and amuse the tiny bundle of humanity whom Ja told me would one day rule the tribe, for Ja, it seemed, was the chief of the community.
===Chinese===
事实上，年轻的梅佐普男子所受的教育，四分之三都在于熟悉这些丛林通道；一个成年人的地位，很大程度上取决于他在自己岛上能认得多少条小径。女子们从不学这些，因为她们从生到死都不离开出生村落所在的那片林间空地——除非被外村的男子娶去做配偶，或在战争中被部族的仇敌掳走。
在丛林里走了想必不下五英里之后，我们忽然钻进一大片林间空地；空地正中立着一座人们尽可以想象得出的模样最古怪的村落。
一棵棵大树在离地十五至二十英尺处被砍断，树顶上建起一个个球形的居所，以枝条编成，外糊泥浆。每座球形的房屋顶上都安着某种雕刻的偶像，雅告诉我，那标示着房主的身份。
一道道水平的窄缝，高六英寸、宽二三英尺，用来采光和通风。进屋要从树底的小洞口钻入，再顺着简陋的梯子爬上中空的树干，上到楼上的房间。房屋大小不一，从两间到好几间不等。我进去过的最大一座，分为上下两层、八个房间。
村子四周、村子与丛林之间，铺展着一片片耕作精美的田地，梅佐普人在里面种植所需的谷物、果品和菜蔬。我们朝村子走去时，妇女和孩子正在园子里劳作。他们一见雅便恭恭敬敬地行礼，对我却丝毫不加理会。他们中间和耕作区的外缘一带还有许多武士，他们也向雅行礼——把矛尖触在自己正前方的地面上。
雅把我领到村子中央的一座大屋——就是有八个房间的那座——带我上去，给了我吃的喝的。在那里我见到了他的妻子，一个标致的姑娘，怀里抱着个吃奶的婴儿。雅对她讲了我是怎样救他一命的，此后她待我便极为和气殷勤，甚至让我抱那小小的一团小人儿、逗他玩。雅告诉我，这孩子有一天会统领整个部落——原来，雅似乎便是这村社的首领。

===Original===
We had eaten and rested, and I had slept, much to Ja’s amusement, for it seemed that he seldom if ever did so, and then the red man proposed that I accompany him to the temple of the Mahars which lay not far from his village. “We are not supposed to visit it,” he said; “but the great ones cannot hear and if we keep well out of sight they need never know that we have been there. For my part I hate them and always have, but the other chieftains of the island think it best that we continue to maintain the amicable relations which exist between the two races; otherwise I should like nothing better than to lead my warriors amongst the hideous creatures and exterminate them﻿—Pellucidar would be a better place to live were there none of them.”
I wholly concurred in Ja’s belief, but it seemed that it might be a difficult matter to exterminate the dominant race of Pellucidar. Thus conversing we followed the intricate trail toward the temple, which we came upon in a small clearing surrounded by enormous trees similar to those which must have flourished upon the outer crust during the carboniferous age.
Here was a mighty temple of hewn rock built in the shape of a rough oval with rounded roof in which were several large openings. No doors or windows were visible in the sides of the structure, nor was there need of any, except one entrance for the slaves, since, as Ja explained, the Mahars flew to and from their place of ceremonial, entering and leaving the building by means of the apertures in the roof.
“But,” added Ja, “there is an entrance near the base of which even the Mahars know nothing. Come,” and he led me across the clearing and about the end to a pile of loose rock which lay against the foot of the wall. Here he removed a couple of large boulders, revealing a small opening which led straight within the building, or so it seemed, though as I entered after Ja I discovered myself in a narrow place of extreme darkness.
“We are within the outer wall,” said Ja. “It is hollow. Follow me closely.”
The red man groped ahead a few paces and then began to ascend a primitive ladder similar to that which leads from the ground to the upper stories of his house. We ascended for some forty feet when the interior of the space between the walls commenced to grow lighter and presently we came opposite an opening in the inner wall which gave us an unobstructed view of the entire interior of the temple.
===Chinese===
我们吃了东西，歇息了一阵；我还睡了一觉，这让雅大为有趣——他似乎极少睡觉，或者根本从不睡。随后这红皮肤人提议我陪他到玛哈尔人的神庙去，庙就坐落在他村子不远的地方。“按理说我们不该去，”他说，“可那些大人物们听不见，只要我们藏得妥帖，他们就永远不必知道我们去过。就我而论，我恨他们，向来就恨；可岛上别的首领们认为，最好还是维持两族之间现有的和睦关系；不然的话，再没有比率领我的武士冲进那些丑类中间、把他们斩尽杀绝更合我心意的事了——要是没有他们，佩卢西达尔会是个更好的住处。”
我完全赞成雅的看法，只是要把佩卢西达尔的统治种族灭绝干净，看来是件难事。我们就这样边说边顺着曲折的小径向神庙走去，最终在一小片林间空地上望见了它；空地四周环着参天巨树，同地表石炭纪时代想必曾遍布各地的那些树木如出一辙。
眼前是一座凿岩筑成的宏伟神庙，呈不甚规则的椭圆形，穹顶浑圆，顶上开着几个大口子。庙身四壁看不见一扇门窗，也用不着——只消留一个供奴隶进出的入口——因为照雅的解释，玛哈尔人飞来飞去参加他们的典礼，从屋顶上的洞口进出这座建筑。
“不过，”雅又补充道，“基座附近还有一个入口，连玛哈尔人自己也一无所知。来。”他领我穿过空地，绕到庙的一端，来到一堆倚着墙脚的碎石前。他搬开两块大圆石，露出一个小口子，看样子直通庙内；可我跟着雅钻进去之后，却发现自己置身于一道狭窄至极、伸手不见五指的夹缝里。
“我们这是在外墙里头，”雅说，“墙是空心的。紧跟住我。”
红皮肤人在前头摸索着走了几步，随即开始攀一道原始的梯子，同他家从地面通往楼上的梯子相仿。我们攀了约莫四十英尺，夹壁内的空间渐渐亮了起来，不多时我们便来到内墙一处开口的近旁，从那里可以把神庙内部一览无余。

===Original===
The lower floor was an enormous tank of clear water in which numerous hideous Mahars swam lazily up and down. Artificial islands of granite rock dotted this artificial sea, and upon several of them I saw men and women like myself.
“What are the human beings doing here?” I asked.
“Wait and you shall see,” replied Ja. “They are to take a leading part in the ceremonies which will follow the advent of the queen. You may be thankful that you are not upon the same side of the wall as they.”
Scarcely had he spoken than we heard a great fluttering of wings above and a moment later a long procession of the frightful reptiles of Pellucidar winged slowly and majestically through the large central opening in the roof and circled in stately manner about the temple.
There were several Mahars first, and then at least twenty awe-inspiring pterodactyls﻿—*thipdars*, they are called within Pellucidar. Behind these came the queen, flanked by other *thipdars* as she had been when she entered the amphitheater at Phutra.
Three times they wheeled about the interior of the oval chamber, to settle finally upon the damp, cold boulders that fringe the outer edge of the pool. In the center of one side the largest rock was reserved for the queen, and here she took her place surrounded by her terrible guard.
===Chinese===
底层是一泓清澈的大水池，许多丑陋的玛哈尔人正在池中懒洋洋地上下游动。花岗岩堆成的人工小岛点缀着这片人工的海，其中几座岛上，我看见了同我一样的人类男女。
“这些人在这里做什么？”我问。
“等着吧，你自会看见，”雅答道，“女王驾临之后的仪式里，他们要充当重要的角色。你该庆幸，你没有同他们站在墙的同一边。”
他话音未落，我们便听见头顶上一阵响亮的振翅之声；片刻之后，一长列佩卢西达尔可怕的爬虫缓缓而威严地鼓动着翅膀，穿过屋顶正中的大洞口飞了进来，绕着神庙庄严地盘旋。
打头是几名玛哈尔人，随后是至少二十只令人望而生畏的翼手龙——在佩卢西达尔，人们称它们为*提普达*（*thipdar*）。再后面是女王，两侧有别的*提普达*护卫，同她当初进入富特拉的圆形剧场时一样。
他们在椭圆形殿堂内部转了三圈，最后纷纷落定在水池外缘环列的湿冷大圆石上。一侧正中那块最大的岩石留给女王，她便在那里就位，四周环着她那可怕的卫队。

===Original===
All lay quiet for several minutes after settling to their places. One might have imagined them in silent prayer. The poor slaves upon the diminutive islands watched the horrid creatures with wide eyes. The men, for the most part, stood erect and stately with folded arms, awaiting their doom; but the women and children clung to one another, hiding behind the males. They are a noble-looking race, these cave men of Pellucidar, and if our progenitors were as they, the human race of the outer crust has deteriorated rather than improved with the march of the ages. All they lack is opportunity. We have opportunity, and little else.
Now the queen moved. She raised her ugly head, looking about; then very slowly she crawled to the edge of her throne and slid noiselessly into the water. Up and down the long tank she swam, turning at the ends as you have seen captive seals turn in their tiny tanks, turning upon their backs and diving below the surface.
Nearer and nearer to the island she came until at last she remained at rest before the largest, which was directly opposite her throne. Raising her hideous head from the water she fixed her great, round eyes upon the slaves. They were fat and sleek, for they had been brought from a distant Mahar city where human beings are kept in droves, and bred and fattened, as we breed and fatten beef cattle.
The queen fixed her gaze upon a plump young maiden. Her victim tried to turn away, hiding her face in her hands and kneeling behind a woman; but the reptile, with unblinking eyes, stared on with such fixity that I could have sworn her vision penetrated the woman, and the girl’s arms to reach at last the very center of her brain.
Slowly the reptile’s head commenced to move to and fro, but the eyes never ceased to bore toward the frightened girl, and then the victim responded. She turned wide, fear-haunted eyes toward the Mahar queen, slowly she rose to her feet, and then as though dragged by some unseen power she moved as one in a trance straight toward the reptile, her glassy eyes fixed upon those of her captor. To the water’s edge she came, nor did she even pause, but stepped into the shallows beside the little island. On she moved toward the Mahar, who now slowly retreated as though leading her victim on. The water rose to the girl’s knees, and still she advanced, chained by that clammy eye. Now the water was at her waist; now her armpits. Her fellows upon the island looked on in horror, helpless to avert her doom in which they saw a forecast of their own.
The Mahar sank now till only the long upper bill and eyes were exposed above the surface of the water, and the girl had advanced until the end of that repulsive beak was but an inch or two from her face, her horror-filled eyes riveted upon those of the reptile.
===Chinese===
各自落定之后，满殿寂静了好几分钟，简直会让人以为他们在默祷。小小岛上的可怜奴隶们睁大眼睛，望着这些可怕的生物。男人们大多抱臂直立、庄重威严，静候自己的厄运；女人们和孩子则相互偎依，躲在他们身后。这些佩卢西达尔的穴居人是个相貌高贵的种族；倘若我们的祖先便是他们这副模样，那么随着世代的前行，地表的人类不是进步了，而是退化了。他们所缺的只是机会。而我们所有的，差不多也只剩机会而已。
女王动了起来。她抬起丑陋的头颅，环顾四周；随后十分缓慢地爬到宝座边缘，无声无息地滑入水中。她在长长的水池里来回游动，到两端便折返，就像你们见过的被囚的海豹在小池子里转身那样，翻过脊背，潜入水下。
她离小岛越来越近，最后在最最大的一座跟前停了下来——那岛正对着她的宝座。她从水中抬起狰狞的头，把那双又大又圆的眼睛盯住了奴隶们。这些人都养得膘肥体壮，因为他们是从一座遥远的玛哈尔城市运来的；在那座城里，人类成群地被豢养、配种、催肥，就像我们饲养催肥肉牛一样。
女王把目光钉在一位丰满的年轻姑娘身上。那猎物想转过身去，双手掩面，跪在一名妇人身后；可那爬虫的眼睛一眨不眨，直勾勾地盯个不停，盯得那样死，我几乎可以起誓：她的视线穿透了那妇人，又穿透姑娘的双臂，最后直抵她脑髓的正中央。
爬虫的头颅开始缓缓地来回摇动，那双眼睛却始终钻向吓坏了的姑娘；接着，猎物有了回应。她睁大惊惶的双眼望向玛哈尔女王，缓缓站起身来，随即仿佛被某种看不见的力量拖拽着，如中了梦魇一般径直朝那爬虫走去，呆滞的目光锁在她捕捉者的眼睛上。她走到水边，一步也不曾停顿，径直踏进小岛旁的浅水。她继续朝那玛哈尔人走去，后者则缓缓后退，仿佛在引着猎物上前。水漫到姑娘的膝头，她仍在前进，被那只阴冷黏湿的眼睛锁住不放。水到了她的腰际，又到了她的腋下。岛上的同伴们惊恐地看着，无力挽回她的厄运——在那厄运里，他们看见了自己下场的前兆。
这时玛哈尔人往下沉去，直到只有长长的上喙和眼睛还露在水面上；姑娘也已走到那令人作呕的喙尖离她的脸只有一二英寸的地方，她那盈满恐惧的眼睛牢牢钉住爬虫的眼睛。

===Original===
Now the water passed above the girl’s mouth and nose﻿—her eyes and forehead all that showed﻿—yet still she walked on after the retreating Mahar. The queen’s head slowly disappeared beneath the surface and after it went the eyes of her victim﻿—only a slow ripple widened toward the shores to mark where the two vanished.
For a time all was silence within the temple. The slaves were motionless in terror. The Mahars watched the surface of the water for the reappearance of their queen, and presently at one end of the tank her head rose slowly into view. She was backing toward the surface, her eyes fixed before her as they had been when she dragged the helpless girl to her doom.
And then to my utter amazement I saw the forehead and eyes of the maiden come slowly out of the depths, following the gaze of the reptile just as when she had disappeared beneath the surface. On and on came the girl until she stood in water that reached barely to her knees, and though she had been beneath the surface sufficient time to have drowned her thrice over there was no indication, other than her dripping hair and glistening body, that she had been submerged at all.
Again and again the queen led the girl into the depths and out again, until the uncanny weirdness of the thing got on my nerves so that I could have leaped into the tank to the child’s rescue had I not taken a firm hold of myself.
Once they were below much longer than usual, and when they came to the surface I was horrified to see that one of the girl’s arms was gone﻿—gnawed completely off at the shoulder﻿—but the poor thing gave no indication of realizing pain, only the horror in her set eyes seemed intensified.
The next time they appeared the other arm was gone, and then the breasts, and then a part of the face﻿—it was awful. The poor creatures on the islands awaiting their fate tried to cover their eyes with their hands to hide the fearful sight, but now I saw that they too were under the hypnotic spell of the reptiles, so that they could only crouch in terror with their eyes fixed upon the terrible thing that was transpiring before them.
===Chinese===
水漫过了姑娘的口鼻——露在外面的只剩眼睛和额头——她却仍旧跟着节节后退的玛哈尔人往前走。女王的头缓缓消失在水面之下，她猎物的眼睛也随之沉没——只有一圈缓缓的涟漪向岸边荡开，标记出两个身影消失的地方。
一时间，神庙内一片寂静。奴隶们吓得一动不动。玛哈尔人们盯着水面，等待他们的女王重新现身；不多时，在水池一端，她的头缓缓露出了水面。她是倒退着浮上来的，眼睛仍直勾勾望着前方，同她把那无助的姑娘拖向厄运时一模一样。
接着，令我万分惊愕的是，我看见那少女的额头和眼睛也从深处缓缓浮现，追随着爬虫的视线，同她没入水面之下时如出一辙。姑娘一步一步走来，直到站进水深刚过膝的地方；她在水面之下待的时间足以把她淹死三回，可除了滴水的头发和湿亮的身体，看不出她曾没入水中的半点迹象。
女王一次又一次领着姑娘沉入深处，又带出水面，直到这事情骇人的诡异把我的神经磨得几乎发狂；若不是我狠狠地克制住自己，我简直要跳进水池去救那孩子了。
有一次，他们在水下待得比往常久得多；待浮出水面时，我惊骇地看见，姑娘的一条胳膊没有了——整个从肩头被啃了去——可这可怜的人儿却丝毫没有露出痛苦的样子，只是她那双凝滞的眼睛里的恐怖，似乎更深重了。
再出现时，另一条胳膊也没有了，随后是双乳，再后来是半张脸——真是太可怕了。岛上那些静候自己命运的可怜生灵想用手掩住眼睛，遮蔽这骇人的景象；可这时我看出，他们也已落入爬虫的催眠魔咒，只能惊恐地蜷缩着，眼睛死死盯住眼前正在发生的可怕之事。

===Original===
Finally the queen was under much longer than ever before, and when she rose she came alone and swam sleepily toward her boulder. The moment she mounted it seemed to be the signal for the other Mahars to enter the tank, and then commenced, upon a larger scale, a repetition of the uncanny performance through which the queen had led her victim.
Only the women and children fell prey to the Mahars﻿—they being the weakest and most tender﻿—and when they had satisfied their appetite for human flesh, some of them devouring two and three of the slaves, there were only a score of full-grown men left, and I thought that for some reason these were to be spared, but such was far from the case, for as the last Mahar crawled to her rock the queen’s *thipdars* darted into the air, circled the temple once and then, hissing like steam engines, swooped down upon the remaining slaves.
There was no hypnotism here﻿—just the plain, brutal ferocity of the beast of prey, tearing, rending, and gulping its meat, but at that it was less horrible than the uncanny method of the Mahars. By the time the *thipdars* had disposed of the last of the slaves the Mahars were all asleep upon their rocks, and a moment later the great pterodactyls swung back to their posts beside the queen, and themselves dropped into slumber.
“I thought the Mahars seldom, if ever, slept,” I said to Ja.
“They do many things in this temple which they do not do elsewhere,” he replied. “The Mahars of Phutra are not supposed to eat human flesh, yet slaves are brought here by thousands and almost always you will find Mahars on hand to consume them. I imagine that they do not bring their Sagoths here, because they are ashamed of the practice, which is supposed to obtain only among the least advanced of their race; but I would wager my canoe against a broken paddle that there is no Mahar but eats human flesh whenever she can get it.”
“Why should they object to eating human flesh,” I asked, “if it is true that they look upon us as lower animals?”
===Chinese===
最后，女王在水下待的时间比以往任何时候都长；待她浮上来时，孤身一人，睡意惺忪地朝自己的大圆石游去。她登上石头的那一刻，似乎便是其他玛哈尔人入池的信号；于是一场更大规模的表演开始了，重演着女王引她猎物经历过的那诡异的一幕。
只有女人和孩子成了玛哈尔人的猎物——他们最弱小、最细嫩——等到他们噬食人肉的餍足之后——其中几个吞下了两三个奴隶——只剩二十个成年男人。我原以为这些人不知为何会被饶过，其实远非如此：最后一名玛哈尔人爬上自己的岩石时，女王的*提普达*们腾空而起，绕神庙盘旋一周，随即像蒸汽机一般嘶嘶啸叫着，朝剩下的奴隶们猛扑下去。
这里没有催眠术——只有食肉猛兽赤裸裸的凶残，撕、扯、吞咽着它的肉食；但即便如此，也比玛哈尔人那诡异的手法少些恐怖。等到*提普达*们把最后一批奴隶收拾干净，玛哈尔人已全都睡在各自的岩石上；片刻之后，巨大的翼手龙们飞回女王身旁的岗位，也自沉入了梦乡。
“我还以为玛哈尔人即使睡觉，也极少极少呢。”我对雅说。
“他们在这座神庙里干的事，有许多是在别处不干的，”他答道，“富特拉的玛哈尔人照理不吃人肉，可奴隶们成千上万地被运到这里，而你也几乎总能看到玛哈尔人守在一旁把他们吃掉。我猜想他们不带萨戈思人来，是因为他们羞于此道——据说这习气只在他们种族中最不开化的那些中间才有；可我敢拿我的独木舟对赌一支断桨：没有一个玛哈尔人不是一弄到人肉就要吃的。”
“倘若他们果真把我们看作低等动物，”我问，“那他们又何必反感吃人肉呢？”

===Original===
“It is not because they consider us their equals that they are supposed to look with abhorrence upon those who eat our flesh,” replied Ja; “it is merely that we are warm-blooded animals. They would not think of eating the meat of a *thag*, which we consider such a delicacy, any more than I would think of eating a snake. As a matter of fact it is difficult to explain just why this sentiment should exist among them.”
“I wonder if they left a single victim,” I remarked, leaning far out of the opening in the rocky wall to inspect the temple better. Directly below me the water lapped the very side of the wall, there being a break in the boulders at this point as there was at several other places about the side of the temple.
My hands were resting upon a small piece of granite which formed a part of the wall, and all my weight upon it proved too much for it. It slipped and I lunged forward. There was nothing to save myself and I plunged headforemost into the water below.
Fortunately the tank was deep at this point, and I suffered no injury from the fall, but as I was rising to the surface my mind filled with the horrors of my position as I thought of the terrible doom which awaited me the moment the eyes of the reptiles fell upon the creature that had disturbed their slumber.
As long as I could I remained beneath the surface, swimming rapidly in the direction of the islands that I might prolong my life to the utmost. At last I was forced to rise for air, and as I cast a terrified glance in the direction of the Mahars and the thipdars I was almost stunned to see that not a single one remained upon the rocks where I had last seen them, nor as I searched the temple with my eyes could I discern any within it.
For a moment I was puzzled to account for the thing, until I realized that the reptiles, being deaf, could not have been disturbed by the noise my body made when it hit the water, and that as there is no such thing as time within Pellucidar there was no telling how long I had been beneath the surface. It was a difficult thing to attempt to figure out by earthly standards﻿—this matter of elapsed time﻿—but when I set myself to it I began to realize that I might have been submerged a second or a month or not at all. You have no conception of the strange contradictions and impossibilities which arise when all methods of measuring time, as we know them upon earth, are nonexistent.
===Chinese===
“他们照理对吃我们肉的同类深恶痛绝，倒并非因为他们把我们当作同侪，”雅答道，“只因我们是温血动物罢了。他们绝想不到去吃*塔格*（*thag*）的肉——那可是我们视作珍馐的东西——就像我绝想不到去吃蛇一样。说实在的，他们中间何以存在这种情感，实在难以解释清楚。”
“不知他们可曾留下一个活口。”我说着，把身子探出石墙上的洞口，想把神庙看得更真切些。正下方，池水就贴着墙边荡漾——这一带的圆石有个缺口，神庙侧面另外几处也有这样的缺口。
我的双手正撑在一小块花岗岩上，那是墙体的一部分；我全身的重量压上去，它便承受不住了。石头一滑，我猛地向前扑去。没有任何可以攀抓的东西，我头朝下栽进了下面的水里。
所幸水池此处很深，我这一摔并未受伤；可当我向水面浮升时，心头已塞满处境的恐怖——一想到那些爬虫的眼睛一旦落到这个搅扰了它们睡眠的家伙身上，怎样的灭顶之灾便会在那一刻等着它。
我尽量待在水面之下，朝小岛的方向疾游，好把性命多延片刻。终于，我不得不浮上来换气；当我朝玛哈尔人和提普达们的方向投去惊恐的一瞥时，我几乎惊呆了：在我上次看见它们的那片岩石上，竟一只也不剩；我用目光搜遍神庙，里面也寻不出任何一只。
一时间我百思不得其解，直到意识到：这些爬虫都是聋的，我落水的声响根本吵不到它们；况且佩卢西达尔之内根本不存在时间这种东西，谁也说不清我在水下待了多久。要用地球上的标准来计算这件事——流逝时间的问题——实在难以办到；可当我认真去琢磨，才渐渐意识到：我也许在水下待了一秒钟，或者一个月，又或者压根儿不曾待过。当地球上我们所知的一切计时方法统统不存在时，会生出怎样离奇的矛盾和不可能，你们是毫无概念的。

===Original===
I was about to congratulate myself upon the miracle which had saved me for the moment, when the memory of the hypnotic powers of the Mahars filled me with apprehension lest they be practicing their uncanny art upon me to the end that I merely imagined that I was alone in the temple. At the thought cold sweat broke out upon me from every pore, and as I crawled from the water onto one of the tiny islands I was trembling like a leaf﻿—you cannot imagine the awful horror which even the simple thought of the repulsive Mahars of Pellucidar induces in the human mind, and to feel that you are in their power﻿—that they are crawling, slimy, and abhorrent, to drag you down beneath the waters and devour you! It is frightful.
But they did not come, and at last I came to the conclusion that I was indeed alone within the temple. How long I should be alone was the next question to assail me as I swam frantically about once more in search of a means to escape.
Several times I called to Ja, but he must have left after I tumbled into the tank, for I received no response to my cries. Doubtless he had felt as certain of my doom when he saw me topple from our hiding place as I had, and lest he too should be discovered, had hastened from the temple and back to his village.
I knew that there must be some entrance to the building beside the doorways in the roof, for it did not seem reasonable to believe that the thousands of slaves which were brought here to feed the Mahars the human flesh they craved would all be carried through the air, and so I continued my search until at last it was rewarded by the discovery of several loose granite blocks in the masonry at one end of the temple.
A little effort proved sufficient to dislodge enough of these stones to permit me to crawl through into the clearing, and a moment later I had scurried across the intervening space to the dense jungle beyond.
Here I sank panting and trembling upon the matted grasses beneath the giant trees, for I felt that I had escaped from the grinning fangs of death out of the depths of my own grave. Whatever dangers lay hidden in this island jungle, there could be none so fearsome as those which I had just escaped. I knew that I could meet death bravely enough if it but came in the form of some familiar beast or man﻿—anything other than the hideous and uncanny Mahars.
===Chinese===
我正要为自己暂得脱身的奇迹庆幸，却猛然想起玛哈尔人的催眠之力，顿时忧惧起来：会不会他们正在对我施展那诡异的法术，让我误以为自己独在庙中？一想到这里，我浑身的毛孔都沁出冷汗；从水里爬上一座小岛时，我抖得像片树叶——光是想到佩卢西达尔那些可憎的玛哈尔人，人的心里会生出怎样的恐怖，你们无法想象；何况是感到自己落在他们的掌握之中——他们正爬着、黏滑着、可憎地，要把你拖下水底吞吃掉！这真是可怕极了。
可他们没有来。我终于断定，神庙之内确实只我一人。接下来袭上心头的问题是：这份孤单还会持续多久——于是我再度发疯似的四处游动，寻找脱身之计。
我几次呼喊雅，但他一定是在我栽进水池之后便走了，因为我的呼喊没有换到半点回应。毫无疑问，他眼看我从藏身之处跌落时，对我的死期同我本人一样确信；为了不让自己也被人发现，他匆匆离开神庙，回村里去了。
我知道，除了屋顶的那些洞口，这座建筑必定还有别的入口——被运到这里、供玛哈尔人饱啖渴求的人肉的成千上万的奴隶，总不至于全从空中运来。于是我继续寻找，终于有了回报：在神庙一端的石壁上，我发现了几块松动的花岗岩。
稍稍用力，便足以撬下足够的石块，容我钻出去爬到空地上；片刻之后，我已一溜烟穿过当中的空地，钻进了外面茂密的丛林。
我扑倒在巨树之下盘结的草丛上，气喘吁吁，浑身发抖；因为我觉得，自己刚刚是从自家坟墓的深处、从死神龇露的獠牙底下逃了出来。这座岛上的丛林里无论藏着怎样的凶险，都断不会比我刚刚逃脱的更加骇人。我知道，只要死神化作某种熟悉的野兽或人的模样前来，我都尽可以从容赴死——什么都行，只消不是那些丑恶诡异的玛哈尔人。

## XIII The Sly One / 十三 狡猾鬼

===Original===
The Sagoths were gaining on us rapidly, for once they had sighted us they had greatly increased their speed. On and on we stumbled up the narrow canyon that Ghak had chosen to approach the heights of Sari. On either side rose precipitous cliffs of gorgeous, parti-colored rock, while beneath our feet a thick mountain grass formed a soft and noiseless carpet. Since we had entered the canyon we had had no glimpse of our pursuers, and I was commencing to hope that they had lost our trail and that we would reach the now rapidly nearing cliffs in time to scale them before we should be overtaken.
Ahead we neither saw nor heard any sign which might betoken the success of Hooja’s mission. By now he should have reached the outposts of the Sarians, and we should at least hear the savage cries of the tribesmen as they swarmed to arms in answer to their king’s appeal for succor. In another moment the frowning cliffs ahead should be black with primeval warriors. But nothing of the kind happened﻿—as a matter of fact the Sly One had betrayed us. At the moment that we expected to see Sarian spearmen charging to our relief at Hooja’s back, the craven traitor was sneaking around the outskirts of the nearest Sarian village, that he might come up from the other side when it was too late to save us, claiming that he had become lost among the mountains.
Hooja still harbored ill will against me because of the blow I had struck in Dian’s protection, and his malevolent spirit was equal to sacrificing us all that he might be revenged upon me.
As we drew nearer the barrier cliffs and no sign of rescuing Sarians appeared Ghak became both angry and alarmed, and presently as the sound of rapidly approaching pursuit fell upon our ears, he called to me over his shoulder that we were lost.
A backward glance gave me a glimpse of the first of the Sagoths at the far end of a considerable stretch of canyon through which we had just passed, and then a sudden turning shut the ugly creature from my view; but the loud howl of triumphant rage which rose behind us was evidence that the gorilla-man had sighted us.
===Chinese===
萨戈思人正在迅速逼近我们，因为一旦发现了我们，他们便大大加快了速度。我们跌跌撞撞，顺着格哈克选定的那条通往萨里高地的窄峡谷不住向上爬。两侧耸起陡峭的崖壁，岩色斑斓，蔚为壮观；脚下厚厚的山草铺成一张松软无声的地毯。自从进了峡谷，我们再没瞥见过追兵，我开始指望他们已跟丢了我们的踪迹，指望我们能在被追上之前，及时赶到越来越近的崖壁脚下，攀上崖去。
前方，我们既没看到也没听到任何可以预示胡贾使命成功的迹象。到这时，他本该已到达萨里人的前哨，我们至少该听见部族战士们响应他们国王的求援、纷纷拿起武器时发出的狂野呼号。再过片刻，前方那逼人的峭壁本该布满黑压压的原始武士。可这样的情形一样也没有发生——事实上，是狡猾鬼出卖了我们。就在我们指望看见萨里长矛手跟着胡贾冲来解围的那一刻，那个卑怯的叛徒正在最近的萨里村子外围鬼鬼祟祟地绕圈子，打算等来不及救我们的时候，从另一头露面，只推说自己在山里迷了路。
胡贾因为我为保护黛安而给他的那一拳，对我仍怀恨在心；他那歹毒的心肠，完全干得出牺牲我们大家来向我报复的事。
我们离屏障似的峭壁越来越近，却不见前来营救的萨里人的半点踪影，格哈克又怒又慌；不久，急促逼近的追兵之声落进我们耳中，他回头对我喊道：我们完了。
回头一瞥，我瞥见了走在最前面的那个萨戈思人，正在我们刚走过的一大段峡谷的远端；随即一个急转弯，把那丑陋的家伙挡出了我的视线；可背后响起的一声得胜狂怒的长嚎证明，那大猩猩似的人已经看见了我们。

===Original===
Again the canyon veered sharply to the left, but to the right another branch ran on at a lesser deviation from the general direction, so that appeared more like the main canyon than the lefthand branch. The Sagoths were now not over two hundred and fifty yards behind us, and I saw that it was hopeless for us to expect to escape other than by a ruse. There was a bare chance of saving Ghak and Perry, and as I reached the branching of the canyon I took the chance.
Pausing there I waited until the foremost Sagoth hove into sight. Ghak and Perry had disappeared around a bend in the left-hand canyon, and as the Sagoth’s savage yell announced that he had seen me I turned and fled up the right-hand branch. My ruse was successful, and the entire party of man-hunters raced headlong after me up one canyon while Ghak bore Perry to safety up the other.
Running has never been my particular athletic forte, and now when my very life depended upon fleetness of foot I cannot say that I ran any better than on the occasions when my pitiful base running had called down upon my head the rooter’s raucous and reproachful cries of “Ice Wagon,” and “Call a cab.”
The Sagoths were gaining on me rapidly. There was one in particular, fleeter than his fellows, who was perilously close. The canyon had become a rocky slit, rising roughly at a steep angle toward what seemed a pass between two abutting peaks.
